# Module contract

The exported signature of every module, in dependency order. This is the reference a change is written against, and the file to update when a public signature moves.

The per-module API of `@ashlr/lexicon`, in dependency order. Every module codes against `src/core/types.ts`. Cross-module imports inside `src/core` go through `../core/index.js`; `src/util/*` is the one exception, a dependency-free bottom layer every other layer imports directly by path. ESM, `.js` suffix on relative imports, NodeNext resolution. No default exports. Node >= 20, no `any` in public signatures. Update this file when a public signature changes.

Several files are facades: they re-export from the modules they were split into so their old import paths keep working. Where that is so, this document says where a name is *defined* as well as where it can be imported from.

## Module map (dependency order)

Each layer imports only from the layers above it, and siblings only through `src/core/index.js`.

```text
src/util/          errors, json, which, atomic, package      <- nothing (the bottom layer; every other layer imports it directly)
src/core/          types, schema, store, trust, matcher (facade over matcher/{build,text,tokenize,tuning} + stoplist),
                   normalize, suggest, learn, pending, stats, notes, suggestTerms, harvest, packs, shell, importers/, exporters/, index   <- util
src/daemon/        clipboard-backends, clipboard, expander, expander-server   <- core, util
src/serve/         config, server, index                     <- core, util
src/voice/         process, devices, models, recorder, transcribe, history, voice   <- core, daemon (clipboard write), util
src/cli/           cli-entry, io, serve-paths, prompt, claude-settings, commands (facade), cmd-doctor, cmd-install,
                   cmd-import, cmd-trust, cmd-learn, cmd-review, cmd-suggest, cmd-serve, cmd-voice, cmd-pack,
                   cmd-setup (facade over setup/{types,detect,steps}), index   <- core, daemon, serve, voice, util
src/hooks/         user-prompt-submit                        <- core, util
src/mcp/           server, shared, tools/{index,terms,harvest,trust,setup,packs}   <- core, cli (runDoctorReport, runInstall, runImport, runSetup), util
```

Data and other trees, none of which `src/` imports at build time:

```text
packs/             the six starter-pack YAML lexicons (developer, ai, business, voice-tools, cloud-devops, profession-q8), read at runtime by src/core/packs.ts
plugin/            committed esbuild bundles of src/mcp/server.ts and src/hooks/user-prompt-submit.ts
extension/src/     browser extension; its core.ts bundles the browser-safe slice of src/core   <- core (build time only)
web/               Next.js marketing site for lexicon.ashlr.ai; web/lib/generated holds a browser bundle of src/core   <- core (build time only)
site/              the standalone GitHub Pages demo (hand-rolled, no framework), built by scripts/build-site.mjs   <- core (build time only)
apps/macos/        LexiconBar, SwiftPM; talks to the CLI as a subprocess and to src/serve over loopback HTTP, never imports TypeScript
bench/             benchmark corpora and runners; bench/audio/models is also a whisper model fallback dir
```

## Non-English name benchmark (issue #8)

Run this text benchmark from the repository root after `npm install`, with Node 20 or
newer. Keep the same repository revision, corpus, lexicon and default matcher settings
when comparing runs; record the revision, Node version and command with the report.
The committed `bench/corpus.jsonl` and `bench/lexicon.yaml` are the inputs, so no
corpus generation or personal lexicon is needed. The untagged cases are the original
baseline; the `lang`-tagged cases cover German, Spanish, Polish, Japanese, Arabic and
Nordic names. They include accent folding, transliterations, anglicized and syllable-split
spellings, first-name and diminutive cases, multiple names, and clean prose that sounds
like a name. The lexicon includes terms both with and without aliases. These are
hand-written examples of STT errors, not a representative sample of real dictation.

For the baseline run, save the markdown and JSON outside the checkout:

```bash
bench_dir=$(mktemp -d)
npm run bench -- --out "$bench_dir/results.json" > "$bench_dir/report.md"
```

Use the `Non-English subset (lang-tagged cases)` table to record the case counts and
the untagged baseline, all tagged cases and each language group separately. Report raw
term recall from `heard` and recall after normalization, term precision and F1,
positive sentence accuracy, and the negative prose false-positive rate, with the
numerators and denominators shown in the report. Recall counts expected canonical
term slots recovered; precision counts recovered slots against recovered slots plus
spurious replacements. Sentence accuracy requires an exact match to `expected`;
prose false positives are negative cases with any replacement. Include expected-hard
cases in the main figures and report the positive accuracy excluding them alongside
it; inspect failures with `npm run bench -- --verbose --out "$bench_dir/verbose.json"`.
Compare raw and normalized recall and the tagged and untagged subsets, while keeping
their different inputs in mind. The runner's warm latency is a separate whole-corpus
measurement, sensitive to the machine and load. For definitions and fixture changes,
see [bench/README.md](../bench/README.md); measured results, if needed, live in
[BENCHMARK.md](BENCHMARK.md). This synthetic procedure measures the matcher on the
fixture, not recognition quality or real-world performance across speakers and languages.


## src/util/ (errors, record, json, which, atomic, package)

The bottom layer: no imports from `src/core`, `src/cli` or anything else in the tree. Every other layer imports these directly by path rather than through `core/index.js`, which is the one documented exception to the sibling rule. These exist because seven waves of agents each wrote their own copy; if you find yourself writing an eightieth `String(err)` or `PATH.split(':')`, it belongs here.

### src/util/errors.ts
- `errorMessage(err: unknown): string` the message for anything thrown, without `any` and without `[object Object]`.
- `isEnoent(err: unknown): boolean` true for a missing-file error.
- `class InputError extends Error` (name `'InputError'`) and `isInputError(err: unknown): boolean` (by name): an error caused by the caller's input, thrown by `learnCorrection`'s checks, `addTerm`/`removeAlias` on an empty or unknown name, and a write whose YAML would not read back. The local API maps it to 400 and every other unexpected error to 500; the message is what the CLI and MCP server show, unchanged.

### src/util/record.ts
- `isRecord(value: unknown): value is Record<string, unknown>` the type guard the codebase uses instead of `any` when walking parsed JSON; re-exported from `json.js`, where callers already import it.

### src/util/json.ts
- `formatJson(value: unknown): string` the canonical 2-space encoding written to config files.
- `JsonFileRead { exists; value?; error?; text? }`, `readJsonFile(file): Promise<JsonFileRead>` and `writeJsonFile(file, value, original?): Promise<void>`: read-modify-write of another tool's config (`~/.claude/settings.json`, `~/.cursor/mcp.json`, ...) without losing what was already there. `readJsonFile` parses with `parseJsonc` (comments and trailing commas accepted; a BOM is still an error) and returns the text read as `text`; `writeJsonFile` with that text as `original` writes `editJsonText(original, value)`, so only what changed is rewritten, and `formatJson(value)` without it, through `writeFileAtomic` (a link followed, the mode kept), so a write that fails partway leaves the old file.

### src/util/jsonc.ts
- `parseJsonc(text): unknown`: JSON plus `//` and `/* */` comments and trailing commas; a repeated key keeps the last value, as `JSON.parse` does. Throws an Error naming the line and column of the first problem.
- `editJsonText(text, next): string`: `text` rewritten to hold `next`, changing only what differs: a changed value is replaced in place (indented as the line it starts on), a new key or array element goes after the last one in the file's own indent unit (tabs, or its smallest run of spaces) and trailing comma style, after a comment that closes that line, compact in a one-line object or array; every other byte, comments included, is kept. An object that lost or reordered a key is written whole. Empty or unparseable `text`, or an edit that would not read back as `next`, gives `formatJson`-style 2-space JSON instead.

### src/util/which.ts
Executable lookup without spawning `which` / `where`.
- `WELL_KNOWN_BIN_DIRS = ['/opt/homebrew/bin', '/usr/local/bin', '/usr/bin']` (none on win32).
- `FindOnPathOptions { env?; platform?; exists?: (candidate: string) => Promise<boolean> }`, `findOnPath(bin, opts?): Promise<string | undefined>`.
- `FindOnPathSyncOptions { env?; platform? }` (no `exists` hook), `findOnPathSync(bin, opts?): string | undefined`.
- Both split `PATH` (`Path` / `path` are accepted too) on `;` for win32 and `:` elsewhere, and on win32 expand `PATHEXT` (default `.COM;.EXE;.BAT;.CMD`, upper then lower case) before trying the bare name, unless `bin` already has an extension. Both iterate **directory-major**: an earlier `PATH` entry always wins, and within a directory `PATHEXT` order decides. The one deliberate difference is the probe: `findOnPath` requires `X_OK` on POSIX, `findOnPathSync` uses `existsSync` (`F_OK`), so a non-executable file on `PATH` is reported by the sync form only.
- `LocateOptions extends FindOnPathOptions { extraDirs?: readonly string[] }`, `locateTool(names, opts?): Promise<string | undefined>`, `locateToolSync(names, env?, platform?): string | undefined` (positional args, so `extraDirs` cannot be overridden).
- `locateTool` runs in two phases with deliberately opposite nesting. Phase 1 searches `PATH` **name-major**: every `PATH` directory is tried for `names[0]` before `names[1]` is considered at all, so name order is preference order and `whisper-cli` in the last `PATH` entry beats `whisper-cpp` in the first. Phase 2 runs only when every name failed on `PATH`, and falls back to `WELL_KNOWN_BIN_DIRS` **directory-major**, so there `/opt/homebrew/bin/whisper-cpp` beats `/usr/local/bin/whisper-cli`.
- **The sync and async forms resolve identically.** They used to disagree: the sync lookup iterated directories in the outer loop while the async one iterated names, so `lexicon doctor` could report `whisper-cpp` while `lexicon voice` ran `whisper-cli`. `tests/which.test.ts` asserts they agree; keep it that way if you touch either.

### src/util/atomic.ts
- `AtomicWriteOptions`, `writeFileAtomic(target, data, opts?): Promise<void>` the single write-temp-then-`rename(2)` primitive behind `writeLexiconFile`, the trust registry, `serve.json`, the voice state file, `writeJsonFile` and the Codex `config.toml` writers of `lexicon install` and `lexicon uninstall`. A reader never sees a half-written lexicon. A write that fails once the temp is opened (the temp write itself included) cleans the temp up, and the temp name always carries the writer's pid, so two writers that end up in the critical section together cannot corrupt each other's temp.
  - The target is resolved through `fs.realpath` first when it exists, so a symlinked lexicon path (a dotfiles repo, a synced folder) is written *through* rather than replaced by a regular file.
  - Without an explicit `mode`, an existing target keeps the mode it already has. A file the user chmod'd to 0600 stays 0600; only a file being created takes the umask.
  - `backup: true` keeps two copies, both written from the target through a temp plus rename: `<target>.bak` is the contents before this write, and `<target>.1.bak` is refreshed only once it is `BACKUP_ROTATE_MS` (15 minutes) old. One generation was not enough, because the prompt hook's hit counters replaced it seconds after a write that turned out to be wrong. `BACKUP_SUFFIX`, `OLDER_BACKUP_SUFFIX` and `BACKUP_ROTATE_MS` are exported.
- `withFileLock(target, fn, opts?: FileLockOptions)` holds `<target>.lock` across a whole read-modify-write, not just the rename. Atomicity alone was never enough: five writers share the lexicon file (the MCP server, the prompt hook's hit counters, `lexicon serve`, the clipboard daemon and the menu bar app), and twelve concurrent adds left one term while every one of them reported success. The lock is `O_CREAT | O_EXCL`, so it needs no dependency and works between unrelated processes, and it is re-entrant through `AsyncLocalStorage` and queued per path within a process. Acquisition throws `FileLockError` after `DEFAULT_LOCK_TIMEOUT_MS` rather than proceeding, because a silent overwrite is the failure being fixed. A lock directory that cannot be created because a file is in the way on its path (`LEXICON_PATH=/tmp/somefile/lexicon.yaml`) is a `FileLockError` at once, naming that file (`<file> is not a directory, so the lock cannot be created next to it`), not contention. `isFileLockError` and `LOCK_SUFFIX` are exported alongside. `FileLockError.held` is true only for a lock another live writer still held at the timeout (the one failure a retry can get past; false for a lock that cannot be created or an in-process ordering bug), and `isHeldLockError(err)` tests for it, duck-typed like `isFileLockError`.
  - The lock filename, queue and re-entrant identity use the OS-canonical physical target, matching atomic writes through existing file symlinks. Directory aliases and Windows short/long names share that identity. Missing paths resolve their nearest existing ancestor and retain the missing suffix, keeping the identity stable when the first write creates them. Dangling file links retain their own filename; locking does not create their missing destination. Unrelated targets retain separate locks.
  - **Liveness, not age, decides whether a lock may be broken.** A lock this host wrote is broken the moment its pid is gone (the Ctrl-C and crash path) and never while that pid is alive, however old the file is. Only a lock that cannot be attributed at all (another host, an unreadable payload, a pid no process can have) expires by age at `DEFAULT_LOCK_STALE_MS`, and a holder refreshes its own lock's mtime while it works, so age means what it says. Breaking on age alone was a real, executed data loss: `installPack` holds one lock for around a minute on a three thousand term lexicon, and a `lexicon add` arriving half a minute in broke the live lock, wrote, reported success, and was renamed away by the install.
  - **Lock ordering.** The lexicon file's lock is always the outer one; `hits.json`, `trust.json`, `serve.json` and `pending-aliases.yaml` are taken inside it and never the reverse. When two lexicon locks are held at once (only `acceptPending` does this) the global file's is outer and the project file's inner. `tests/pending.test.ts` holds a lexicon lock, queues a correction and runs `acceptPending` concurrently, which deadlocked while accept took the pending lock first. Waiting for this process's own turn is bounded by `queueTimeoutMs` (`DEFAULT_LOCK_QUEUE_TIMEOUT_MS`, five minutes) so an ordering mistake fails with a `FileLockError` somebody can read instead of hanging forever.

### src/util/package.ts
- `PACKAGE_NAME = '@ashlr/lexicon'`, `UNKNOWN_VERSION = '0.0.0'`.
- `findPackageRoot(from: string): string | undefined` walks up from a path or `file:` URL to the directory whose `package.json` is named `@ashlr/lexicon`.
- `packageVersion(moduleUrl: string): string` one answer to "what version am I" that holds from `src/`, from `dist/` and from the `plugin/` bundles, falling back to `UNKNOWN_VERSION`. Used by `cli/index.ts`, `cmd-doctor.ts`, `serve/server.ts` and `mcp/shared.ts`.
- **Name collision to know about.** `src/core/packs.ts` also exports a `findPackageRoot`, with an *optional* argument defaulting to its own module URL, and `core/index.js` re-exports it. `import { findPackageRoot } from './core/index.js'` and `from './cli/cli-entry.js'` are therefore different functions. Both do the same walk; prefer the `util/package.ts` one in new code.

## src/core/types.ts
Dependency-free shared types.
- `TermCategory = 'brand' | 'person' | 'product' | 'acronym' | 'identifier' | 'place' | 'other'`, `TermScope = 'global' | 'project'`, `TermSource = 'user' | 'harvest:repo' | 'harvest:git' | 'harvest:package' | 'import' | 'import:contacts' | 'import:calendar' | 'import:github' | 'learned' | 'pack'` (`TERM_SOURCES`; `'pack'` is written by `installPack`).
- `Term { canonical; aliases: string[]; phonetic?; category?; caseSensitive?; scope?; source?; notes?; createdAt?; hits?; never?: string[] }`. `parseLexicon` fills `aliases` with `[]` when a term has none, and nothing else: `never` (and `settings.protectedWords` / `settings.packs`) is present only when the file has that key, so a term read from a file and one `addTerm` just created have the same shape in `list --json`, `learn --json`, `add --json` and the MCP results (no `never` key unless there are never-words). `addTerm` drops an empty `never`, and reading a file and writing it back adds no `never: []` line; an explicit `never: []` in a hand-written file is read back as `[]` and kept. A key left empty in a hand-edited file (`terms:`, `aliases:`, `never:`, `settings:`, `notes:` with nothing after them, which YAML reads as null) is read as if it were left out (so `terms` and `aliases` come back `[]` and the optional keys stay absent); a null `canonical`, and a null entry inside a list, are still errors.
- `LexiconSettings { minConfidence?; phonetic?; fuzzy?; protectedWords?; skipCode? }`, `Lexicon { version: 1; terms: Term[]; settings? }`.
- `LexiconFile { path; scope; lexicon; exists }`, `LoadedLexicon { merged; global: LexiconFile; project?: LexiconFile; projectTrust?: 'trusted' | 'untrusted' | 'changed'; skippedProject?: LexiconFile; globalStale?: true; globalStaleProblem?: string }` (`globalStale` and `globalStaleProblem` only from `loadLexicon` with `staleGlobal`, see below).
- `MatchReason = 'alias' | 'phonetic' | 'fuzzy'`, `Replacement { start; end; original; replacement; canonical; reason; confidence }`, `NormalizeResult { input; output; replacements; changed }`, `NormalizeOptions { minConfidence?; phonetic?; fuzzy?; skipCode?; dryRun?; shouldStop?: () => boolean }`, `NormalizeIncomplete { reasons: ('length' | 'time')[]; checkedChars?; totalChars; note }` (the `incomplete` field of MCP `normalize_transcript`, serve `POST /normalize` and the extension's embedded engine).
- `HarvestCandidate { canonical; category; source; evidence: string[]; count; suggestedAliases }`, `HarvestOptions { limit?; minCount?; git?; packages?; identifiers?; symbols?; ignore?: string[] }`.
- `EXPORT_FORMATS` (the 15 format names, `as const`) and `ExportFormat = (typeof EXPORT_FORMATS)[number]` are declared here and re-exported by `exporters/index.ts`; `ExportOptions { categories?: TermCategory[]; limit? }`.

## src/core/schema.ts
- `LexiconSchema`, `TermSchema`, `LexiconSettingsSchema`, `TermCategorySchema`, `TermScopeSchema`, `TermSourceSchema` (zod v4) mirroring types.ts.
- `parseLexicon(raw: unknown): Lexicon` throws a readable error on invalid input: `Invalid lexicon:` and one `  - <field path>: <problem>` line per problem, at most `MAX_LISTED_ISSUES` (20) of them, then `  - ... and <n> more problem(s) like these` (a file with thousands of bad entries made a message of hundreds of kilobytes, which every CLI command printed and every MCP tool, resource and prompt returned).
- `LIMITS`: `word` 80 (canonical, alias), `text` 200 (notes, phonetic), `aliases` 64 (aliases and `never` per term), `terms` 5000, `protectedWords` 1000. No control characters or line breaks in any field (including U+2028/U+2029 and NEL). No U+FFFE, U+FFFF or unpaired surrogate in any field either: `findUnencodableChar(s)` returns the first as `U+XXXX` (undefined when there is none; a surrogate pair is fine), and the schema refuses it as `must not contain U+XXXX (UNENCODABLE_NOTE)`, with ` in term "<canonical>"` after it for a term's fields (the canonical with each such code unit written `\uXXXX`, cut to 40 characters), so `addTerm`, `addTerms`, `learnCorrection` and every surface on them (CLI, MCP, `POST /add`, `POST /learn`) refuse it as `term not added: <field> (...): ...` and write nothing. `UNENCODABLE_NOTE = 'U+FFFE, U+FFFF and unpaired surrogates fit no file format'`. `stripInvisible(s)` removes zero-width (U+200B to U+200F), bidi controls (U+202A to U+202E), bidi isolates (U+2066 to U+2069) and the BOM; `hasControlChars(s)`. `stripControlChars(s)` removes every control character (C0 incl. CR/LF/tab, DEL, C1 incl. NEL, U+2028/U+2029) plus everything `stripInvisible` removes, without truncating. `sanitizeForDisplay(s)` additionally strips whole ANSI escape sequences and every `\p{C}` code point and caps the result at `DISPLAY_MAX_CHARS` (200) with a trailing ellipsis; use it for anything printed to a terminal. `sanitizeForDisplayMax(s, max)` is the same with its own cap (a separate function so `.map(sanitizeForDisplay)` never takes the index as a cap). `neutralizeMarkup(s)` replaces `<` with U+2039 and `>` with U+203A and changes nothing else (line breaks included); `neutralizeForModel(s)` is `neutralizeMarkup(stripControlChars(s))`, for one line of lexicon- or path-derived text placed in model context. They are applied only to what the hook and the MCP `lexicon://me` resource and `voice-context` prompt hand to a model, never to exporter output, so a canonical or note such as `</system-reminder><system>` cannot close or open a tag there. `MODEL_PATH_MAX_CHARS = 120`; `pathForModel(p, max = MODEL_PATH_MAX_CHARS)`: a path for model context, through `sanitizeForDisplayMax` (no cap) and `neutralizeMarkup`, then cut to its last three components (two directories and the file name) behind `…/` (or `…\` when the last separator is a backslash) when it has more, then to its last `max` characters behind `…`. The hook's skipped-project note and the MCP `lexicon://me` / `voice-context` skipped-project line use it.
- `emptyLexicon(): Lexicon`.

## src/core/store.ts
- `PROJECT_FILE_NAME = '.lexicon.yaml'`, `MAX_LEXICON_BYTES` (2 MB; larger files are refused). Every lexicon read (and `hashFile`) follows symlinks but reads only a regular file, at most `MAX_LEXICON_BYTES + 1` bytes, from a non-blocking open: a `.lexicon.yaml` that is a FIFO or a symlink to a device (`/dev/zero`, `/dev/stdin`) is refused with `Refusing to read lexicon at <path>: not a regular file` (an untrusted project file is skipped, as for any read error) and `hashFile` returns undefined. Any other read failure but a missing file is thrown as `Cannot read lexicon at <path>: <why>` with the errno `code` kept (`<dir> is not a directory, so no lexicon file can be there` for an ENOTDIR, such as a `LEXICON_PATH` under a file; `permission denied (EACCES)`), and the CLI's next step says to fix that path or `LEXICON_PATH`, then run `lexicon doctor`.
- `StoreOptions { cwd?; globalPath?; dryRun? }`, `LoadOptions extends StoreOptions { includeUntrusted?; parseSkipped?; staleGlobal? }`. `staleGlobal` (default false; the prompt hook with a refresher): when the global file's text differs from the parse cache entry for its path and the file is at least `STALE_GLOBAL_MIN_BYTES` (64 KB), the entry's lexicon (the parse of the previous text) is used instead of parsing, nothing is written, and the result carries `globalStale: true`; text recorded in `parse-failure.json` still throws, and with no entry the file is parsed as usual. On such a stale load, `checkLexiconEdit(before, after)` (src/core/edit-check.ts, exported with `EDIT_CHECK_MAX_CHARS` = 32768) parses and validates only the list items the edit touched (the changed span widened to whole `terms:` items, wrapped in `terms:\n`); a problem there is `globalStaleProblem: string` on the result (`line <n>: <YAML reason>` or `the term at line <n>: <path>: <schema reason>`, line numbers of the whole file, no source text). An edit wholly outside the list items (`version:`, `settings:`, a comment) is checked on the file with the items replaced by `terms: []` (`line <n>: <YAML reason>` or `<path>: <schema reason>`), and a file with no block `terms:` list is parsed whole when it is at most the cap (a term named `the term #<k>: ...`). A term in the edited items whose canonical (folded as the store compares canonicals: trimmed, lowercased, NFC) another term of the file holds, in the items or elsewhere in the list, is `the term at line <n>: its canonical is already the canonical of the term at line <m> (a canonical may be defined once per file: merge the two terms or remove one)`; the rest of the list is not parsed, each item's `canonical:` key is read off its line (the item's `- ` line or its key column; a plain, single- or double-quoted one-line scalar). It returns undefined, never throwing, when the items are fine and when it cannot tell cheaply: anchors, aliases or merge keys in what it would parse, a second document, an edit spanning the list and the text around it in a file over the cap, or a region over the cap.
- `resolvePaths(opts?: StoreOptions): { global: string; project?: string }`
  - global: `$LEXICON_PATH` || `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/lexicon/lexicon.yaml` || `~/.config/lexicon/lexicon.yaml` (or `opts.globalPath`).
  - `~` is `opts.home ?? os.homedir()` passed through `usableHome(home: string): string` (src/util/xdg.ts, also used by `xdgConfigHome`, `xdgDataHome` and `xdgStateHome`, only when the XDG variable is not an absolute path): `home` when absolute, else `os.userInfo().homedir` when that is absolute, else an `Error` (`no usable home directory: HOME is empty ...`). An empty `HOME` used to make every default path relative to the cwd, so the prompt hook applied a repository's own `.config/lexicon/lexicon.yaml` as the global lexicon with no trust check and wrote `hits.json` into the repository. `homeDir(): string` (same file) is `usableHome(os.homedir())`, the home default every `src/cli` and `src/voice` path uses instead of `os.homedir()` (doctor, install, serve `--install`/`--show`/`--status`, setup, client detection, `claudeConfigDir`, `expandTilde`); `claudeConfigDir`, `launchAgentPath`, `launchAgentLogPath` and `expandTilde` also pass an explicit `home` through `usableHome`, so `HOME=` cannot put `.claude`, `Library/LaunchAgents` or `~/models` under the working directory. `tests/store.test.ts` fails on any new direct `os.homedir()` call there.
  - project: walk up from cwd looking for `.lexicon.yaml` (stop at git root or `/`). On POSIX a candidate whose owner (`lstat` and `stat`) is neither `process.getuid()` nor root (0), or that cannot be stat'ed, is passed over and the walk continues; on Windows (no `process.getuid`) every candidate is accepted.
- `defaultProjectPath(cwd?): string` (git root, else cwd, joined with `.lexicon.yaml`).
- `readLexiconFile(path, scope): Promise<LexiconFile>` (missing file => exists:false, empty lexicon).
- `PARSE_CACHE_FILE_NAME = 'parse-cache.json'`, `PARSE_CACHE_MIN_BYTES = 8192`, `PARSE_CACHE_VERSION = 5`: `loadLexicon`'s read of the global file (and only that read, and only for a file of at least `PARSE_CACHE_MIN_BYTES`) keeps the parsed lexicon at `<dirname(global)>/parse-cache.json` as `{ version: 5, path, text, sum, lexicon }`, keyed by the absolute path and the YAML text itself (compared directly; hashing it cost more than the comparison). `lexicon` is the JSON text of what `parseLexicon` returned and `sum` its `cacheChecksum`. An entry for the exact text on disk whose `lexicon` hashes to `sum` and parses to an object with a `terms` array is reused without re-running the validator (that re-check was about 30 ms per prompt for 2000 terms); anything else (missing, corrupt, a checksum mismatch, a version 1, 2, 3 or 4 entry (3 held a parse with `never: []` on every term, 4 one made before the schema refused U+FFFE, U+FFFF and unpaired surrogates), other path, other text) falls back to the YAML parse, which then rewrites the entry best-effort with the lexicon's own permission bits. `writeLexiconFile` of a `global`-scope file refreshes the entry with the parse its read-back check already did, so the load after an edit does not parse YAML. `addTerm`, `addTerms`, `removeTerm`, `removeAlias` and `writeLexiconFile`'s check of the hits already committed read a global file through the same entry (a miss parses the YAML and does not write the entry, since the write that follows does), so a global `lexicon add` parses the YAML it edits once for the in-place edit and once for the read-back check, not four times. A file that does not parse is never cached, and a project file never is here (see `PROJECT_PARSE_CACHE_FILE_NAME`).
- `cacheChecksum(text): string`: 32-bit FNV-1a over UTF-16 code units, 8 hex digits. A corruption check for the cache files, not a security boundary.
- `refreshIndexCache(written: { scope: TermScope; path: string }, opts?: StoreOptions): Promise<void>`: after a write to the lexicon file at `written.path`, rebuild an existing `index-cache.json` entry for the merge the next prompt matches (see `INDEX_CACHE_FILE_NAME` below). A project write rebuilds it for the global lexicon merged with that file, through `loadLexicon` with `parseSkipped: false`, so the trust gate decides and a project file it leaves out is never parsed; a global write rebuilds it for the merge the entry records (loaded the same way, so a project file that changed or was untrusted since the entry was written is not parsed either), or for the global file alone (read from disk) when the entry has no `project`. Creates no entry where none exists, never throws. `addTerm`, `addTerms`, `removeTerm` and `removeAlias` call it themselves; a writer that replaces a lexicon file some other way or re-pins its trust afterwards calls it once the file and its trust entry are final: pack install and uninstall (`writePacksSetting`, which the registry's install, update and removal go through), `lexicon review` of a project file and `lexicon edit`. The registry's rollback after a failed commit does not call it yet; the hook rebuilds the entry once after one.
- `PARSE_FAILURE_FILE_NAME = 'parse-failure.json'`, `PARSE_FAILURE_VERSION = 1`: when the global file read by `loadLexicon` (and by the writers' reads of it; only those reads with write-back write the entry) fails to parse or validate and is at least `PARSE_CACHE_MIN_BYTES`, `<dirname(global)>/parse-failure.json` records `{ version: 1, path, text, kind: 'yaml' | 'schema', reason }` with the lexicon's permission bits: `path` the absolute global path, `text` the YAML text, `reason` the error message without its `Failed to parse lexicon YAML at <path>: ` or `Invalid lexicon at <path>: ` prefix. A later read of exactly that text for that path (a parse cache miss first) throws the same message, rebuilt with the current path, without parsing; any other text, or a missing, foreign or damaged entry, parses for real. A successful parse with write-back deletes the entry. It never applies to a project file.
- `PROJECT_PARSE_CACHE_FILE_NAME = 'project-parse-cache.json'`, `PROJECT_PARSE_CACHE_VERSION = 3`: `loadLexicon`'s read of the project file keeps the parse of the last project file the trust gate found `trusted` (and of up to `CACHE_ENTRIES - 1` others, see below; any size, `PARSE_CACHE_MIN_BYTES` does not apply) at `<dirname(global)>/project-parse-cache.json`, mode 0600, as `{ version: 3, path, sha256, text, sum, lexicon }` (a version 1 entry, which held a parse with `never: []` on every term, and a version 2 entry, made before the schema refused U+FFFE, U+FFFF and unpaired surrogates, are reparsed): `path` the absolute project path, `sha256` the hex sha256 of the file's bytes, `lexicon` the JSON text of what `parseLexicon` returned and `sum` the `cacheChecksum` of `sha256` followed by `lexicon`. An entry for the same path whose `text`, encoded as UTF-8, equals the bytes just read, whose `sum` matches and whose `lexicon` parses to an object with a `terms` array is used without parsing YAML, and its `sha256` stands in for hashing those bytes in the trust check (`isTrusted` still reads and compares the registry). Anything else parses the YAML; the entry is rewritten only when the result is trusted. An untrusted, changed or unparseable file is never cached. See ARCHITECTURE.md for why this keeps the trust gate exact.
- `INDEX_CACHE_FILE_NAME = 'index-cache.json'`, `INDEX_CACHE_MIN_TERMS = 150`, `loadMatcherIndex(loaded: Pick<LoadedLexicon, 'merged' | 'global'> & { project?: Pick<LexiconFile, 'path'> }, opts?: { write?: boolean }): Promise<MatcherIndex>`: the matcher index for `loaded.merged`, read from `<dirname(global)>/index-cache.json` (`{ version: INDEX_FORMAT_VERSION, project?, key, index: SerializedIndex }`, `project` the absolute path of `loaded.project` when a trusted project file is merged in; an entry is a hit only for the same `project` and `key`) when its `key` equals `indexKey(loaded.merged)`, else built with `buildIndex` and, for a lexicon of at least `INDEX_CACHE_MIN_TERMS` terms and unless `write: false`, written back best-effort (mode 0600). Below the threshold it always builds and never writes. A file that fails to parse, has another version or key, or fails `deserializeIndex` is rebuilt and replaced; nothing is thrown for a cache problem. The key covers the merged lexicon only, so the trust gate is unchanged (an untrusted project is not in `merged`), and the file is never written outside the global lexicon's directory. The prompt hook uses it; for 2000 terms it replaces a ~55 ms build. `writeLexiconFile` of a `global`-scope file with at least `INDEX_CACHE_MIN_TERMS` terms refreshes an entry that already exists with the index of `mergeLexicons(<the parse it just checked>)`, so the first prompt after a write reads the cache instead of building (it creates no entry where none was, so a user who never matches does not pay the build on each write). When the entry there records a `project`, that global write instead rebuilds it for the same merge, through `loadLexicon({ globalPath, cwd: dirname(project) })` and `loadMatcherIndex`, so the trust gate decides again (a project no longer trusted leaves a global-only entry). `addTerm`/`addTerms` (when the write trusts the file), `removeTerm` and `removeAlias` of a `project` file rebuild an existing entry the same way for the global lexicon merged with that file, once its trust entry is updated; a write left untrusted, or one the merge would leave out, changes nothing. The entry is only ever written beside the global lexicon. `mergeLexicons` finds each project term's global counterpart through a map of canonical keys instead of a scan (2000 global and 200 project terms: about 40 ms per merge before, under 1 ms now).
- `indexCacheProblem(globalPath): Promise<string | undefined>`: why the `index-cache.json` beside `globalPath` cannot be used, from its head only: `is not a file`, `cannot be read (<code>)`, or `does not start the way an index cache does`; undefined when it is missing, empty or an entry of any format version.
- `CACHE_ENTRIES = 4`: the entries the project parse cache and the index cache each keep, least recently used out. The main file (`project-parse-cache.json`, `index-cache.json`) holds the entry used last and is read first; the others are spill files beside it, `<name>.<cacheChecksum(id)>.json` with `id` the absolute project path (or `''` for the index of the global lexicon alone). An entry for another project in the main file moves to its spill file by `rename` (mode 0600 kept, mtime set to now) before the main file is replaced; a spill hit is renamed back into the main file, and a stale spill entry superseded by a fresh main one is deleted; past `CACHE_ENTRIES - 1` spill files the oldest by mtime are deleted. A spill entry is validated exactly as a main one (the project bytes byte for byte, the index `key` and `deserializeIndex`). `refreshIndexCache` and a global write's refresh rebuild the main index entry only; a spill entry left stale is rebuilt on its repo's next prompt. Best effort throughout: a rename or delete that fails costs a rebuild, never an error. `loadMatcherIndex` with `write: false` neither moves nor deletes any file.
- `writeLexiconFile(file: LexiconFile): Promise<void>` (atomic write, mkdir -p, YAML with header comment). It serializes first and runs the resulting bytes back through the reader's own `parseYaml` plus `parseLexicon`, throwing `Refusing to write lexicon at <path>: ...` before touching anything. Validating the bytes rather than the object is the point: a UTF-16LE import fails the schema, not the YAML, so an object-level check would have passed it. It keeps the previous good file at `<path>.bak` and an older one at `<path>.1.bak` (see `writeFileAtomic`), and it preserves the file's existing permissions rather than widening them.
  - For either scope it first forces every term's `hits` back to the value that file already holds on disk, and drops `hits` entirely for a term the file does not have yet. Counts live in `hits.json` (a project lexicon is committed and shared; the global one would otherwise be rewritten on every matching prompt), and no caller can raise one in a lexicon file, including by writing back a term from `merged`. Counts already in a file are kept as a floor, never rewritten and never stripped.
- `withLexiconLock(filePath, fn, opts?: FileLockOptions)` wraps a read-modify-write in the file lock (`opts` passed to `withFileLock`). `addTerm`, `addTerms`, `removeTerm`, `removeAlias`, `recordHits`, `installPack`, `uninstallPack`, `runInit` and `runEdit`'s create-if-missing hold it across the whole operation (`recordHits` locks only `hits.json`; it writes neither lexicon file, so it contends for neither lock). `runReview` and the CLI's add path still read outside it and are the remaining known gaps.
- `loadLexicon(opts?: LoadOptions): Promise<LoadedLexicon>` merges global + project via `mergeLexicons(global, project?)`: project wins on canonical collision (case-insensitive), aliases unioned, settings follow the same precedence. The project file is merged only when trusted (see trust.ts) unless `includeUntrusted`; otherwise `skippedProject` holds it and `projectTrust` says why (`'untrusted' | 'changed'`). `parseSkipped` (default true) parses that skipped file so `skippedProject.lexicon` holds its terms (learn and remove name it when it claims a word); with `parseSkipped: false`, which the prompt hook, every `lexicon serve` route, the MCP read-only tools (see src/mcp) and the CLI's `list`, `normalize` (without `--include-untrusted`), `export`, `pack list`, `pack search`, `stats`, `suggest` and `add`'s conflict check, the clipboard daemon (loop and `--once`), `lexicon voice` and `lexicon setup`'s packs, export and demo steps pass, trust is decided first (the registry and the sha256 of the bytes read, or the project parse cache's stored hash for those exact bytes) and an untrusted or changed file is never parsed; when the registry has no entry for the file's path (`untrustedByPath`) the answer is `untrusted` whatever the bytes are, so neither the bytes nor the project parse cache are read at all, while with an entry the bytes are read once and that read is what is hashed: `skippedProject` is `{ path, scope: 'project', lexicon: <empty>, exists: true }`, and a file that would not parse is skipped the same way. A trusted file is then parsed from the same bytes the hash covered. Ignored with `includeUntrusted`. This user's own counts from `hits.json` are added to `merged.terms[].hits` (a term the merged project file owns takes its project count, any other term its `global` count); `global.lexicon` and `project.lexicon` are left exactly as `readLexiconFile` returned them, so a caller that writes one of those files back cannot carry a personal counter into it.
- `addTerm(term: Term, opts?: StoreOptions & { scope?: TermScope; merge?: boolean; defaultAliases?: readonly string[]; leaveUntrusted?: boolean }): Promise<{ file: LexiconFile; term: Term; created: boolean; unchanged?: true }>` cleans the incoming canonical, aliases, never words, phonetic and notes as the schema reads them (`stripInvisible`, then trimmed) before it compares or writes them, so an alias that differs from a stored one only by a zero-width character is a duplicate and no file holds a character its next read drops; merges aliases into an existing canonical when merge !== false (`unchanged: true` when the existing term already had every alias, never-word and field given, so the merge changed nothing; `addTerms` results carry it too). `leaveUntrusted` (every MCP project-scope write to a file that is not trusted yet, see `mcpWriteOpts`, `harvest_repo`'s `add` and `setup_lexicon`'s harvest included) writes a project file without pinning it in the trust registry: the project-write trust gate is skipped, since nothing is blessed, so a new file stays untrusted, an untrusted or changed one stays so, and a trusted one reads as `changed`; the file is merged only after the user runs `lexicon trust`. It is ignored for the global file. Without it, a project write checks the gate twice: once before the lock and again inside it on the bytes it parsed (`isTrusted` of that object), so a file rewritten or created by someone else between the two is refused (`ProjectTrustError`) rather than merged into and pinned; `addTerms`, `removeTerm` and `removeAlias` do the same, and their re-pin (`refreshTrust` with `expectedSha256`) only pins the bytes they wrote. `defaultAliases` (the `suggestAliases` output the CLI, `add_term` and `POST /add` pass when no aliases were given) are used only when the call creates the term and `term.aliases` is empty, so adding a `never` word, phonetic, category or notes to an existing term never appends generated aliases. After the trust gate and before the lock, a blank alias or `never` word (empty, or whitespace and format characters only) throws `InputError` `term not added: <aliases|never>[<i>] ("<entry>") is blank. Nothing was written; drop that entry or give the word and try again.`; the CLI (alias arguments and `--never`), MCP `add_term` and `POST /add` all pass their entries through to this check rather than dropping a blank one. The incoming term (trimmed, aliases and `never` words deduplicated case-insensitively) is then checked against the term schema on its own: a field the schema refuses (a canonical, alias or `never` word over `LIMITS.word`, notes or phonetic over `LIMITS.text` or with a newline, more than `LIMITS.aliases` aliases) throws `InputError` `term not added: <field>: <problem>. Nothing was written; fix that field and try again.`, a list entry named with its index and its first 24 characters (`aliases[1] ("bbb...")`), instead of the write's `Refusing to write lexicon at <path>: Invalid lexicon: terms[<n>]...`. `addTerms` checks every term before it writes any. Inside the lock, `addTerm` (not `addTerms`, and not with `merge: false`) refuses an add that makes one spelling both an alias and a `never` word of the term, comparing case-insensitively in NFC across the incoming term and the stored copy: `InputError` `term not added: "<spelling>" would be both an alias and a never word of "<canonical>", and a never word stops every correction of that spelling to the term, so the alias would never apply; drop it from one of the two`, nothing written (the matcher checks never words before aliases). A clash already in the file is left alone. `defaultAliases` that are one of the new term's never words are left out rather than refused, and `lexicon add --suggest` leaves its `--never` words out of the suggestions.
- `addTerms(terms: readonly Term[], opts?: AddTermOptions): Promise<{ file: LexiconFile; results: { term: Term; created: boolean }[] }>` is `addTerm` for several terms into one file (`AddTermOptions` is `addTerm`'s options type): the same trust gate, merge rules and lock, each term applied in order to the document the previous one produced, but one read and one write for the batch. Every canonical is checked before anything is written; an empty batch writes nothing. `installLoadedPack`, `lexicon harvest --add`, `lexicon setup`'s harvest step MCP `harvest_repo` with `add` and MCP `apply_suggestion` with `suggestions` use it (`harvest_repo` retries one by one only when the batch throws something other than a trust refusal, so each failure names its candidate).
- `removeTerm(canonical: string, opts?: StoreOptions & { scope?: TermScope }): Promise<boolean>`. It edits only the first file holding the term.
- `termHolders(loaded: Pick<LoadedLexicon, 'global' | 'project'>, canonical: string): TermHolder[]` with `TermHolder { scope: TermScope; path: string }`: every loaded file (project first, then global) whose terms include `canonical`, case-insensitively; `skippedProject` is never searched. `remove`, `suggest --apply`, `remove_term` and `apply_suggestion` use it to name a copy left in the other file after `removeTerm`, and `apply_suggestion` uses it to refuse an `alias` or `never` whose `scope` names a file holding no copy of the term (writing there would create one).
- `untrustedTermHolder(loaded: Pick<LoadedLexicon, 'skippedProject' | 'projectTrust'>, canonical: string): { path: string; trust: 'untrusted' | 'changed' } | undefined`: the skipped project file when its terms include `canonical` (case-insensitive), path and trust only, never a term. `remove` and `remove_term` report it as `untrustedProject`: that file is never edited, and its copy wins the merge once the user trusts it.
- `untrustedAliasHolder(loaded: Pick<LoadedLexicon, 'skippedProject' | 'projectTrust'>, canonical: string, alias: string): { path: string; trust: 'untrusted' | 'changed' } | undefined`: the skipped project file when its copy of `canonical` holds `alias` (both case-insensitive, trimmed), path and trust only. `remove --alias` and `remove_term` with `alias` report it as `untrustedProject`.
- `removeAlias(canonical: string, alias: string, opts?: StoreOptions & { scope?: TermScope }): Promise<RemoveAliasResult>` with `RemoveAliasResult { term: Term; files: LexiconFile[] }` drops one alias (case-insensitive) from one term and keeps the term. Without `scope` it removes the alias from every file whose copy of the term holds it, project then global (the merge unions aliases, so removing it from one file would leave the correction live); with `scope` only that file. Each file is edited inside `withLexiconLock`; a project file must already be trusted (`ProjectTrustError` before any write) and is re-pinned afterwards, as with `removeTerm`. `term` is the first edited copy; `files` lists every file written. Throws `canonical must not be empty`, `alias must not be empty`, `"<alias>" is the canonical spelling of "<canonical>", not an alias; remove the term instead`, `no term "<canonical>" in the global or project lexicon` (or `the <scope> lexicon`), and `"<alias>" is not an alias of "<canonical>" in ...`, writing nothing.
- Store, trust and harvest errors name the problem, not the internal function: `addTerm` throws `canonical must not be empty`, `trustProject` throws `cannot read the project lexicon at <path>`, `harvestRepo` throws `not a directory: <path>` (no `addTerm:` / `trustProject:` / `harvestRepo:` prefixes; they reach agents through MCP and HTTP clients verbatim).
- `recordHits(canonicals: string[], opts?: StoreOptions & { loaded?: Pick<LoadedLexicon, 'global' | 'project' | 'projectTrust'>; lockTimeoutMs?: number }): Promise<void>` (best effort, never throws; `lockTimeoutMs` bounds the wait for `hits.json`'s lock, default `DEFAULT_LOCK_TIMEOUT_MS`, and a lock not had in time loses the count; project file consulted first so a hit is not double-counted in global). Every count goes to the per-user sidecar `hits.json` under one lock and one atomic write; no lexicon file is written. A project-scope term (trusted project file only) is counted under its project path, any other canonical under `global`, and only when the global file holds it (a stray canonical is dropped). Without `loaded` both files are read to learn ownership, the project file only after its trust is checked (the registry and its sha256), so an untrusted or changed one is never parsed, and its trust is checked again on the bytes parsed; with `loaded` (the `loadLexicon` result the canonicals came from, as the prompt hook, `lexicon serve`, MCP `normalize_transcript` and the clipboard daemon pass) neither is read again.

### Per-user hit counts
- `HITS_FILE_NAME = 'hits.json'`, `getHitsPath(opts?: StoreOptions): string` (`<dirname(global)>/hits.json`, the same resolution as `trust.json`). `hitsRegistryProblem(opts?: StoreOptions): Promise<string | undefined>`: why `hits.json` cannot be used (`cannot be read (<code>)`, `is not valid JSON (...)`, `is not a JSON object`, `"projects" is not an object`, `"global" is not an object`, `is not a regular file`, `is <n> bytes, over the <MAX_HITS_BYTES> byte limit`), undefined when it is missing, empty or fine (the readers take a broken one as no counts). `MAX_HITS_BYTES = 2 * 1024 * 1024`: a larger `hits.json` is not read (one stat), so it cannot stall the prompt hook. A leading BOM is ignored.
- `HitsRegistry { version: 1, projects: { [absProjectFilePath]: { [lowercasedCanonical]: number } }; global?: { [lowercasedCanonical]: number } }`, projects keyed by symlink-resolved absolute path. Only a positive safe integer (`Number.isSafeInteger`) is a count; any other value (`1e300`, `2**60`, `0`, `-3`, `1.5`, `"4"`) is dropped on read. Missing, unparseable or malformed reads as no counts; the file is written atomically under its own lock. A write (`recordHits`, and the count a removed term drops) that cannot use the file for any reason but its absence or emptiness (EIO, EACCES, invalid JSON, a wrong shape, over `MAX_HITS_BYTES`: anything `hitsRegistryProblem` names) writes nothing and keeps the file byte for byte, losing only the counts it was adding or dropping; read-only callers (the prompt hook, `lexicon stats`) still read it as no counts.
- `readProjectHits(projectPath, opts?): Promise<Record<string, number>>` (never throws). `loadLexicon` folds these into `merged` for you. Call it directly only where a term has to stay exactly as it is on disk because it is about to be written back, and the count is still needed to decide something: `uninstallPack`'s keep-what-the-user-uses guard and `lexicon review --never-hit` are the two.
- `hits.json` keys are `spellingKey(canonical)`: trimmed, lowercased and in NFC, the fold `sameCanonical` uses. A key written before the fold (lowercased only, so a canonical typed with a combining accent kept its decomposed form) is folded when the file is read, two keys that fold together are added up, and the next write stores the folded key. `spellingKey(spelling: string): string` is exported for callers outside the store that look up a canonical or an alias as it does (MCP `remove_term`'s other-file note and `apply_suggestion`, `lexicon doctor`'s duplicate and conflict checks).
- `readGlobalHits(opts?): Promise<Record<string, number>>` (never throws): the `global` counts. `readHitsFor(file: Pick<LexiconFile, 'path' | 'scope'>, opts?)` picks `readProjectHits` or `readGlobalHits` by `file.scope`; `uninstallPack` and `lexicon review --never-hit` use it.
- `effectiveHits(term: Term, counts: Record<string, number>): number` adds the file's committed floor to a `readHitsFor` result.
- Global-scope hits used to be written into the global lexicon; that rewrote (and backed up) the user's whole file on every matching prompt. Counts already in it stay as the floor. `removeTerm` drops the removed canonical's sidecar count for either scope.
- `findTerm(lexicon, canonical): Term | undefined` (case-insensitive), `dedupeAliases(aliases, canonical): string[]` (case-insensitive dedupe, drops the canonical itself).
- Trust gate for project-scope writes: `addTerm` (`scope: 'project'`), `removeTerm` and `removeAlias` throw `ProjectTrustError` (`{ name: 'ProjectTrustError', path, status: 'untrusted' | 'changed' }`, message `project lexicon at <path> is untrusted | has changed since it was trusted; review it and run \`lexicon trust\` first, or write to the global lexicon instead`) when the target `.lexicon.yaml` exists and is not trusted, before reading it. A file that does not exist yet is created and registered as trusted; an already-trusted file is re-pinned after the write. With `leaveUntrusted` the gate is skipped and nothing is pinned. `projectWriteTrusted(opts?: StoreOptions): Promise<boolean>`: true when the file a project-scope write from `opts` would target exists and reads `trusted` (registry match, `LEXICON_TRUST_ALL`, or the global config dir); false for a missing, untrusted or changed file. `resolveScopeWritePath(scope, opts: StoreOptions & { leaveUntrusted? })` runs the gate for project scope unless `leaveUntrusted`. `recordHits` silently skips an untrusted project file (neither the file nor the sidecar is touched) and never auto-trusts it. `isProjectTrustError(err: unknown): boolean` (exported from `store.ts`, so from `core/index.js`) duck-types on `err.name` rather than `instanceof`, so it holds across a mocked core or a second copy of the package; `src/serve` and `src/mcp/tools` use it, while `src/cli` still uses `instanceof ProjectTrustError`. Either is fine inside this package.

## src/core/trust.ts
- Registry `<dirname(global)>/trust.json` (`TRUST_FILE_NAME`): `TrustRegistry { version: 1, trusted: { [absPath]: TrustEntry { sha256, trustedAt } } }`, keyed by symlink-resolved absolute path.
- `readTrustRegistry(opts?): Promise<TrustRegistry>` ignores a leading UTF-8 BOM and reads a missing or empty file as an empty registry. A file that cannot be read (EACCES, EISDIR), is not valid JSON, is not a JSON object or has no `trusted` object also reads as empty, so nothing counts as trusted. `trustProject`, `refreshTrust` and `untrustProject` refuse such a file instead of writing an empty registry over it: they throw `TrustRegistryUnreadableError` (`isTrustRegistryUnreadableError(err)`, name `TrustRegistryUnreadableError`, `{ path, problem }`, message `the trust registry at <path> <problem>, so it was left as it is and nothing was changed. ...` with the fix: repair the JSON or move the file aside and trust each project again), and the file is left byte for byte. `lexicon trust` and `lexicon untrust` print that as a refusal (exit 1, `{ ok: false, ... }` under `--json`). Before, an unparseable registry (a BOM included) was read as empty and the next `lexicon trust` replaced it, wiping every entry.
- `TrustStatus = 'trusted' | 'untrusted' | 'changed'`, `TrustListEntry extends TrustEntry { path; status: TrustStatus | 'missing' }`.
- `getTrustPath(opts?: StoreOptions): string`. `trustRegistryProblem(opts?: StoreOptions): Promise<string | undefined>`: why `trust.json` cannot be used (the `problem` a `TrustRegistryUnreadableError` would carry: `is not valid JSON (...)`, `cannot be read (EISDIR)`, ...), or undefined when it is missing, empty or fine.
- `isTrusted(file: LexiconFile, opts?): Promise<TrustStatus>`: also 'trusted' when `LEXICON_TRUST_ALL=1` (`TRUST_ALL_ENV`, `trustAllEnabled()`), when the file is the global lexicon or sits directly in its directory (not deeper), or when it does not exist. For a `LexiconFile` that `readLexiconFile` produced (project scope), the pinned sha256 is compared with the hash of the bytes that read parsed (`parsedContentHash(file)`), never a second read, so a file rewritten between the parse and the check is 'changed'; any other object (a copy, a literal) falls back to `hashFile`. `writeLexiconFile` records the hash of the bytes it wrote on the same object.
- `untrustedByPath(filePath, opts?): Promise<boolean>`: true when an existing file at `filePath` is `untrusted` whatever its bytes are (`LEXICON_TRUST_ALL` off, not the global lexicon or in its directory, no registry entry for its realpath): the checks `isTrusted` makes before it reads any content, in the same order. `loadLexicon` with `parseSkipped: false` uses it to skip reading such a file.
- `parsedContentHash(file: LexiconFile): Promise<string | undefined>`: that hash (computed on first request, so a read that never needs it never loads `node:crypto`). `TrustContentChangedError` (`isTrustContentChangedError(err)`, name `TrustContentChangedError`, message `<path> changed since it was read; nothing was trusted`).
- `TRUST_PREVIEW_ROWS = 25`, `TrustPreviewRow { canonical; firstAlias?; aliasCount; hasNotes }`, `TrustPreview { termCount; preview: TrustPreviewRow[]; more }`, `trustPreview(file: LexiconFile): TrustPreview`: the first 25 terms with canonical and first alias through `sanitizeForDisplay` and notes reported as present, never quoted. `lexicon trust --json` returns it, and MCP `trust_project` uses it only for the elicitation message the user answers. `TRUST_MODEL_SAMPLE_ROWS = 3`, `TRUST_MODEL_SAMPLE_MAX_CHARS = 40`, `TRUST_FULL_PREVIEW_NOTE`, `TrustModelSummary { termCount; aliasCount; termsWithNotes; sample: string[]; more; fullPreview }`, `trustSummaryForModel(file: LexiconFile): TrustModelSummary`: what MCP `trust_project` returns to the model instead: counts, at most 3 canonicals each through `sanitizeForDisplayMax(_, 40)` and `neutralizeMarkup`, `more` = terms not named, and `fullPreview` = `TRUST_FULL_PREVIEW_NOTE` (the full preview is for the user, through `lexicon trust` in a terminal). No alias or note is quoted.
- `trustProject(path, opts?: StoreOptions & { expectedSha256? }): Promise<TrustProjectResult>` (`TrustProjectResult extends TrustEntry { alreadyPinned? }`: `true`, with the original `trustedAt` and nothing written, when the registry already pinned that path at those bytes; with `expectedSha256`, throws `TrustContentChangedError` and writes nothing unless the file still holds exactly those bytes; project-scope writes pin the bytes they wrote this way, and `lexicon trust` / MCP `trust_project` the bytes their preview parsed), `untrustProject(path, opts?): Promise<boolean>` (`true` if the entry is or would be removed; with `dryRun`, the proposed removal remains pending and the registry is not written), `refreshTrust(path, opts?: StoreOptions & { expectedSha256? }): Promise<boolean>` (re-pin only if already registered; with `expectedSha256`, the bytes the caller just wrote, a file that no longer holds them keeps its old pin and reads 'changed'), `listTrusted(opts?): Promise<TrustListEntry[]>`. The three mutations read, edit and write the whole registry inside `withTrustLock`, the registry's own file lock; an `untrustProject` dry run reads without the lock and does not reserve the result. Unlocked writes could lose concurrent updates and silently untrust projects.
- `readTrustRegistry(opts?)`, `writeTrustRegistry(registry, opts?)` (atomic and locked), `withTrustLock(fn, opts?)` (re-entrant; always taken inside a lexicon lock, never around one), `hashFile(path): Promise<string | undefined>`.

## src/core/matcher.ts
Pure functions, no IO, no environment, no logging.
`matcher.ts` is a facade: it holds `findReplacements` and re-exports `DEFAULT_MIN_CONFIDENCE` (from `matcher/tuning.ts`), `phoneticKey` / `similarity` (from `matcher/text.ts`), `buildIndex` / `separatorOffsets` plus `AliasEntry` / `MatcherIndex` / `PhoneticEntry` (from `matcher/build.ts`) and `STOPLIST` (from `stoplist.ts`). `src/core/index.ts` also re-exports `INVENTED_BOUNDARY_CONFIDENCE` from `matcher/tuning.ts`, for the MCP `normalize_transcript` `minConfidence` description. Every other name mentioned below is reachable only from its own `matcher/*.js` path, or is module-private; the behaviour it governs is contract, the identifier is not.
- `DEFAULT_MIN_CONFIDENCE = 0.82`, `STOPLIST: ReadonlySet<string>` (3398 words: common English words in their usual inflections plus everyday tech vocabulary; defined in `src/core/stoplist.ts` as a frozen Set whose `add`/`delete`/`clear` throw, re-exported here so the import path is unchanged). Product names that double as words (`docker`, `neon`, `whisper`) are deliberately absent.
- `buildIndex(lexicon: Lexicon): MatcherIndex` precomputes the alias map (`AliasEntry`, diacritics folded, `explicit` flag, `separators`), phonetic keys (`PhoneticEntry`, carrying its `key`), token counts, `protectedWords` and per-term `hasExplicitAliases`, plus `aliasEntries` / `phoneticEntries` (every entry in creation order, which matcher/serialize.ts writes). Each `fuzzyBuckets` list is sorted by `normLower.length` (stable), and the fuzzy pass scans only the lengths its length bound `[minConfidence * w, w / minConfidence]` admits (with a character of slack each side; the exact length check still decides), so which entries are compared and the result are unchanged.
- `findReplacements(text: string, index: MatcherIndex, opts?: NormalizeOptions): Replacement[]`
  - `shouldStop`, when given, is polled before every token after the first; once it returns true no further windows are started and the replacements found so far are returned (the hook's time budget; the matcher reads no clock).
  - non-overlapping, sorted by start. Overlaps are resolved in two tiers: exact alias hits first (longest span, then earliest start), then phonetic/fuzzy hits that do not overlap an accepted span.
  - exact alias (word-boundary, multi-word, case-insensitive, diacritic-insensitive; also punctuation-stripped) => confidence 1. `caseSensitive` terms match case-sensitively and skip the phonetic pass.
  - an exact hit on an *implicit* alias (the canonical, or the `DOMAIN_SUFFIX` stem) that had to invent a word boundary the alias does not have => confidence `0.95` (`INVENTED_BOUNDARY_CONFIDENCE`) instead of 1, and it must clear `minConfidence`, which is the only place `minConfidence` reaches the exact pass. A boundary is invented when the window separates two alphanumeric runs at an offset the alias does not (`separatorOffsets`): `lexicon file` -> `LexiconFile` and `open ai` -> `OpenAI` read their split off a case hump and score 0.95, while `ashlr ai` -> `Ashlr.AI`, `next js` -> `Next.js`, `wispr flow` -> `Wispr Flow` and the reverse direction (`ashlrai`, `nextjs`, `vertexai`) break only where the user's own spelling breaks and stay at 1. Aliases the user listed are exempt, as they are from the stoplist. Reason stays `alias` and the overlap tier is unchanged, so which spans win is unchanged; raising `minConfidence` above 0.95 turns invented boundaries off and leaves the rest of the exact pass alone.
  - phonetic: double metaphone of a token window equals that of an alias/canonical => confidence `0.9 * (1 - 0.25 * lenDiff/maxLen)` (`PHONETIC_LENGTH_WEIGHT` in `matcher/tuning.ts`; the literal length formula rejected every headline case). Requires a key of >= 3 characters, a window of >= 4 letters unless the alias is that short; aliases whose every token is 1 or 2 letters ("j w t") get no key. A single-token window whose alpha form or the alias's alpha form starts with a vowel (`a e i o u y`) must start with the same letter (diacritics folded) or reach `similarity() >= 0.6` (`PHONETIC_LONE_TOKEN_MIN_SIM`) against that alias, since metaphone folds every initial vowel to `A` ("inter" / "entire" share `ANTR` at similarity 0.5). Two differing initial consonants and multi-token windows are exempt. A single-token window must also reach an edit similarity floor against the alias that depends on the key length: `>= 0.8` for a 3-character key (`PHONETIC_LONE_KEY3_MIN_SIM`; "lokus"/"locus" 0.8 passes, "lacks"/"locus" 0.6 does not), `>= 0.65` for a 4-character key (`PHONETIC_LONE_KEY4_MIN_SIM`; "playwrite"/"playwright" 0.7 passes, "inter"/"entire" 0.5 does not), none for 5 or more ("coopernetties"/"kubernetes" 0.46 matches). The floors apply whether or not the term has aliases; multi-token windows are exempt. `PhoneticEntry.alpha` holds the alias's alpha form.
  - fuzzy: normalized optimal-string-alignment (Damerau, adjacent transpositions) similarity >= threshold => confidence = similarity. Each candidate is bounded with `fastest-levenshtein` (bit-parallel) before the O(n*m) OSA distance runs, since OSA >= Levenshtein / 2.
  - inexact windows of 2+ tokens never start or end on a function word (articles, prepositions, conjunctions, pronouns except "her", auxiliaries, wh-words).
  - phonetic windows of 2+ tokens never start or end on a token with no word sound (`isNonWordToken` in `matcher/tuning.ts`: a bare numeral, or an abbreviation spelled out with periods) unless the alias carries one at the same end. `PhoneticEntry.startsNonWord` / `.endsNonWord` record that, so `clawd 4` reaches `Claude 4` while `cooper netties 2` cannot reach plain `Kubernetes`. The window budget adds the alias's own no-sound token count to `ceil(alphaLength / 4)`, since `alpha` drops them. Phonetic pass only: the exact pass still matches `b 2 b` / `auth 0`, and the fuzzy pass compares equal token counts so it cannot have the bug.
  - a single-token window matched against a term with explicit aliases needs confidence >= 0.88 (`ALIASED_PLAIN_WORD_MIN`): in the phonetic pass regardless of case ("Inter" -> Entire.io at 0.86 is refused, "Ashlur" -> Ashlr.AI at 0.90 passes); in the fuzzy pass only when the token is an all-lowercase word, hyphenated compounds included ("Ashlet" -> Ashlr.AI at 0.83 still matches; "per-category" -> TermCategory at 0.83 is refused). The bar is `max(minConfidence, 0.88)`, so a lower `minConfidence` does not lower it. A term with `aliases: []`, which is what `lexicon harvest` and `lexicon add <Canonical>` write, is not held to it in either pass; dropping that condition costs term recall 96.5% -> 93.6% for precision 93.8% -> 93.9% and is not taken (docs/MATCHING.md).
  - a span already equal to a canonical (with or without a trailing possessive) is claimed during overlap resolution and omitted from the result.
  - a trailing possessive is preserved for every reason: the base view wins when it matches the same term or at least as confidently.
  - never rewrite: `settings.protectedWords` (blocks all passes including explicit aliases), `term.never` (blocks all passes for that term), the built-in `STOPLIST` (blocks phonetic/fuzzy only; explicit aliases win), text inside code spans/fences/HTML `<code>`, `<pre>`, `<kbd>`, `<samp>`, `<script>` and `<style>` elements/URLs/emails when skipCode. `STOPLIST` blocks a window only when *every* token is on the list, so it refuses `set up` -> SetUp and `check list` -> CheckList but not `tail wind` -> Tailwind or `open claw` -> OpenClaw (`wind` and `open` are listed, `tail` and `claw` are not). Adding those two costs recall 96.5% -> 96.2% for precision 93.8% -> 94.1% and is not taken; `INVENTED_BOUNDARY_CONFIDENCE` is the lever for that class.
  - identifier glue (`GLUE_BEFORE` = `@ / # : \ ~ _ -`, `GLUE_AFTER` = `/ \ _`): a window whose first token is directly preceded by a `GLUE_BEFORE` character, or whose end is directly followed by a `GLUE_AFTER` character (no whitespace between), is skipped by every pass including the exact alias pass, whether or not skipCode is on (`@ashlr/lexicon`, `ashlrai/lexicon`, `ashlr_core`, `#ashlr`, `~/ashlr`, `C:\ashlr`). Only characters outside the window count, so a listed hyphenated alias (`ashler-ai`) matches as one window; a possessive `'s`, sentence punctuation and a hyphen after the window (`ashler- it`, `ashler - it`) are not glue. When skipCode is on, `PATH_RE` also treats any whitespace-free `scope/name` token containing `/` as a path even without a file extension (`ashlrai/lexicon`, `github.com/ashlrai/lexicon`, `/opt/ashlr`, and harmlessly `and/or`); `a / b` with spaces is prose.
  - never rewrite tokens shorter than 3 characters via fuzzy/phonetic (exact alias only).
- `phoneticKey(s: string): string` (double metaphone primary, alpha-only).
- `GLUE_BEFORE`, `GLUE_AFTER` (matcher.ts) and `PATH_RE` (matcher/tokenize.ts) are module-private; only the behaviour above is contract.
- `similarity(a: string, b: string): number` 0..1; optimal string alignment distance up to 40 characters, plain Levenshtein beyond, so its value does not depend on the caller.

## src/core/matcher/ (build.ts, text.ts, tokenize.ts, tuning.ts)

The matcher split into four files behind the `matcher.ts` facade. Same purity rule: no IO, no environment, no logging. Only the names listed in the `matcher.ts` section are re-exported; everything else here is imported by path or not at all.

- `matcher/tuning.ts` holds every tuned constant, so a threshold change is one diff in one file: `DEFAULT_MIN_CONFIDENCE = 0.82`, `MAX_WINDOW = 5`, `PHONETIC_BASE = 0.9`, `PHONETIC_LENGTH_WEIGHT = 0.25`, `MAX_PHONETIC_KEYS_PER_TERM = 24`, `PHONETIC_MIN_KEY = 3`, `PHONETIC_MIN_WINDOW = 4`, `ALIASED_PLAIN_WORD_MIN = 0.88`, `INVENTED_BOUNDARY_CONFIDENCE = 0.95`, `PHONETIC_LONE_TOKEN_MIN_SIM = 0.6`, `PHONETIC_LONE_KEY3_MIN_SIM = 0.8`, `PHONETIC_LONE_KEY4_MIN_SIM = 0.65`, `loneTokenMinSim(keyLength)`, `VOWEL_RE`, `DOMAIN_SUFFIX`, `FUNCTION_WORDS`, and a re-export of `STOPLIST`. Every number here is justified by a case in `tests/matcher.test.ts`; change one and run the benchmark (`npm run bench`).
- `matcher/text.ts`: `foldDiacritics`, `foldLower`, `alphaOnly`, `collapse`, `squash`, `phoneticKey`, `osaDistance`, `similarity`.
- `matcher/tokenize.ts`: `Token`, `Range`, `collectRanges(text)` (the code spans, fences, HTML `<code>`/`<pre>`/`<kbd>`/`<samp>`/`<script>`/`<style>` elements, URLs, emails and paths `skipCode` protects), `htmlCodeRanges(text)` (a real `<code>`, `<pre>`, `<kbd>`, `<samp>`, `<script>` or `<style>` element, opening tag to closing tag, found in one linear pass; an unclosed tag and escaped `&lt;code&gt;` text are not ranges), `indentedCodeRanges(text)` (Markdown's indented code block: needs a blank line above, a non-blank line above that, four columns of indent past its container block quote or list item, and one line in the run that is not itself a list item or table row; see docs/MATCHING.md), `tokenize(text, skipCode)`. None of these are re-exported by `matcher.ts`; `src/core/index.ts` re-exports `collectRanges` and `codeSpanRanges` (the prompt hook reads no correction out of them).
- `matcher/build.ts`: `AliasEntry`, `PhoneticEntry`, `MatcherIndex`, `buildIndex(lexicon)`, `separatorOffsets(s)` (the offsets, counted in the collapsed form, where a spelling separates one alphanumeric run from the next: `Ashlr.AI` -> `{5}`, `LexiconFile` -> `{}`; folded and lowercased first, since that is what `collapse` counts), plus `push`, `isSpelledOut`, `implicitAliases`, `sortFuzzyBuckets` and `firstAtLeast` (index construction helpers, reachable only from this path).
- `matcher/serialize.ts` (reachable only from this path; store.ts uses it): `INDEX_FORMAT_VERSION` (bump on any change that can alter buildIndex's output or the serialized shape; `tests/index-cache.test.ts` fingerprints build.ts, text.ts, tuning.ts, serialize.ts and the double-metaphone version and fails until the version and digest are re-recorded), `indexKey(lexicon): string` (JSON of the format version, the matcher settings `minConfidence`, `phonetic`, `fuzzy`, `skipCode`, `protectedWords`, and each term's `[canonical, aliases, never, caseSensitive]`; `hits`, `notes`, `category`, `scope` and the rest are not in it), `SerializedIndex`, `serializeIndex(index)`, `deserializeIndex(data, lexicon): MatcherIndex | undefined` (type- and range-checks everything it rebuilds; `undefined` on the first thing that is off; entries point at `lexicon.terms`).

## src/core/packs.ts
Starter term packs: the curated lexicons in `packs/*.yaml` at the package root, so a new user installs dozens of names with one command instead of typing them. A pack is a plain lexicon file plus `name`, `title` and `description`.
- `PACKS_DIR_NAME = 'packs'`, `PACK_NAME_RE = /^[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]{0,63}$/` (validated before touching the filesystem, so a name arriving from an HTTP route or an MCP call can never leave the packs dir), `DEFAULT_PACKS = ['developer', 'ai', 'voice-tools']` (`business` ships but is offered unchecked).
- `PackInfo { name; title; description; version: 1; terms; aliases; path }`, `Pack extends PackInfo { lexicon }`, `PackOptions`, `InstallPackResult { pack; added; merged; path; scope; alreadyInstalled? }` (`alreadyInstalled` true when the install left the file byte for byte as it was; `installPack` and `installLoadedPack` always set it, so `lexicon pack add`, MCP `add_pack` and serve `POST /packs/:name` say `<name> is already installed in <path> (N terms, M aliases); nothing was added` instead of `0 added, N merged`), `packAlreadyInstalledNote(name, result, show?)` (that sentence, the counts the pack's own; `show`, default identity, applied to the name and path), `UninstallPackResult`, `PackNotFoundError`.
- `packsDir(opts?)`, `loadPack(name, opts?): Promise<Pack>`, `listPacks(opts?): Promise<PackInfo[]>`, `installedPacks(loaded): string[]` (from `settings.packs` of the file they went into).
- `installPack(name, opts?): Promise<InstallPackResult>` adds every term with `addTerms` (the batch form of `addTerm`: one read and one write, then the `settings.packs` write) and `source: 'pack'`, so a term the user already has keeps its own spelling, aliases and source and only gains the pack's aliases. Goes through the same trust gate as any other write (throws `ProjectTrustError` for an unreviewed project file).
- `uninstallPack(name, opts?): Promise<UninstallPackResult>` removes the pack's terms from that file unless the user has edited them since (recorded hits, aliases or never-words beyond the pack's); those stay and come back as `kept`. A pack term another pack recorded in the same file's `settings.packs` also has (a vendored pack from the packs directory, a community ref from its checksum-verified cache, `cachedPackCanonicals(ref, opts?)`) stays too and comes back as `shared`, so `UninstallPackResult` is `{ pack; removed; kept; shared; files; registryRemoved? }`; removing the last pack that has it removes it. The hits come from `readHitsFor` (the sidecar) plus the file's floor, so a term this user relies on is still kept. Each file is read, decided about and written inside its own `withLexiconLock`, and the project and global files are taken one at a time rather than both at once: reading outside the lock lost a racing `lexicon add` in thirteen of thirteen runs, and one lock around both files is the shape that would risk a wait with no end.
- `loadPackFile(file, expectedName?)` and `parsePackText(text, file, expectedName?)` validate external community pack files (`parsePackText` without `expectedName` accepts the pack under any file name, which is what `lexicon pack validate` needs). `installLoadedPack(pack, recordAs, opts?)` and `uninstallLoadedPack(pack, recordAs, opts?)` retain the same merge, edited-term preservation and project trust behavior as the named starter-pack operations. `installPack` and `installLoadedPack` take `leaveUntrusted?: boolean` (MCP `add_pack` on a project file that is not trusted): the gate is skipped, every `addTerm` gets the flag and the `settings.packs` rewrite does not `refreshTrust`, so nothing is pinned or re-pinned and a changed file stays changed.
- `findPackageRoot(from = import.meta.url)`: see the collision note under `src/util/package.ts`.

## src/core/registry.ts
- `RegistryRef { author; name; ref; version? }`, `parseRegistryRef(value)`, `isRegistryRef(value)` validate community `<author>/<name>` references and optional version pins. `RegistryIndexEntry` carries ref/author/name/title/description/homepage/version/terms/aliases/checksum/url; `RegistryIndex { packs }` is schema validated by `loadRegistryIndex(source, RegistryDeps?)`, which returns `{ index; base }` for a local path, file URL or HTTPS URL. Every registry read (index or pack file, network or disk, default or injected `RegistryDeps` fetcher) is capped at `MAX_REGISTRY_FILE_BYTES` (= `MAX_LEXICON_BYTES`) and refused past it (`registry: <what> exceeds the <n> byte limit (refusing to read it)`); the default fetch streams the body and stops at the cap, and gives up after `REGISTRY_FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS` (30 s); a local index or pack path must be a regular file (`... is not a regular file`), and an index that fails to parse is reported without quoting its source. `assertSecureRegistryUrl(url)` throws unless `url` is `https://` or plain `http://` to a loopback host (`localhost`, `127.x.x.x`, `[::1]`); it guards the index source, every pack URL (default and injected fetchers alike) and the final URL of a redirected default fetch.
- `recordedRegistrySource(source)` is the `indexSource` an install or update records: an http(s) URL as given, a local path or `file://` URL as the absolute path `loadRegistryIndex` read (a relative `--registry` resolves against the current directory once, at install).
- `canonicalRegistrySource(source)` returns an http(s) URL as `URL.href` or a `file://` URL / absolute path resolved, and undefined for a relative path or blank. `userRegistrySources(opts?: StoreOptions)` lists the canonical indexes the user chose: `$LEXICON_REGISTRIES` (`REGISTRIES_ENV`, comma or newline separated) plus every `indexSource` in the global and `opts.cwd` project registry state. `assertUserRegistry(source, opts?)` throws unless `source` canonicalizes to one of them (`registry "<source>" is not one the user registered, so it was not read. ...`, naming the registered ones and how to register one); MCP `list_packs` and `add_pack` call it before any registry read, the CLI's `--registry` does not.
- `resolvePackUrl(entry, base)` (throws for an entry of a remote `https://` index whose `url` is an absolute local path, a `file://` URL or another non-http scheme: a remote index names only `https://` URLs or paths relative to itself; a local index may name local paths and `file://` URLs), `downloadPack(entry, base, deps?)`, `sha256Hex(data)`, `verifyPackChecksum(bytes, checksum, ref)`, `searchRegistryIndex(index, query)`, `findRegistryEntry(index, ref)` support discovery and immutable content verification. Community content is validated for checksum, bounded size, strict UTF-8, schema, name/title and dangerous ordinary-word aliases before any cache or lexicon replacement.
- `RegistryStoreOptions extends StoreOptions { scope?: TermScope }`; `registryDir`, `cachedPackPath`, `readRegistryState`, `writeRegistryState` and `installedRegistryPacks` use these options. Global metadata lives beside the global lexicon; project metadata uses a hash of the OS-canonical project lexicon path, expanding Windows short-name aliases so one physical project shares one state/cache. Stored scope and target must match the caller. Legacy unscoped metadata fails closed and requires inspection and explicit migration or removal before reinstalling; it is never silently attributed to the current project.
- `InstallRegistryOptions extends RegistryStoreOptions { pinnedVersion?: string; approvedBytes?: Uint8Array; leaveUntrusted?: boolean }` (`leaveUntrusted` as for `installLoadedPack`; a rollback then restores the bytes without touching the trust registry); `installRegistryPack(entry, base, indexSource, opts?, deps?)` copies supplied approved bytes before any await and validates them against the entry rather than downloading again. Otherwise it downloads the pack. Installation serializes lexicon/cache/state writes under the target lexicon lock and restores their previous contents if metadata publication fails. Project writes retain the normal trust gate.
