- `previewRegistryUpdates(refs: string[] | undefined, indexSource, opts?, deps?)` returns `RegistryUpdatePreview[]` with status/current version/error plus complete incoming `terms` and `changed: { before: Term; after: Term }[]`, including aliases, phonetic, caseSensitive and notes. Available previews bind `scopeIdentity`, `installedChecksum`, `installedStateDigest` and `approvedChecksum` alongside entry/base; `applyRegistryUpdate(preview, indexSource, opts?, deps?)` rejects a changed destination, checksum or validated installation record (including version pin, index source and timestamp) under the target lock before applying. Missing state digests require a new preview. Removed canonical names are reported but existing terms are kept.
- `RegistryRecordMismatch { fileOnly: string[]; recordOnly: string[] }`; `registryRecordMismatch(loaded, scope, recorded): RegistryRecordMismatch | undefined` (pure) compares one scope's loaded file `settings.packs` refs with that scope's registry records; `findRegistryRecordMismatch(loaded, opts?: StoreOptions): Promise<RegistryRecordMismatch | undefined>` unions it over global and, when it was merged, the project scope, skipping a scope whose state cannot be read. Undefined when they agree. `lexicon pack list`, `pack search` and MCP `list_packs` report it as `registryMismatch`.
- `uninstallRegistryPack(ref, opts?, deps?)` removes only the scoped installed pack using its checksum-verified cached snapshot (or the exact pinned content recovered from its registry), preserves edited terms, and removes that scope's pin/cache. Its result sets `registryRemoved: true`, including with `dryRun`, even when no lexicon file remains or lists the pack (`files: []`); the CLI reports the registry record/cache removal rather than saying the pack is not installed. `dryRun` preserves the lexicon, registry and cache while returning the same removal plan. A failed metadata commit restores the previous dictionary, cache, state and project trust so a subsequent removal remains usable. `validateCommunityPack(pack)` reports schema and ordinary-word safety problems for authoring tools.

## src/core/import-sources.ts and import-preview.ts
- `IMPORT_SOURCE_IDS = ['contacts','calendar','github','email','slack']`; `IMPLEMENTED_IMPORT_SOURCES` contains contacts, calendar and github. `ImportSourceId` is the union. Email and Slack are descriptive unavailable entries; no OAuth or mailbox integration is implemented. Local harvesting adds no automatic upload or telemetry; previews return personal names/evidence to the requesting CLI/MCP client and may reach its model provider.
- `VocabImportSource { id; label; privacy; implemented; checkAvailable(deps?): Promise<SourceAvailability>; harvest(deps?): Promise<HarvestCandidate[]> }`. `SourceAvailability { available; reason? }`. `ImportSourceDeps` injects platform/home, filesystem probes/readers and an exec function; `resolveImportSourceDeps(deps?)` fills defaults. `listImportSources()` returns the source descriptors; `getImportSource(id)` rejects unknown ids.
- `harvestImportSources(ids, deps?, { limit? }): Promise<HarvestCandidate[]>` reads only the chosen implemented, available sources, merges candidates case-insensitively and limits the result (default50). `mergeCandidates(candidates)` preserves losing spellings as aliases and sorts by count/name. Contacts opens the local AddressBook SQLite source read-only with an empty startup file (`-init /dev/null`), so user `.sqliterc` commands are not executed; a file disappearing before its query is never recreated. Calendar uses local `.ics` files; GitHub uses existing `gh` auth and paginated JSON arrays slurped into one flattened array.
- `applyImportCandidates(candidates, StoreOptions & { scope?; leaveUntrusted? }): Promise<ApplyImportResult { added; merged; skipped; path? }>` writes only the supplied approved candidates through `addTerm` (with `leaveUntrusted` when given), stamps their import source and observes the normal project trust gate. It does not harvest.
- `importCandidatePreview(candidate)` returns canonical/category/source/aliases/evidence/count. `importCandidateId(candidate)` hashes that complete data with SHA256. `importPreviewDigest(sources, candidates, destination)` binds the selected source ids, destination and all candidate data. `selectImportCandidates(candidates, approvedIds)` preserves the approved subset and rejects an absent id.

## src/core/normalize.ts
- `normalize(text: string, lexicon: Lexicon, opts?: NormalizeOptions): NormalizeResult` applies findReplacements, preserves surrounding whitespace/punctuation, writes the canonical exactly as stored. `dryRun` returns `changed: false` with the candidate `replacements`; callers inspect `replacements.length`.
- `normalizeWithIndex(text: string, index: MatcherIndex, opts?: NormalizeOptions): NormalizeResult`: the same against a prebuilt index (`index.lexicon` is the lexicon matched); `normalize` is `normalizeWithIndex(text, buildIndex(lexicon), opts)`.
- `diffSummary(result: NormalizeResult): string` one line per replacement: `"Ashler" -> "Ashlr.AI" (alias, 1.00)`.
- `timeBudgetIncomplete(totalChars: number, budgetMs: number): NormalizeIncomplete` = `{ reasons: ['time'], totalChars, note }`, the note naming the budget: what a caller returns when its `shouldStop` budget ran out.

## src/core/demo.ts
- `DEMO_LEXICON` is the built-in miniature example used for the first-run demonstration and for argument-based normalization when the user's lexicon is empty. It is a literal because `examples/` is not shipped in the published package; it is announced as an example and is never written or merged into the user's lexicon.
- [Issue #217](https://github.com/ashlrai/lexicon/issues/217) leaves open whether to add Hetzner back to `DEMO_LEXICON` so the empty-lexicon demo corrects `head sner`. Hetzner was deliberately removed to keep the example small; the current terms already show alias, phonetic and fuzzy matching. Adding it would also require updating the relevant test and docs that paste first-run sentences.

## src/core/suggest.ts
- `compareText(a: string, b: string): number` (collate.ts): a fixed `en` collation, then code units, so an order never follows the user's locale. `computeStats`' `topTerms`, `suggestCanonicalFor`, `harvestRepo` (its candidates and the order it walks a directory), `suggestTerms`, `listTrusted` (by path), the registry index (`loadRegistryIndex`, by ref), `searchRegistryIndex` and `lexicon stats`' category and source lines sort or break ties with it, and the exporters' `compareCanonical` is it.
- `suggestAliases(canonical: string): string[]` generates likely STT misspellings (never a single STOPLIST word, which would be rewritten wherever it is said, except an acronym's pronounced form such as `sass`): split camel/dots ("Ashlr.AI" -> "Ashlr AI", "Ashler", "Ashlar"), vowel insertions for consonant clusters, drop dots/hyphens, common phoneme confusions. Deterministic, <= 8 results, never returns the canonical itself.

## src/core/learn.ts
- `Correction { heard; meant }` (`heard` may be empty when the sentence only names the intended spelling; the caller fills it from context), `LearnResult { term; created; file; aliasAdded; reversed?: true; conflict?; untrustedProject?: { path; trust: 'untrusted' | 'changed' } }` (`reversed`: `meant` is an alias of `term` and `heard` its canonical, so the lexicon already corrects the other way and nothing was written; `untrustedProject`: the `skippedProject` file, when a term in it other than the target also holds `heard`; only its path and trust state are returned, the file is never merged and none of its terms are reported), `LearnConflict { canonical; scope; as: 'alias' | 'canonical'; spelling }` (another term that also holds `heard`, as an alias or as its own canonical, so a transcript can still go to it; `spelling` is `heard` as that term writes it; that term is never edited), `CanonicalSuggestion { term; confidence }`, `MAX_CORRECTION_LENGTH = 60`.
- `parseCorrection(text, opts?: ParseCorrectionOptions { allowUnrelated?; lexicon?: CorrectionLexicon }): Correction | undefined` table-driven detection; `CORRECTION_EXAMPLES` holds one example per pattern: "it's X, not Y" (also spelled/spelt/written/called; the apostrophe may be `'` or U+2019 or missing ("its X not Y"), and "it is X not Y" spelled out is the same form, as in "not Y, it's X"), "I said X not Y", "I meant X not Y", `"X" not "Y"` (quoted only), "not Y, X", "replace Y with X", "Y -> X" (also `=>`, `→`), "that/this/it should be X", "Y should be X", "spelled X". Sides may be double-quoted, curly-quoted, backticked or bare (1 to 4 words); bare sides must look like a name for the `replace` and `Y should be` forms; a side made only of stoplist words is rejected, and so is a `meant` with no letter in it ("it's 2024 not 2023" corrects a fact, not a spelling). When both sides are bare they must also be spellings of one name, `spellingsAlike(heard, meant): boolean` (after the words both share are set aside, the squashed rest is equal, holds a digit, has edit `similarity` at least 0.5, has the same `phoneticKey`, or opens with the same letter with similarity at least 0.3), so "it's the API not the UI" is no correction; `allowUnrelated: true` returns it anyway (the CLI uses that to say why nothing was learned). A pair that check rejects still counts when, once the shared words are set aside, each side is one word written as a name (letters, not all capitals, at least 3) and `heard` is not on the stoplist, and either their loose phonetic keys (`phoneticKey` with B, F, V as P, X and J as S, TH as T, and H, W, Y dropped) are equal and `meant` is not on the stoplist ("it's Siobhan not Shivon"), or `opts.lexicon` (`CorrectionLexicon { terms: readonly Pick<Term, 'canonical' | 'aliases'>[] }`) holds `meant` as a canonical or alias, holds `heard` as no other term's spelling, and the loose keys open with the same letter and have `similarity` at least 0.5 ("it's Niamh not Neve" with Niamh a term). "it's the API not the UI", "it's Monday not Tuesday" and "it's Postgres not MySQL" stay refused either way. The prompt hook (`parseCorrections(text, lexicon?)`) and `lexicon learn` pass the loaded lexicon. A quoted side skips the check.
- `learnCorrection(c: Correction, opts?: StoreOptions & { scope?; leaveUntrusted? }): Promise<LearnResult)` (`leaveUntrusted` is passed to every `addTerm` it makes) adds `heard` as an alias of the term whose canonical or alias equals `meant` (written to that term's file unless `scope` is given). When `meant` is an alias of the term and `heard` is its canonical ("it's Ashlr not ashlr.ai" with Ashlr an alias of Ashlr.AI), nothing is written and the result is `created: false, aliasAdded: false, reversed: true` with the term's own file. When that file's term already holds `heard` (as an alias, or its canonical in another case or Unicode form) nothing is written and the result is `created: false, aliasAdded: false` with that file; under a `scope` the term does not live in, `heard` is written into that file's copy, never a copy without it. Otherwise it creates a term `meant` with `heard` plus `suggestAliases(meant)` and `source: 'learned'`. Throws on an empty side, sides over 60 characters (`<heard|meant|heard and meant> must be at most 60 characters; nothing was written. Pass only the misspelled name as heard and the name the user wants as meant, not the sentence around them.`, the words MCP `learn_correction` checks with first; `POST /learn` returns it as the 400), or identical spellings. No stoplist check: user intent wins.
- `spellingConflicts(lexicon: Lexicon, spellings: readonly string[], target: string): LearnConflict[]`: one `LearnConflict` per spelling that a term other than `target` also holds, in order (the check `learnCorrection` makes for `heard`, over a list).
- `conflictNote(conflicts: readonly LearnConflict[], audience?: ConflictNoteAudience): string` (`ConflictNoteAudience = 'cli' | 'mcp'`, default `'cli'`): the `note` that goes with `conflict`/`conflicts` in a JSON answer, one sentence per conflict, unsanitized. For an alias it names, if the user agrees, `conflictRemoveCommand(c)` (`'cli'`) or the `remove_term` call with `canonical`, `alias` and `scope` as JSON string literals (`'mcp'`); a canonical conflict says the spelling may still be kept as written and, if the user never means it as its own term, names `conflictRemoveTermCommand(c)` (`'cli'`) or the `remove_term` call with `canonical` and `scope` (`'mcp'`). The third argument `platform` (default `hostPlatform()`) picks the shell the `'cli'` commands are quoted for. CLI `learn --json` and `add --json`, MCP `add_term` (`'mcp'`) and serve `POST /learn` all use it. `conflictRemoveCommand(c: LearnConflict, show?: (s: string) => string, platform?: string): string` = `commandLine(['lexicon', 'remove', show(canonical), '--alias', show(spelling), '--<scope>'], platform)` (`show` default identity), a command that runs as printed in a POSIX shell or, on win32, PowerShell; `conflictRemoveTermCommand(c: Pick<LearnConflict, 'canonical' \| 'scope'>, platform?: string): string` = `commandLine(['lexicon', 'remove', canonical, '--<scope>'], platform)`. The extension popup's conflict sentences use both. `LearnConflict`, `ConflictNoteAudience`, `conflictNote`, `conflictRemoveCommand` and `conflictRemoveTermCommand` live in `src/core/conflict-note.ts` (no IO, no `node:` imports, so the extension bundles them) and are re-exported by `learn.ts`.
- `unchangedTermNote(canonical: string): string` (`conflict-note.ts`, re-exported by learn.ts): `Nothing was added: "<canonical>" already has every alias and field given.`, what `lexicon add` and MCP `add_term` say when `addTerm` reports `unchanged`.
- `learnUnchangedNote(heard: string, canonical: string, ctx?: LearnUnchangedContext { meant?; scope?; audience?; platform? }): string` (`conflict-note.ts`, re-exported by learn.ts): with `ctx.meant` not the canonical and `heard` the canonical, `reversedCorrectionNote(heard, meant, canonical, scope ?? 'global', audience ?? 'cli', platform)`: `Nothing was added: "<meant>" is an alias of "<canonical>", so it is already corrected to "<canonical>", the other way round from this correction ("<heard>" to "<meant>").` then, `'cli'`, `If you want "<meant>" written instead, make it the canonical: run lexicon edit[ --project] and rename the term "<canonical>" to "<meant>", keeping "<canonical>" as an alias.` or, `'mcp'`, the remove_term and add_term calls that do it; `lexicon learn` (text and `--json`), serve `POST /learn` and MCP `learn_correction` (its `summary`, `'mcp'`) pass `meant`. Otherwise the sentence for a correction that wrote nothing (neither `created` nor `aliasAdded`), `Nothing was added: "<heard>" was already an alias of "<canonical>".` or, for `heard` that is the canonical in another case, `Nothing was added: "<canonical>" is already the canonical spelling; "<heard>" is corrected to it.` (`heard` trimmed, whitespace collapsed; the "Nothing was added:" of `unchangedTermNote` with the reason MCP `learn_correction` gives). `lexicon learn` (text and `--json`) and serve `POST /learn` use it. Unsanitized.
- `suggestCanonicalFor(heard, lexicon): CanonicalSuggestion[]` top 3 existing terms by max(similarity, phonetic-key match ? 0.9 : 0), confidence >= 0.6, exact alias/canonical = 1.

## src/core/pending.ts
The review queue for corrections noticed automatically (the UserPromptSubmit hook's "it's X not Y"). The matcher never reads it, so a queued alias changes no transcript until it is accepted. CLI: `lexicon pending` (src/cli/cmd-pending.ts).
- `PENDING_FILE_NAME = 'pending-aliases.yaml'`, `MAX_PENDING = 200`. The file sits next to the global lexicon (`pendingPathFor(globalLexiconPath): string` = `<dirname>/pending-aliases.yaml`; `pendingPath(opts?: StoreOptions): string` resolves the global path first), written atomically with mode 0600 as a three-line comment header and `{ version: 1, pending: PendingAlias[] }`, oldest `firstSeen` first. Every read-modify-write holds `withLexiconLock` on the pending file (its own `.lock`, not the lexicon's).
- `PendingAlias { id; heard; meant; source; count; firstSeen; lastSeen }`: `id` is `pendingId(heard, meant): string`, the first 8 hex characters of the sha256 of both sides trimmed, whitespace collapsed and lowercased (joined by U+0000), so the same correction in another case is one entry; `source` where it was noticed (`prompt-hook` from the hook, `manual` by default); `count` how many times; `firstSeen` / `lastSeen` ISO timestamps.
- `PendingFileError extends Error`: the file is over 256 KiB, is not YAML, has a scalar root or a `pending` that is not a list, or holds an entry missing one of the `PendingAlias` fields or with one of the wrong type (a map without `pending` reads as empty). Such a file is never overwritten.
- `MAX_REJECTED = 500`, `RejectedAlias { id; rejectedAt }`: the file may also hold `rejected: RejectedAlias[]` (ids only, never the words), written only when non-empty, newest last, capped at `MAX_REJECTED`. A `rejected` that is not a list of id and rejectedAt strings is a `PendingFileError`. `readPendingFile(file): Promise<{ entries; rejected }>` returns both; an older lexicon that rewrites the file drops `rejected` (additive, no version bump).
- `readPending(file: string): Promise<PendingAlias[]>`: the entries; `[]` for a missing or empty file; throws `PendingFileError` as above and any other read error as is. `listPending(opts?: StoreOptions): Promise<{ file; entries: PendingAlias[] }>` is the same for `pendingPath(opts)`.
- `queueRefusal(heard: string, meant: string): string | undefined`: why a correction cannot be queued, `'empty'` (a side blank after trimming), `'too-long'` (a side over `MAX_CORRECTION_LENGTH`, 60) or `'same'` (identical after trimming and collapsing whitespace; case counts), else `undefined`. `alreadyKnown(lexicon: Lexicon, heard: string, meant: string): boolean`: a term whose canonical is `meant` already has `heard` as its canonical or an alias (case-insensitive).
- `QueueOptions extends StoreOptions { source?; now?: Date; lexicon?: Lexicon; lockTimeoutMs?: number }` (`lockTimeoutMs` bounds the wait for the pending file's lock, default `DEFAULT_LOCK_TIMEOUT_MS`; past it `FileLockError` is thrown and nothing is queued), `QueueResult { queued: PendingAlias[]; skipped: number; file }`, `queueCorrections(corrections: ReadonlyArray<{ heard; meant }>, opts?: QueueOptions): Promise<QueueResult>`: never touches the lexicon. A correction `queueRefusal` refuses, or that `alreadyKnown(opts.lexicon, ...)` finds, counts in `skipped`, and so does one whose `id` is in `rejected`; one already queued (same `id`) gets `count + 1` and `lastSeen = now`; a new one is stored with both sides trimmed and whitespace collapsed, `count: 1`. When anything was queued the list keeps the `MAX_PENDING` most recently seen and is written; otherwise the file is not written. Throws `PendingFileError` for a bad file, leaving it alone.
- `ResolveSelection { ids?: readonly string[]; all?: boolean }`: `all` picks every entry (and `ids` is ignored); otherwise each id is trimmed and lowercased, duplicates dropped, and an id with no entry goes to `unknown`.
- `rejectPending(sel: ResolveSelection, opts?: StoreOptions): Promise<{ rejected: PendingAlias[]; unknown: string[]; file }>`: removes the picked entries without learning them and appends their ids to `rejected` (`opts.now` sets `rejectedAt`), so `queueCorrections` skips them from then on; an explicit `lexicon learn` is unaffected. `acceptPending` keeps `rejected` as is. The file is written only when something was picked.
- `AcceptedAlias { entry: PendingAlias; result?: LearnResult; error?: string }`, `acceptPending(sel: ResolveSelection, opts?: StoreOptions, learn?: typeof learnCorrection): Promise<{ accepted: AcceptedAlias[]; unknown: string[]; file }>`: learns each picked entry through `learn` (default `learnCorrection`, as `lexicon learn` does, with `opts`) in order. It takes the lock of the global lexicon, then of the project lexicon when `resolvePaths(opts)` finds one, then the pending file's lock, and holds all of them from reading the queue to writing it back, so the lexicon lock stays outer (see `withFileLock`) and a concurrent accept, reject or queue never sees an entry half accepted. A learned entry carries `result` and is removed; one whose learn throws carries `error` (the message) and stays queued. The file is written only when at least one was learned.

## src/core/stats.ts
- `TOP_TERMS = 10`, `NEVER_HIT_CAP = 20`.
- `computeStats(loaded: LoadedLexicon): LexiconStats { termCount; aliasCount; totalHits; topTerms: { canonical; hits }[]; neverHit: string[] (the first `NEVER_HIT_CAP`, 20); neverHitCount (all of them); byCategory; bySource; files: { path; scope; terms }[] }`. `renderStats` prints `never hit (<neverHitCount>)` and `, and N more` after the list when the cap hid some.

## src/core/notes.ts
The notes the CLI, the MCP server and the local API add when a result needs a word, built once so their wording cannot drift. Each builder defaults to the CLI's next step; MCP passes its own in tool terms.
- `skippedProjectNote(loaded, consequence?, voice?: TrustNoteVoice { who; trust }): string | undefined`: `An untrusted project .lexicon.yaml exists and was not loaded; review it and run \`lexicon trust\` to enable it.` (or `The project .lexicon.yaml changed since it was trusted and was not loaded; review it and run \`lexicon trust\` again.`), then `consequence`; undefined when no project file was skipped. MCP `skippedProjectFields` passes `{ who: 'the user can ', trust: cliCommand('trust') }`; CLI `skippedProjectJson` and serve `lexiconNote` / `statsNote` / `exportNote` use the default.
- `noTermsYet(opts?: { so?; lower? }): string`: the opening every empty-lexicon note shares, `The lexicon has no terms yet` (`the ...` with `lower`), then `, so <so>`. `onlyUntrustedTerms(fate): string`: `the only terms are in the untrusted project lexicon, which <fate>`. `FIRST_TERM_STEP` (`run \`lexicon setup\`, or add one with: lexicon learn <heard> <meant>`) and `TRUST_STEP` (`review it and run \`lexicon trust\``) are the CLI steps, without a closing period. The clipboard daemon's `emptyLexiconHint`, `lexicon remove`'s empty-lexicon step and the MCP `voice-context` / `lexicon://me` / `lexicon://json` empty note are built from them; no other source file spells these openings (`tests/notes.test.ts`).
- `emptyNormalizeHint(loaded: Pick<LoadedLexicon, 'skippedProject'>): string`: `POST /normalize`'s `hint` (also exported from `src/serve/server.ts`).
- `emptyLexiconNote(step?): string`: `The lexicon has no terms yet; <step>` (`lexicon list`, MCP `list_terms`, `GET /lexicon`).
- `pageTermList(items, request?: TermPageRequest { offset?; limit? }): { items; page?: TermPage { offset; limit?; returned; nextOffset? } }`: one page of a matched term list (`lexicon list --offset/--limit`, MCP `list_terms`); `page` is undefined when neither was given, so an unpaged list keeps its shape, and `nextOffset` is set only while items remain past the page. `offsetPastEndNote(offset, matched): string`: `No term at offset <offset>: only <matched> terms matched, at offsets 0 to <matched - 1>.` (`1 term matched, at offset 0` for one).
- `noTermMatchesNote({ filter, filters, total, lister?: TermLister { name; verb } }): string`: `No term matches <filter>; the lexicon has <total> term(s) in all, which <lister.name> without it|them <lister.verb>.` (`lexicon list --json` / `--full` with the default `{ name: 'lexicon list', verb: 'shows' }`, MCP `list_terms` with `{ name: 'list_terms', verb: 'returns' }`).
- `emptyStatsNote(loaded, step?): string | undefined`: `The lexicon has no terms yet, so nothing is corrected or counted; <step>`, undefined when there are terms or a project file was skipped (`lexicon stats`, MCP `lexicon_stats`, `GET /stats`).
- `emptyExportReason(loaded, format, opts?: ExportOptions): EmptyExportReason | undefined` (`'no-terms' | 'only-untrusted' | 'no-match' | 'no-rows'`): the lexicon has no terms; only a skipped project file has them; none matched `opts.categories`; or an alias-row format (`EXPORT_ALIAS_ROW_FORMATS`) whose exported terms all lack an alias, also when `opts.limit` (which counts only terms with one there) left none. `emptyExportNote(reason, { format, filter, steps?: Partial<EmptyExportSteps> }): string` is its sentence, `filter` naming the category filter as the caller took it (`--category brand`, `?category=brand`, `categories ["brand"]`). `lexicon export`, `GET /export/:format` and MCP `export_lexicon` (which words `only-untrusted` as `no-terms` and adds the skipped file's note) all decide with `emptyExportReason`.

## src/core/suggestTerms.ts
- `SuggestionKind = 'alias' | 'term' | 'never' | 'stale'`; `TermSuggestion { kind; canonical; alias?; reason; confidence (0..1); evidence: string[] (<= 5, each through sanitizeForDisplay and <= 120 chars); count; aliases?: string[] (for 'term': what to add with it); category? (for a harvest 'term'); scope?: TermScope; path? (the file applying it writes: the file the merged term came from for 'alias' / 'never' / 'stale', `project` only when `loaded.project` is merged, else the global file; always set by `suggestTerms`) }`; `VoiceHistoryEntry { at; raw; output; model?; ms? }` (structurally accepts `voice/history.ts` `HistoryEntry`); `SuggestInput { loaded; history?; cwd?; limit? (default 20); now? (epoch ms for the stale age, tests) }`.
- `SUGGEST_DEFAULT_LIMIT = 20`, `SUGGEST_MAX_LIMIT = 500` (the largest `lexicon suggest --limit` and MCP `suggest_terms` `limit`), `STALE_AFTER_DAYS = 30`, `AUTO_APPLY_CONFIDENCE = 0.8` (what `lexicon suggest --yes` and any unattended caller apply).
- `voiceHistoryPath(globalPath)` = `<dirname(globalPath)>/voice/history.jsonl`; `loadVoiceHistory(globalPath?): Promise<VoiceHistoryEntry[]>` (default path from `resolvePaths()`, so `$LEXICON_PATH` applies) never throws: missing file, unreadable file, a path that is not a regular file (checked before a non-blocking open, so a FIFO cannot block it) and malformed lines yield nothing; reads at most the last `VOICE_HISTORY_READ_MAX_BYTES` (16 MiB, exported; the one bound for the voice history, which `src/voice/history.ts` reads with as `HISTORY_READ_MAX_BYTES`, so a newest entry the writer keeps on its own past the 4 MiB cap is seen here too) through `readRegularFileTail` (src/util/bounded-read.ts, shared with the writer) and drops the line that bound cuts; keeps the newest 1000 entries.
- `suggestTerms(input): Promise<TermSuggestion[]>`; `history` defaults to `loadVoiceHistory(loaded.global.path)`. Identical `{ raw, output }` lines collapse into one with a count. Signals:
  - 'alias': (a) a 1..3 token window of `raw` (some non-stoplist token, no function word or bare number at an edge, never a lone stoplist word) that is still in `output`, is near an existing alias/canonical (edit similarity >= 0.7, or a shared double-metaphone key plus similarity >= 0.6/0.5/0 for keys of 3/4/5+ consonants), recurs >= 2 times: confidence `0.3 + 0.45 * score + 0.05 * min(count, 4)`; (b) a window the current matcher corrects by phonetic/fuzzy match >= 3 times: reason `the matcher would guess this as <canonical> (N times in history); make it an exact alias`, confidence `max(0.8, mean matcher confidence)` capped at 0.95; (c) a rewrite found by a token diff of `raw` vs `output` that reads as a canonical but that the current matcher no longer reproduces (and is not an ordinary word): confidence <= 0.75.
  - 'term': runs of capitalized / CamelCase tokens in `output` (not a lone word at a sentence start, not stoplist / HARVEST_STOPLIST, not a lexicon spelling nor a word of a multi-word canonical, not near an existing term, >= 4 letters) recurring >= 3 times: confidence `0.35 + 0.1 * min(count, 5)` capped at 0.9, `aliases = suggestAliases(canonical)`. With `cwd`, `harvestRepo(cwd, { limit: 10, minCount: 5 })` candidates not in the lexicon are added with the harvester's aliases and category, reason `seen N times in <dir> (harvest, <source>)`, confidence capped at 0.7 (below AUTO_APPLY_CONFIDENCE: repo identifiers are review material).
  - 'never': a rewrite in the diff whose original is a single ordinary word (lowercase, in STOPLIST or a function word) and that was not an explicit alias: `alias` is the word, reason starts `ordinary word rewritten by guess`, confidence 0.7 when the current matcher still guesses it, else `0.5 + 0.05 * min(count, 3)` (<= 0.65). Skipped when the term's `never` already lists it.
  - 'stale': `hits` 0/undefined, `createdAt` at least 30 days before `now`, and no spelling (canonical or alias, word by word) occurs in the history: reason `never matched in N days`, confidence 0.3, count 0. Terms without `createdAt` are never stale.
  - Dedupe key `(kind, canonical, alias)` case-insensitive: counts take the max (signals (a) and (b) may cite the same lines), confidence and reason follow the stronger one, evidence unions. Rank by `confidence * log(1 + max(count, 1))` descending, then confidence, then canonical; cap at `limit`.
  - Budget: under 100 ms for 1000 history lines and 200 terms (measured in tests/suggest-terms.test.ts; about 10 ms on an M-series Mac). The matcher runs only on lines that hold a near window or differ from their output; near-miss lookups are cached per window text.

## src/core/harvest.ts
- `harvestRepo(root: string, opts?: HarvestOptions): Promise<HarvestCandidate[]>` is `harvestReport(root, opts).candidates`. `harvestReport(root, opts?): Promise<HarvestReport { candidates; truncated; filesScanned; belowMinCount; symbolsOnly }>`: `truncated` when the scan stopped at `HARVEST_MAX_FILES = 5000` files, `filesScanned` the files read, `belowMinCount` and `symbolsOnly` the names left out for `minCount` or for appearing only as a source symbol (without `symbols`). `harvestTruncatedNote(root): string` = `The scan stopped after the first 5000 files under <root>, so names in the rest of it were not seen; harvest a subdirectory to scan that part in full.` (unsanitized), which `lexicon harvest` and MCP `harvest_repo` give for `truncated`. `HARVEST_MAX_LIMIT = 1000` is the largest `limit` `lexicon harvest --limit` and MCP `harvest_repo` take (the core does not clamp a direct call).
  - package/module names (package.json name + deps, pyproject, Cargo.toml, go.mod)
  - PascalCase identifiers >= 2 humps from source files (ts/js/py/go/rs/java/rb/swift/kt), exported classes/types preferred
  - git log author names (`git log --format=%an`, 5s timeout), deduped; below the top of a work tree (`git rev-parse --show-prefix` not empty) only commits that touch `root` (`-- .`), so a subdirectory, or a directory git does not track, does not get the whole enclosing repo's authors
  - project dir name
  - README proper nouns (capitalised tokens; a sentence-initial one counts only for a word that also occurs mid-sentence)
  - filters `HARVEST_STOPLIST` (README-word filter, distinct from matcher `STOPLIST` to avoid an `export *` collision in `core/index.ts`) and generic identifiers (String, Error, Component, ...).
  - **external evidence**: a name seen only as a source identifier is not proposed (source still counts towards one that is named in a manifest, a README sentence, the git log, the directory name or as a dot-TLD brand). `symbols: true` proposes the uncorroborated ones too.
  - ranks by count, returns candidates with `suggestedAliases` from suggest.ts, minus the ones that would invent a word boundary: an `identifier` gets none, and a canonical with no separator of its own (`LexiconBar`) keeps only the single-word suggestions, while one that has a separator (`Ashlr.AI`, `pasture-notes`) keeps them all.
  - never reads node_modules/dist/.git/vendor/build (plus `opts.ignore`); caps file size 512 KB; caps files scanned 5000. In a git work tree it reads only the files `git ls-files --cached --others --exclude-standard` lists (so nothing a `.gitignore`, `.git/info/exclude` or the global excludes leave out), regular files only, under the same filters; outside one, when git is missing or slow, or when git lists nothing, it walks the directory.

## src/core/importers/index.ts
Pure, no IO.
- `ImportFormat = 'wispr' | 'superwhisper' | 'macos' | 'csv' | 'espanso' | 'text' | 'json' | 'auto'`, `ConcreteImportFormat = Exclude<ImportFormat, 'auto'>`.
- `IMPORT_FORMATS: readonly ImportFormat[]`, `IMPORT_FORMAT_INFO: Record<ImportFormat, { description }>`, `isImportFormat(value): value is ImportFormat`.
- `importLexicon(content: string, format: ImportFormat, opts?: ImportOptions { source?: TermSource; filename? }): ImportResult { terms: Term[]; format: ConcreteImportFormat; skipped: { line: number; reason: string }[] }` (`line` is 1-based; an entry index for JSON inputs). Rows are merged by `mergeRows(rows, source?)`: one term per canonical (case-insensitive, first spelling wins), aliases deduped case-insensitively, an alias equal to the canonical dropped, `source` defaults to `'import'`, blank canonicals reported in `skipped`. Throws a readable Error on unparseable input or an unknown format, and on any term carrying U+FFFD (the backstop for callers that pass a string rather than bytes, so a lossy decode upstream cannot be stored as a spelling).
- `detectImportFormat(content, filename?): ConcreteImportFormat`: plist header -> macos; JSON array of `{original,replacement}` (or `{replacements}`) -> superwhisper; JSON/YAML with `terms` -> json; YAML `matches:` -> espanso; `word,replacement` header -> wispr; header containing `canonical` -> csv; then the extension (`.plist` -> macos, `.csv` -> wispr, `.yml`/`.yaml` -> espanso); else text. JSON of an unknown shape throws.
- One file per format, each returning `RawImport { rows: ImportRow[]; skipped: ImportSkip[] }` (`importers/shared.ts`): `parseWisprImport`, `parseSuperwhisperImport`, `parseMacosImport` (regex plist scanner, entities decoded), `parseCsvImport` (`canonical,alias,category,phonetic`, header-ordered, unknown category dropped), `parseEspansoImport` (skips `vars`, regex and multi-line matches; strips a leading `:`/`;` trigger prefix), `parseTextImport` (`Canonical`, `Canonical: a, b`, `Canonical = a | b`, `#` comments, `\#` escapes a leading `#`; inside a line `\=`, `\|`, `\,`, `\:` and `\\` read as the character and never separate, any other backslash is kept; the colon of `scheme://` never separates), `parseJsonImport` (parseLexicon; drops scope/createdAt/hits).
- `importers/csv-parse.ts`: `parseCsv(content): CsvRecord { line; fields }[]` (RFC 4180 quoting, CRLF, BOM), shared by wispr and csv.
- `importers/encoding.ts`, the bytes-to-text step `lexicon import` runs before any parser:
  - `detectImportEncoding(bytes: Uint8Array): ImportEncoding | UnsupportedImportEncoding`. `ImportEncoding = 'utf-8' | 'utf-16le' | 'utf-16be'`; `UnsupportedImportEncoding = 'utf-32le' | 'utf-32be' | 'not-utf-8' | 'not-text'`. BOMs first (UTF-32 before UTF-16, since UTF-32LE opens with the UTF-16LE mark), then UTF-16 without a BOM from the NUL in every other byte, then a NUL anywhere (`not-text`), then a UTF-8 round-trip check.
  - `decodeImportBytes(bytes, source?: ImportSource { path?; label? }): DecodedImport { text; encoding }`. UTF-8 comes back byte-identical, BOM included (the parsers strip it); UTF-16 is decoded either endianness and its BOM removed; a detected UTF-16 input with an incomplete final code unit (odd byte length) is refused by input name before parsing, rather than silently dropping its last byte. Anything else throws `importEncodingError(source, encoding)`, which names the *input* file and gives the `iconv` / PowerShell command that converts it. An 8-bit encoding is never guessed: Latin-1, Windows-1252 and KOI8-R are the same bytes, and guessing would store a wrong spelling silently.
  - `REPLACEMENT_CHAR` is `'�'`.

## src/core/exporters/index.ts
- `exportLexicon(lexicon: Lexicon, format: ExportFormat, opts?: ExportOptions): string`: `exportLexiconWithNotes(...).text`.
- `exportLexiconWithNotes(lexicon, format, opts?): ExportResult { text: string; notes: string[]; rows?: number; names?: number }`: the export, plus one sentence per kind of entry the format left out to stay within its target's limits, each naming what was dropped (`left out ...`; at most 10 names quoted, then `and N more`, `shared.ts` `quotedNames`). `lexicon export` prints each as `lexicon: <format> export <note>` on stderr, or under `--json` as `dropped: string[]` (only when not empty); MCP `export_lexicon` adds `The <format> export <note>.` to its note block; `GET /export/:format` says only that entries were left out, in `X-Lexicon-Note` (`exportNote(loaded, format, opts, dropped?)`), since a header is ASCII. `Exporter = (lexicon, opts, sink?: ExportSink { notes: string[]; rows?: number; names?: number }) => string` is the type of each per-format function; an alias-row format sets `sink.rows` to the rows it wrote, returned as `ExportResult.rows`, which `lexicon export` reports as `rows` (fewer than the aliases when a format left some out). `whisper-prompt` and `openai` set `sink.names` to the names the line holds after the default cap and the prompt-token window, returned as `ExportResult.names`, which `lexicon export` reports as `terms` (`--json`, and `wrote <format> export (N terms)` after `--out`).
- `exportClaudeMdWithin(lexicon: Lexicon, maxChars: number, more: (omitted: number) => string): CappedClaudeMd { text; omitted }` (`exporters/claudeMd.ts`): the `claude-md` export in at most `maxChars` characters, for text read into model context whole. The whole export when it fits (`omitted: 0`); otherwise rows are taken in `sortByImportance` order (hits, then brand/person/product, then canonical A to Z, as `limit` keeps them), a row that does not fit what is left is skipped while later rows still count, the kept rows are written in the export's own order, then the line `more(omitted)` and the usual footer. Header and footer always survive; when they alone exceed `maxChars` the text is cut there, ending in `...` and a newline within `maxChars`, never between the halves of a surrogate pair.
- Export text: the alias-row formats (`EXPORT_ALIAS_ROW_FORMATS`) write each term's aliases and canonical in NFC, one row per distinct NFC alias (`exporters/shared.ts` `aliasPairs`), and the boost and prompt formats (`assemblyai`, `speechmatics`, `azure`, `google`, `deepgram`, `whisper-prompt`, `openai`) each canonical once in NFC (`canonicalList`); `json`, `text`, `markdown` and `claude-md` keep the stored text. Every name sort uses `compareCanonical` (a fixed `en` collation, then code units), never the process locale.
- `selectExportTerms(lexicon: Lexicon, opts?: ExportOptions, format?: ExportFormat): Term[]`: the terms `exportLexicon(lexicon, format, opts)` hands the exporter, after the `categories` filter (a term without a category counts as `other`) and the `limit` cap (`whisper-prompt` may cap further). The cap keeps up to `limit` terms ordered by `hits` (highest first), then brand, person and product terms before the rest, then canonical A to Z (`sortByImportance`), in the lexicon's own order afterwards. `EXPORT_MAX_LIMIT = 10000` (a global and a project file of 5000 terms each) is the largest `limit` `lexicon export --limit`, MCP `export_lexicon` and `GET /export/:format?limit=` take; `selectExportTerms` itself does not clamp. For a format in `EXPORT_ALIAS_ROW_FORMATS: readonly ExportFormat[]` (`csv`, `wispr`, `superwhisper`, `macos`, `espanso`, the formats that write one row per alias) only terms with a non-blank alias are counted, so `limit: 1` writes a row whenever any selected term has an alias; without `format` every term counts. `lexicon export --out` counts them for its `wrote ... (N terms)` line and MCP `export_lexicon` uses it to say an export is empty.
- `EXPORT_FORMATS: readonly ExportFormat[]` (15; declared in `types.ts`, re-exported here), `EXPORT_FORMAT_INFO: Record<ExportFormat, { description; ext }>`, `isExportFormat(value)`.
- `EXPORT_FORMAT_ALIASES: Readonly<Record<string, ExportFormat>>` (`wispr-flow`, `wisprflow`, `wispr-flow-csv` -> `wispr`) and `resolveExportFormat(value): ExportFormat | undefined` (trimmed, case-insensitive, aliases included). Every place that parses a format from text uses it: the CLI, `GET /export/:format` and MCP `export_lexicon`; `EXPORT_FORMATS` stays the canonical list.
- `suggestExportFormat(value): ExportFormat | undefined` the canonical format a mistyped name most likely meant (formats and alias keys, at most 2 edits and fewer than half the typed length, a tie suggests nothing). Only the human `lexicon export` error prints it (`Did you mean <format>? Run: lexicon export <format>`); the `--json` error, the HTTP 404 and the MCP error are unchanged.
- One file per format, each `export function export<Format>(lexicon, opts, sink?): string` (an `Exporter`), ordered by `sortByImportance()` from `shared.ts`: `wispr` (CSV word,replacement, LF, no BOM; a pair with a cell a spreadsheet would run as a formula, `shared.ts` `isFormulaCell`: starting with `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, a tab or a CR, is left out with a note, since Wispr Flow would keep a `'` escape as part of the word), `superwhisper` (replacements JSON), `whisper-prompt` (one line of names joined with `, `, whitespace in each name collapsed and each name once (a repeat is skipped with a note), a name holding a comma or starting with `"` written in double quotes with inner quotes doubled (`shared.ts` `listItem(value)`, the CSV style of `joinAliasList`; the format has no separator rule of its own) and counted quoted toward the window, at most `WHISPER_PROMPT_DEFAULT_LIMIT` 100 terms (or `opts.limit`), most important first, and only while the line's `estimateWhisperTokens(text): number` stays within `WHISPER_PROMPT_TOKEN_BUDGET` 223, the prompt tokens Whisper reads (openai-whisper keeps `448 / 2 - 1`); names past the default cap of 100 (no `opts.limit`) get their own note, `left out N names past the 100 a prompt holds by default (the most used kept): ...`, so every name left out is counted; the estimate is offline and errs high: a run of ASCII letters `ceil(n / 2)`, each digit, ASCII punctuation mark or lone space 1, each non-ASCII character its UTF-8 byte count; the rest are left out with a note), `macos` (Text Replacement plist; the C0 control characters XML 1.0 cannot hold (U+0000 to U+001F but tab, LF and CR) are removed from both strings first, with a note, and a pair left with an empty shortcut or phrase is not written; a shortcut two terms share, compared lowercased, is written once as for espanso; a pair whose alias or canonical holds U+FFFE, U+FFFF or an unpaired surrogate, which XML 1.0 cannot hold even as a character reference and the schema lets through, is left out so the rest of the plist stays readable, with a note naming it), `claude-md` (markdown table under `CLAUDE_MD_HEADING = '## Voice lexicon'`; `|` (as `\|`) and every kind of line break escaped in cells, and a `|` right after a backslash written as U+200B then `\|`, since GFM reads `\\|` as an escaped backslash and a cell boundary while the markdown importer reads only `\|`; the schema strips the U+200B on the way back in; the note cell is `category notes (phonetic)`, and a note ending in `)` with no phonetic gets an empty `()` after it so the markdown importer does not read the note's own parenthesis as the phonetic), `csv` (canonical,alias,category,phonetic, LF, no BOM; a formula cell is written `"'<value>"` by `csvTextField`, the spreadsheet text escape, with a note counting them; `lexicon import` keeps the `'`), `json` (raw lexicon, each term without the merged-only `scope` and `hits`; MCP `lexicon://json` writes `loaded.merged` itself and keeps them), `deepgram` (`{ keyterm, keywords }`: `keyterm` for Nova-3, the canonicals as written with whitespace collapsed, each once (one that then equals a keyterm already listed is skipped with a note), at most `DEEPGRAM_KEYTERM_MAX` 100 distinct, a conservative cap, the rest named in a note; `keywords` for Nova-2 and older, `keyword:boost`, a `:` in a canonical becomes a space and whitespace collapses, since the format has no escape for it, a canonical left empty is skipped with a note, and one that then equals a keyword already listed is skipped with a note), `espanso` (match YAML, every string double-quoted (`defaultStringType: 'QUOTE_DOUBLE'`) so no trigger or name reads as a YAML 1.1 or 1.2 boolean, null or number; a trigger two terms share is written once, for the pair `shared.ts` `uniqueTriggers(pairs, key?)` keeps (the term `sortByImportance` ranks first), the rest named in a note by `sharedTriggerNote`; in `replace` each run of two or more `{` is written as `\{\{` pairs, the odd one first, so espanso's variable syntax `{{name}}` is typed as written; a pair whose canonical espanso would still not type as written, one holding a literal `\{\{` or `\}\}`, is left out, named in a leading `# left out "<alias>" -> "<canonical>": espanso would ...` comment and in a note), `assemblyai` (`{ word_boost, boost_param: "high" }`, at most `ASSEMBLYAI_WORD_BOOST_MAX` 1000 entries, the API's limit, the most important kept; a canonical of more than `ASSEMBLYAI_PHRASE_MAX_WORDS` 6 whitespace-separated words is left out, with a note), `speechmatics` (`{ additional_vocab: [{ content, sounds_like? }] }`, content the canonical with whitespace collapsed, `sounds_like` its distinct aliases other than the canonical and omitted when there are none, at most `SPEECHMATICS_VOCAB_MAX` 1000 entries, the most important kept, the rest named in a note), `azure` (`{ phraseList }`, at most `AZURE_PHRASES_MAX` 500, the most important kept), `google` (`{ adaptation: { phraseSets: [{ phrases: [{ value, boost }] }] } }`, boost 20 for brand/person/product else 10; a phrase over `GOOGLE_PHRASE_MAX_CHARS` 100 code points is left out and the set holds at most `GOOGLE_PHRASES_MAX` 1000, the most important; both vendor limits are as last read, not confirmed offline, and each drop is a note), `openai` (delegates to whisper-prompt), `text` (`Canonical: alias1, alias2`, sorted, round-trips exactly with the text importer: a backslash escapes an `=` at the start of a field or after whitespace, a `|` in an `=`-form alias, a `,` in a `:`-form alias, and a backslash before `\ | , : =` or at the end of a field), `markdown` (`- **Canonical** (category): aliases`).
- The MCP server's zod enums live in `src/mcp/shared.ts` (`INSTALL_CLIENT_VALUES`, `INSTALL_SCOPES`, `IMPORT_FORMAT_VALUES`, `SUGGESTION_KINDS`), each `as const satisfies readonly <T>[]` so it fails to compile when the union grows. `export_lexicon` takes its format from an enum of `EXPORT_FORMATS` plus the `EXPORT_FORMAT_ALIASES` keys and resolves it with `resolveExportFormat`.

## src/core/shell.ts
Quoting for every command lexicon prints for a person to copy and run (`to apply, run: ...`, `then run: ...`, the whisper rerun): one source, so all of them are quoted the same way on a platform.
- `posixShellWord(s): string`: bare when it matches `[\w@%+=:,./-]+`, else single-quoted with `'` written as `'\''`.
- `powerShellWord(s): string`: bare when it is plainly safe for PowerShell (word characters and `+ = : . / \ -`, plus `@` and `~` after the first character, so `C:\Users\RUNNER~1\...` stays bare), else single-quoted with `'` and U+2018 to U+201B doubled. A comma is always quoted (bare, PowerShell passes `a,b` as two arguments), and so is a leading `@` or `~`.
- `hostPlatform(): string`: `process.platform`, or `'linux'` where there is no `process` (the browser extension, which bundles this module through conflict-note.ts), so a command printed there is quoted for a POSIX shell.
- `shellWord(s, platform: string = hostPlatform())`: `powerShellWord` on win32, else `posixShellWord`.
- `commandLine(words, platform: string = hostPlatform())`: the words joined, each through `shellWord`; on win32 a first word that had to be quoted gets PowerShell's call operator `& ` in front.

## src/core/index.ts
Re-exports everything above: `types`, `schema`, `store`, `trust`, `matcher`, `normalize`, `harvest`, `exporters`, `importers`, `suggest`, `learn`, `stats`, `suggestTerms`, `packs`, `registry`, `shell`, `import-sources`, `import-preview`. The only cross-module import path inside core; also the package entry point (`main` / `exports["."]`). Note that `export * from './packs.js'` also surfaces `packs.ts`'s own `findPackageRoot`, which is a *different* function from the one in `src/util/package.ts` (see below).

## src/daemon/clipboard-backends.ts
- `ClipboardBackend { name; description?; read(): Promise<string>; write(text): Promise<void> }`. `read` resolves `''` for an empty or non-text clipboard (the tool exits non-zero or prints nothing); a missing binary (`ENOENT`) or a display/session error still rejects, and so does a `ClipboardTooLargeError`.
- `ClipboardExec = (cmd, args, stdin?, opts?: ClipboardExecOptions { env? }) => Promise<string>`; `defaultClipboardExec(cmd, args, stdin?, opts: number | { timeoutMs?, env? } = {})` (a number is the timeout, default `CLIPBOARD_EXEC_TIMEOUT_MS`; `env` the child's whole environment) spawns without a shell (writes ignore stdout and resolve on exit so `xclip -i`'s forked child cannot hold the pipe; a read or a write keeps at most `MAX_CLIPBOARD_WRITE_STDERR_BYTES` (4096) of stderr (a write waiting up to 200 ms after a non-zero exit for the rest) and puts it in its `ExecError`, then lets the pipe go). `ExecError { cmd, exitCode, stderr }`. `CLIPBOARD_EXEC_TIMEOUT_MS = 5000`: a tool still running then (`xclip -o` waiting on a selection owner that does not answer) is killed and the call rejects with `ClipboardTimeoutError { cmd, timeoutMs }` (`isClipboardTimeout(err)`, by name); a backend read reports it rather than reading it as an empty clipboard, so the watcher says so once and keeps polling.
- `MAX_CLIPBOARD_READ_BYTES = 65536`: a read whose stdout passes it is killed and rejects with `ClipboardTooLargeError { cmd, limit }` (name `'ClipboardTooLargeError'`; `isClipboardTooLarge(err)` tests by name, so an injected exec may throw its own). 64 KiB is at least 3 UTF-8 bytes per unit of `MAX_CLIPBOARD_CHARS`, so a clipboard past it is too large whatever it holds; the poll no longer buffers up to 16 MB every 250 ms, and a clipboard over 16 MB is no longer reported as empty.
- `CLIPBOARD_BACKEND_NAMES`, `ClipboardBackendName = 'pbcopy' | 'wl' | 'xclip' | 'xsel' | 'powershell'`, `isClipboardBackendName(v)`, `createClipboardBackend(name, exec?, shell: 'powershell' | 'pwsh' = 'powershell', env = process.env)`, `pbcopyBackend(exec?, env = process.env)` (pbpaste and pbcopy run with `utf8LocaleEnv(env)`: `env` plus `LANG` and `LC_CTYPE` `en_US.UTF-8` when it sets none of `LC_ALL`, `LC_CTYPE`, `LANG`, else `env` unchanged, so non-ASCII text survives a caller with no locale; `detectClipboardBackend` and the daemon pass their `env`), `powershellShell(which): Promise<'powershell' | 'pwsh'>` (what a forced `--backend powershell` runs: `powershell` when on PATH, else `pwsh`; `checkClipboardBackend` accepts either and names both when neither is there), and the factories `pbcopyBackend`, `wlBackend`, `xclipBackend`, `xselBackend`, `powershellBackend`: `pbpaste`/`pbcopy`; `wl-paste --no-newline --type text`/`wl-copy --type text/plain` (`--type text`: without it wl-paste hands over an image's bytes when no text type is offered; `--type text/plain`: without it wl-copy guesses a type from the bytes); `xclip -selection clipboard -o`/`-i`; `xsel --clipboard --output`/`--input`; `powershell -NoProfile -NonInteractive -Command` with `Get-Clipboard -Raw` written via `[Console]::Out.Write` (no trailing newline, UTF-8 without a byte order mark, and a leading U+FEFF dropped from the read) and `Set-Clipboard -Value` of `[Console]::In.ReadToEnd()` from stdin. The PowerShell backend normalizes CRLF to `\n` on read and restores CRLF on the next write when every line break the read had was CRLF; text with both CRLF and LF is passed through unchanged both ways.
- `detectClipboardBackend(platform?, env?, which?, exec?)`: darwin -> pbcopy (both binaries on PATH); linux/BSD -> wl when `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` is set and `wl-paste` + `wl-copy` exist, else xclip, else xsel, else throws with `LINUX_INSTALL_HINT` (`sudo apt install wl-clipboard` / `xclip`); win32 -> powershell (falls back to `pwsh`); anything else throws. `which` defaults to `findOnPath`.
- `findOnPath` is imported from `src/util/which.ts` and is not re-exported here.

## src/daemon/clipboard.ts
Clipboard watcher for macOS, Linux and Windows using the backends above. `ClipboardCommonOptions { dryRun?; quiet?; cwd?; read?; write?; backend?; platform?; env?; exec?; out?; err? }`; `resolveClipboard(opts)` prefers injected `read`/`write` over `backend`/detection (a single injected function is completed from the detected backend).
- `MAX_CLIPBOARD_CHARS = 20000`, `LEXICON_RELOAD_MS = 5000`, `PASTE_APPLESCRIPT`.
- `lexiconFilesStamp(cwd: string): string`: size, modification time and inode of the global lexicon, the project lexicon `resolvePaths({ cwd })` finds and `trust.json` (`-` for a missing file), joined; synchronous `stat` calls only. The loop reloads when it changes.
- `runClipboardDaemon(opts: ClipboardDaemonOptions { intervalMs?; signal? }): Promise<ClipboardDaemonStop>` (loop; `ClipboardDaemonStop = 'SIGINT' | 'SIGTERM' | 'abort'`, what stopped it): poll every 250ms (configurable; an `intervalMs` under `MIN_DAEMON_INTERVAL_MS` (20) is raised to it and `clipboard daemon: --interval <n> is below the 20 ms minimum; polling every 20 ms` goes to `err`), if text changed since last seen and normalize changes it, write back, print diff to stdout. Loop guard: the last value written and the input it came from are skipped (so our own output is never rewritten and a clipboard tool that puts the original back is not fought) until a different value is copied, which clears both, so the same dictated text copied again later is corrected again. A write that fails is retried on the next poll (reported once, like a failing read, then `working again`). A too-large clipboard (either form) is skipped without an error line. Lexicon re-read when `lexiconFilesStamp(cwd)` changed (checked before correcting a new clipboard text), and at least every 5s. Graceful SIGINT/SIGTERM/`signal`: with a poll in flight the loop resolves once it settles (saying so on `err` unless `quiet`), and a second SIGINT or SIGTERM meanwhile calls `process.exit(130)` or `(143)`; no listener is left behind. `daemonStopExitCode(stop)` maps the graceful stop to the same statuses (130 for SIGINT, 143 for SIGTERM, 0 for `signal`), so the exit status follows the signal whether or not a poll was in flight. Unless `quiet`, a lexicon with no terms is said once at start on `err`: `[<time>] clipboard daemon: nothing will be corrected: <emptyLexiconHint>`.
- `runClipboardOnce(opts: ClipboardOnceOptions { paste?; sendPaste? }): Promise<ClipboardOnceResult { changed; written; corrections; pasted }>` (`--once`): `paste` with `dryRun` throws `DRY_RUN_PASTE_ERROR` (`--paste cannot be combined with --dry-run: ...`) before the clipboard is read, since the paste would send the uncorrected text; read once, normalize, write back if changed (unless `dryRun`), print `N correction(s)` + diff or `no changes`, `no changes (<emptyLexiconHint>)` when the lexicon has no terms (`quiet` silences). With `paste: true` on darwin runs `osascript -e 'tell application "System Events" to keystroke "v" using command down'` (injectable `sendPaste`) whether or not the text changed (but not when the clipboard was left as it was: empty, too large, binary or with no letters, which `--paste skipped: <why>, so it was left as it was and nothing was pasted` says on stderr), and throws with an Accessibility hint if that fails; off darwin it prints a one-line notice to stderr and skips. Empty, oversized (> `MAX_CLIPBOARD_CHARS`, or a read that stopped at `MAX_CLIPBOARD_READ_BYTES`: both `no changes (clipboard too large)`, exit 0, and `--paste` does not paste), letterless or binary text is reported as no changes (binary: `no changes (clipboard holds binary data, not text; left as it was)`). `looksBinary(text): boolean`, exported, is true for text holding a NUL or U+FFFD (what UTF-8 decoding leaves for bytes that are not UTF-8); the loop and `--once` never normalize or write such content, which used to replace a copied image with its mangled decoding.
- `runDaemonCommand(opts: DaemonCommandOptions { once?; which? } & both option sets): Promise<number>`: `--which` prints `clipboard backend: <name> (<description>)` (+ `--paste` availability) and returns 0, or 1 with the detection error; `--paste` without `--once` throws `--paste works only with --once (correct the clipboard once, then paste); run: lexicon daemon --once --paste`; `--once` runs once; else loops and returns `daemonStopExitCode` of how the loop stopped (130 after ctrl-C, 143 after SIGTERM, 0 when `signal` aborted). `parseBackendName(value)` validates `--backend`. `emptyLexiconHint(loaded: Pick<LoadedLexicon, 'merged' | 'skippedProject'>): string | undefined`: undefined when `merged` has terms, else `the lexicon has no terms yet; run \`lexicon setup\`, or add one with: lexicon learn <heard> <meant>`, or, with a `skippedProject`, `the project lexicon is not trusted, so its terms were not used; review it and run \`lexicon trust\``. `main(argv)` is the standalone entry.
- `main(argv)` (src/daemon/clipboard.ts, the standalone `node dist/daemon/clipboard.js` entry): `--cwd <dir>` or `--cwd=<dir>`; a missing, blank or flag-shaped value (`--cwd --once`) throws `--cwd expects a directory (got ...); run: lexicon daemon --cwd <dir>`.

## src/daemon/expander.ts
Pure expansion core of the resident expander (no IO). `EXPANDER_NAMED_KEYS` (`Backspace`, `Enter`, `Tab`, `Escape`, `Delete`, `Left`, `Right`, `Up`, `Down`, `Home`, `End`, `PageUp`, `PageDown`, `reset`), `isExpanderNamedKey(v)`, `graphemeCount(s)`, `DEFAULT_EXPANDER_BUFFER = 256`.
- `new Expander(lexicon: Lexicon | MatcherIndex, opts?: ExpanderOptions { normalize?: NormalizeOptions; maxBuffer? })`: matching defaults to `{ phonetic: false, fuzzy: false }`. `type(chunk): ExpansionAction | undefined` appends typed text; when whitespace finishes words, corrections that end in the newly finished words (and start at or after the end of its own last output) become `ExpansionAction { kind: 'expand' | 'undo'; backspaces (grapheme clusters); insert; replacements: { original; replacement; canonical }[] }`. `key(name)`: `Backspace` straight after an expansion returns the `undo` action and settles the restored word; any other Backspace edits the buffer; every other named key resets. `reset()`, `setIndex(lexicon | index)` (keeps the buffer), `buffer`.

## src/daemon/expander-server.ts
### Separate-process boundary (issue #220)

The existing CLI boundary lets a client invoke `lexicon normalize` as a
subprocess, send text on stdin, and read corrected text from stdout after the
command exits. The client handles the exit status and stderr. Each invocation
starts a new process; this boundary does not require a resident service.

**Proposed integration:** a client that needs repeated low-latency corrections
could instead run the opt-in `lexicon daemon --expander` as a separate,
long-lived process and communicate over its local socket (a named pipe on
Windows). The CLI flag and server described below already exist; adoption by a
client, automatic startup, supervision, reconnects and fallback to the CLI
are proposed behavior, not guarantees of the current CLI contract. A client
would own those lifecycle decisions and apply any returned expansion actions;
the server does not capture keystrokes or edit the client's text field. It
holds a lexicon and matcher index in its own process, not shared memory with
the client. Each connection has its own expansion buffer; requests and
responses are newline-delimited JSON in order, with an optional echoed `id`.
The existing socket protocol for a normalization request is, for example:

```json
{"id":"example","op":"normalize","text":"Ashler"}
```

With an `Ashler` alias for `Ashlr.AI`, the response is:

```json
{"id":"example","ok":true,"output":"Ashlr.AI","changed":true,"replacements":[{"original":"Ashler","replacement":"Ashlr.AI","canonical":"Ashlr.AI","reason":"alias","confidence":1}]}
```

See [DAEMON.md](DAEMON.md#resident-expander-opt-in) for the other operations,
socket access and lifecycle details. No client should assume the daemon is
running merely because the CLI is installed.

`runExpanderServer(opts: ExpanderServerOptions { socketPath?; signal?; expanderOptions?; onListening?; load? } & ClipboardCommonOptions): Promise<ClipboardDaemonStop>`: loads the lexicon (fails before listening if it will not load), prepares and listens on the socket (chmod 0600), answers newline-delimited JSON (`ping`, `normalize`, `type`, `key`, `reset`, `clipboard`; see docs/DAEMON.md), one Expander per connection, requests on a connection answered in order, clipboard requests serialized and run through `runClipboardOnce` with the cached lexicon. A request line over `MAX_EXPANDER_REQUEST_CHARS` (80000) is answered with an error and the connection ended; a `type` chunk over `MAX_EXPANDER_TYPE_CHARS` (4096) or `normalize` text over `MAX_CLIPBOARD_CHARS` is an error. Stops on SIGINT, SIGTERM or `signal` and removes the socket.
- `defaultExpanderSocketPath(env?, platform?, home?)`: `\\.\pipe\lexicon-expander-<user>` on Windows, else `<XDG_RUNTIME_DIR (absolute) or XDG state home>/lexicon/expander.sock`.
- `prepareSocketPath(path)`: refuses a path over 103 bytes, creates the directory 0700 (refuses one owned by another user, tightens a loose one), removes a stale socket, refuses a live one or a non-socket.
- `createExpanderSession(ctx)`: the per-connection request handler.
- `runDaemonCommand` takes `expander?` and `socketPath?`: `--socket` without `--expander` throws `--socket works only with --expander; ...`, and `--expander` with `--once`, `--paste` or `--which` throws `<flag> cannot be combined with --expander; ...`. `runClipboardOnce` takes `lexicon?: () => Promise<LoadedLexicon>` (the resident cache).

## src/serve (config.ts, server.ts, index.ts) and src/cli/cmd-serve.ts
Local HTTP API for surfaces without hooks or MCP (browser extension, Claude Desktop, the Codex app, macOS Shortcuts, Raycast, the menu bar app). `node:http` only, no framework. Other agents code against this exactly; see `docs/LOCAL-API.md` for curl examples.
- `src/serve/config.ts`: `SERVE_FILE_NAME = 'serve.json'`, `DEFAULT_PORT = 41733` (fixed so clients can find it), `DEFAULT_HOST = '127.0.0.1'`, `EXTENSION_ORIGIN_PREFIXES` (`chrome-extension://`, `moz-extension://`, `safari-web-extension://`). `ServeConfig { port; token; createdAt; allowedOrigins? }` lives at `getServePath(opts?: StoreOptions)` = `<dirname(global lexicon)>/serve.json`, written atomically with mode 0600 (`writeServeConfig`). `withServeConfigLock(fn, opts?)` is the file's own lock, which both the write and `ensureServeConfig` hold. `recordServePort(port, fallback, opts?): Promise<ServeConfig>` (what `start()` runs when the bound port differs from the file's) re-reads the file under that lock and changes only `port`, keeping every other key, `allowedOrigins` and a token written since `fallback` was read, and returns the config the file now holds; a missing file or one with no usable token gets `fallback` with the port, and one that is not valid JSON is left as it is. `ensureServeConfig(opts?)` reads and creates as one locked step (`port: 41733`, `token`: 32 random hex from `generateToken()`, `createdAt` ISO) and reuses it afterwards, first putting an existing file that group or other can read back to 0600 (POSIX; a chmod that fails throws naming `chmod 600 <path>`); unlocked, two first runs each minted a token and the loser's client was left holding one the server answers 401 to; `readServeConfig` returns undefined for a missing or unusable file, and `readServeConfigResult(opts?): Promise<ServeConfigRead>` reads it once and says which: `{ status: 'ok', path, config }`, `{ status: 'missing', path, reason }` (ENOENT only) or `{ status: 'unusable', path, reason }` (unreadable or a directory, empty, not valid JSON, or no usable token), `reason` the same ending `unusableServeConfigReason(file)` gives (a malformed token is never reused; a usable token is 32 to 128 hex characters in either case, compared byte for byte by the bearer check, so case is never folded); both read past a leading byte order mark. `ensureServeConfig` refuses a serve.json that is not valid JSON (`<path> is not valid JSON (...); fix it, or delete it ...`) instead of minting a new token over a hand edit, and when it does mint one (missing, empty, or no usable token) keeps the file's `allowedOrigins`. `ensureServeConfig(opts?, hooks?: EnsureServeConfigHooks { onTokenReplaced?(message) })` calls `onTokenReplaced` after writing the new file when a non-empty serve.json had no usable token, with `<path> had no usable token (<why>), so a new token was written there; clients paired with the old one are refused until they get it: lexicon serve --pair (browser extension) or lexicon serve --show (copy it)` (`<why>`: `it is not a JSON object`, `it has no "token" key`, `"token" is not a string`, `"token" is not 32 to 128 hex characters (0-9, a-f, A-F)`); `start()` logs it as `[serve] warning: ...` (also when `quiet`), and `lexicon serve --show` and `--install` print it as `lexicon: warning: ...` on stderr. A missing or empty file is replaced without a word. `unusableServeConfigReason(file)` is the `<why>` of the `--status` and `--pair` not-configured line (`does not exist; lexicon serve creates it` for a missing file only, `could not be read (<code>); make it a readable file, or remove it so lexicon serve makes a new token` for one that exists but cannot be read, such as a directory, `is empty; ...`, `is not valid JSON; fix it, or delete it and lexicon serve makes a new token`, `has no usable token (<why>); lexicon serve replaces it`, `<why>` as the token-replaced warning gives it). `isOriginAllowed(origin, allowedOrigins?)`: an extension origin or an exact match in `allowedOrigins`; `*` is never honoured. `serveUrl(port, host?)`, `isLoopbackHost(host)` (`localhost`, an IPv4 address in 127.0.0.0/8, or IPv6 loopback in any spelling, bracketed or not; a hostname such as `127.example.com` is not loopback, so `lexicon serve --host` warns for it). `probeHealth(baseUrl, opts?: { token?; timeoutMs?; fetch? }): Promise<HealthProbe>` GETs `<baseUrl>/health` (bearer sent when `token` is given, default timeout 1.5 s) and returns `{ state: 'up', body }` for 2xx, `{ state: 'failing', status, error }` when the server answered with an error (`error` from its JSON body, else `HTTP <status>`), or `{ state: 'down', error }` when nothing answered.
- `src/serve/server.ts`: `createServer(opts?: ServeServerOptions { port?; host?; cwd?; globalPath?; quiet?; log?; version?; reloadMs?; maxConcurrent?; normalizeBudgetMs?; requestTimeoutMs?; headersTimeoutMs? }): LexiconHttpServer { server: http.Server; start(): Promise<ServeStartInfo { port; token; host; url; configPath }>; stop(): Promise<void> }`. `start()` ensures serve.json, binds `host:port` (default 127.0.0.1:41733; `port: 0` picks a free port), records the live port back into serve.json when it differs, and turns EADDRINUSE into a readable error. Unless `quiet`, `log` gets one line per request: `[serve] <METHOD> <path> <status> <ms>ms` when the response finishes, or `[serve] <METHOD> <path> aborted by the client before a response was sent <ms>ms` (or `after <status> headers were sent`) when the connection closes first. `stop()` closes every connection. No signal handling here (the CLI does it) so tests and the menu bar app can embed it. `MAX_BODY_BYTES` (1 MB), `MAX_CONCURRENT_REQUESTS` (64), `LEXICON_RELOAD_MS` (2000), `NORMALIZE_BUDGET_MS` (1000), `REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS` (30000, the whole request, headers and body, must arrive within it; Node's default was 300 s, so a trickled body held a concurrency slot for five minutes) and `HEADERS_TIMEOUT_MS` (10000, clamped to the request timeout), both overridable per server with `requestTimeoutMs`/`headersTimeoutMs`; a late request is answered 408 with `{ error }` through the `clientError` handler (never when a response is already under way), `EXPORT_CONTENT_TYPES`, `exportContentType(format)`.
  - Request target: `requestUrl(target: string | undefined): URL` (exported) parses `req.url` by prefixing `http://localhost`, so a target starting with `//` (`//health`) keeps that path instead of being read as a host; an absolute-form target keeps only its path and query; anything else is `/`. `//health` is a 404.
  - Host guard (DNS rebinding), before every route including `OPTIONS`: `isAllowedHost(req.headers.host, boundPort, host)` must accept the `Host` header, which is exactly `127.0.0.1:<port>`, `localhost:<port>` or `[::1]:<port>` (case-insensitive); with a non-loopback `--host`, the bound IP literal too (any IP literal for a wildcard `0.0.0.0`/`::` bind), never a DNS name. Otherwise 403 `{ error }` naming the accepted forms, without CORS headers.
  - Auth: every request except `GET /health` (without `?cwd=`), `GET /pair` and `OPTIONS` needs `Authorization: Bearer <token>` (constant-time compare of the SHA-256 digests of both, so a token of the wrong length takes as long as any other); a token that does not match the one the server holds makes it stat `serve.json` and, when the file changed (inode, size or times) since it last looked, re-read it with `readServeConfig`'s checks and adopt its token and `allowedOrigins` (one refresh at a time; a missing, unusable or over-64 KiB file keeps what the server has; `GET /pair` refreshes the same way before it shows the token), so a token `lexicon serve --show` mints after the file was deleted works without a restart and the replaced one is refused; otherwise 401 `{ error }` with `WWW-Authenticate: Bearer`, the `error` naming both ways to the token: `lexicon serve --pair` for the browser extension and `lexicon serve --show` for any other client (`missing or invalid bearer token (...)`, or `/health?cwd= needs the bearer token, which plain /health does not (...)`).
  - CORS: `OPTIONS` answers 204. `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: <origin>` (plus `Allow-Headers: Authorization, Content-Type`, `Allow-Methods: GET, POST, DELETE`, `Expose-Headers: X-Lexicon-Note`, `Vary: Origin`) is set only when `isOriginAllowed`, and never on `/pair`; never `*`, no headers for any other origin. A preflight with `Access-Control-Request-Private-Network: true` (Chrome's Private Network Access check) also gets `Access-Control-Allow-Private-Network: true`, under the same condition.
  - Bodies are JSON objects (UTF-8, a leading byte order mark ignored); over 1 MB (declared or streamed) -> 413 after draining (never buffered, cut off at 16 MB); a `Content-Encoding` other than `identity` -> 415 (bodies are not decompressed); invalid JSON, an empty body (but on `POST /packs/:name`) or a non-object -> 400; schema errors (zod) -> 400 `invalid request body: <field>: <message>`; every one of these body 400s ends with the route's fields, `(POST /normalize takes { text, dryRun?, minConfidence?, cwd? })` and so on (`BODY_SHAPES`, internal); every body schema is `.strict()`, so an unknown key (a typo such as `dry_run`) is a 400 naming it instead of being dropped; `DELETE /packs/:name` with a non-empty body -> 400 (its options are query parameters); query strings are strict too (`strictQuery`): on every route but `/pair` an unknown parameter is a 400 naming it and the ones the route takes (`cwd` on `/health` (checked before the token, so an anonymous caller gets the 400 too), `/lexicon`, `/stats`, `/packs`; `canonical` on `/aliases`; `scope`, `cwd` on `DELETE /packs/:name`; `cwd`, `category`, `limit` on `/export/:format`) and a repeated one is a 400, except `category` on `/export`; a POST route takes none (`noQuery`, checked after the body is read and before anything is written): `unknown query parameter "<key>" (a POST takes its options in the JSON body, not the query string: <the route's fields>)`; unknown route -> 404; wrong method -> 405 with an `Allow` header naming the route's methods (`GET, HEAD`, `POST`, or `POST, DELETE` on `/packs/:name`); `HEAD` on a GET route runs that route and answers with its status and headers and no body (every response sets `Content-Length` from its body, so a HEAD carries the length the GET would have); `ProjectTrustError` -> 403, its message restated for HTTP (`project lexicon at <path> is untrusted, so nothing was written. Review it and run \`lexicon trust\`, then retry; or retry with "scope": "global" in the body to use the global lexicon instead`, `?scope=global` on a DELETE); `PackNotFoundError` -> 404; an `InputError` from core (src/util/errors.ts) -> 400; a `FileLockError` whose lock another writer still held at the timeout (`isHeldLockError`) -> 503 with `Retry-After: 1` and `another writer is still using the lexicon file: <message>; retry after 1 second (Retry-After)` (the advice without `<message>` to a caller without the bearer token); anything else (a lexicon file that does not parse, any other `FileLockError`, an fs error) -> 500, `internal error: <message>` to a caller with the bearer token and `internal error (the lexicon serve log has the detail)` to one without (for a lexicon file that does not parse or validate, `internal error: ` plus `BROKEN_LEXICON_ANONYMOUS`, exported, which names `lexicon serve --status` and `lexicon doctor` and no path), detail always logged. A `100-continue` request whose `Content-Length` is over 1 MB is answered 413 before its body is sent (a `checkContinue` handler invites only bodies within the limit), and a request the HTTP parser rejects (`clientError`) is answered with `{ error }` and 400 (431 for headers over the limit, 408 for a request timeout) and `Connection: close`. `errorStatus(err): number` is that mapping, exported. More than `maxConcurrent` in-flight requests -> 503 with `Retry-After: 1` and `too many concurrent requests (<n> already in flight), so this one was not run; retry it after 1 second (Retry-After)`. `GET /pair`, `/health?cwd=` and every bearer route before `start()` has read `serve.json` (an embedder listening on `server` itself) -> 503 with `Retry-After: 1` and `server is starting; retry after 1 second (Retry-After)`. Every response carries `Cache-Control: no-store`.
  - `cwd` for project resolution: `cwd` in the request body (POST) or query string (GET) when given, else `opts.cwd` (the server's cwd). A given `cwd` that is empty or is not an existing directory (after `path.resolve`) -> 400 `cwd must not be empty ...` / `cwd is not an existing directory: <path>`; `/health?cwd=` checks the token first, so the 401 still comes before it. The lexicon is loaded through `loadLexicon({ cwd, globalPath })`, cached per cwd for `reloadMs` (2 s, at most 32 cwds; past that the least recently used cwd is dropped) and dropped after every write. A load that was in flight when a write invalidated the cache (a generation counter bumped by every write invalidation and by `stop()`) answers its own request but is not cached, so it cannot put the pre-write lexicon back; the hit-count invalidation does not bump it. Which cached loads a project write drops is decided by `canonicalFile(filePath, platform = process.platform, realpath = realpathSync): string` (exported): the realpath when the file exists, case-folded on `win32` and case-folded and NFC-normalised on `darwin`, whose default filesystems ignore both, so two spellings of one file match (on a case-sensitive APFS volume it can only over-match, which costs a reload). A write to the global file (`/learn`, `/add`, `POST` and `DELETE /packs/:name`) drops every cwd; a write to a project file drops only the cached loads whose `project` or `skippedProject` is that file (compared by realpath), or that had neither and whose cwd now resolves to it (`resolvePaths`; the write created it); `DELETE /packs/:name` counts as global when one of its `files` is the global file. A hit-counting `/normalize` drops only the cached loads that hold one of the terms it matched (`recordHits` changes nothing else). The other cwds keep their load and matcher index. The trust gate applies exactly as elsewhere: an untrusted project file is never merged, and only its path is ever reported.
  - `info.ts` exports `ServerInfoSchema`, `ServerInfoResponse` (inferred from the schema), and `SERVER_FEATURES`, also exported by the package root and `src/serve/index.ts`. The strict schema requires nonblank version and host strings, an integer port from 1 to 65535, and an array of nonblank feature identifiers. Clients should tolerate new feature identifiers.
  - Endpoints (JSON in/out, `Content-Type: application/json; charset=utf-8`):
    - `GET /info` -> `ServerInfoResponse { version: string, host: string, port: number, features: string[] }`. Bearer required; no query parameters. Reports the configured bind host and actual listening port (including an ephemeral port selected with port 0). Contains no token, paths or vocabulary and does not load the lexicon. Default bind host is IPv4 loopback `127.0.0.1`, port 41733.
    - `GET /health[?cwd=]` -> `HealthResponse { ok: true, version, terms, projectTrust?, port }`. No auth for plain `/health`; with `?cwd=` the bearer is required (401 otherwise), so an unauthenticated caller cannot probe which directories carry a `.lexicon.yaml` or their trust status.
    - `GET /pair` (`PAIR_PATH`) -> `text/html; charset=utf-8`, `pairPageHtml({ token, port, version })`: inline CSS, no script, no external request, `<meta name="lexicon-token">`, `lexicon-port`, `lexicon-version` (`PAIR_META`), `<p id="status">` for the extension to rewrite, `<div id="install">` revealed by a CSS animation after `PAIR_INSTALL_HINT_MS` (3 s), which on a port other than `DEFAULT_PORT` also holds `<p id="other-port">` saying the extension pairs itself on 41733 only and how to pair this port from its Options (no URL on the page). `pairPortNote(port): string` is the CLI form of that sentence (`the extension pairs itself on port 41733 only; for port <port>, open its Options, set Base URL to http://127.0.0.1:<port> and click "pair in this browser" there, then allow access to that port`). No bearer; instead 403 unless `isLoopbackAddress(req.socket.remoteAddress)`, `isPairHost(req.headers.host, boundPort)` (exactly `127.0.0.1:<port>` or `localhost:<port>`, case-insensitive) and `isPairOrigin(req.headers.origin, req.headers.host)` (no `Origin`, as on a navigation, or exactly `http://<Host>`; a cross-origin fetch, including from any browser-extension origin, is refused, since the page carries the token). No CORS headers are ever sent for it. Extra headers `PAIR_HEADERS`: `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; style-src 'unsafe-inline'`, `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` (plus the usual `Cache-Control: no-store`). Non-GET -> 405.
