    - `POST /normalize { text, dryRun?, minConfidence?, cwd? }` -> `NormalizeResponse` = `NormalizeResult & { summary; incomplete?; projectTrust?; skippedProject? }` (`diffSummary`, `''` when nothing matched; `incomplete` = `timeBudgetIncomplete(text.length, normalizeBudgetMs)` only when the budget stopped matching, otherwise absent; `projectTrust` whenever a project file exists for `cwd`, and `skippedProject` its path when it was not merged, as `/stats` gives them; `hint` = `emptyNormalizeHint(loaded)` only when the merged lexicon has no terms: `The lexicon has no terms yet, so nothing can be corrected; run \`lexicon setup\`, or add one with: lexicon learn <heard> <meant>.`, or the `lexicon trust` step when only a skipped project file holds terms). Records hits best-effort when `changed` and not `dryRun`, then drops only the cached loads holding a matched term. Matches against the cached load's index (`loadMatcherIndex`) and stops after `normalizeBudgetMs` (default `NORMALIZE_BUDGET_MS`, 1000 ms; checked before each token after the first), returning the corrections found so far, so one huge body cannot hold the single-threaded server; `incomplete` says the rest was not checked.
    - `POST /learn { heard, meant, scope?, cwd? }` -> `LearnResponse { term, created, aliasAdded, path, unchanged?, reversed?, conflict?, note?, untrustedProject? }` (`reversed: true` with `unchanged` for `LearnResult.reversed`) (`path` is the file written; `conflict` and `untrustedProject` are those of `LearnResult`; `unchanged: true` when `created` and `aliasAdded` are both false, so nothing was written, and then `note` leads with `Nothing was added: "<heard>" was already an alias of "<canonical>".` or, for `heard` that is the canonical in another case, `Nothing was added: "<canonical>" is already the canonical spelling; "<heard>" is corrected to it.` (`learnUnchangedNote(heard, canonical)`, as `lexicon learn` gives it); `conflictNote([conflict])` is the `note` with `conflict`, after that sentence when both apply, as `learn --json` gives it).
    - `POST /add { canonical, aliases?, phonetic?, category?, notes?, never?, scope?, cwd? }` -> `AddResponse { term, path, created, unchanged?, note?, conflicts? }` (the MCP `add_term` shape; `unchanged: true` with `note` = `unchangedTermNote(canonical)` when `addTerm` wrote nothing; aliases omitted -> `defaultAliases: suggestAliases(canonical)`, which `addTerm` applies only to a term it creates, while an explicit `[]` passes none, as MCP `add_term` does; `conflicts: LearnConflict[]` from `spellingConflicts` over the reloaded lexicon, only when another term also holds one of the aliases).
    - `GET /lexicon[?cwd=]` -> `LexiconResponse { lexicon: <merged Lexicon>, paths: { global, project? }, projectTrust?, skippedProject?: <path only> }`. `paths` names the files merged (`loaded.global.path`, and `loaded.project.path` only when the project file was trusted), the same as MCP `list_terms` `paths`; a skipped file is `skippedProject`, with `projectTrust` set. `note` (`lexiconNote(loaded)`, only when there is something to say): for a skipped project file, the `lexicon trust` step `lexicon list --full` gives (`An untrusted project .lexicon.yaml exists and was not loaded; review it and run \`lexicon trust\` to enable it.`, or `... changed since it was trusted ... run \`lexicon trust\` again.`), else for a lexicon with no terms `The lexicon has no terms yet; add one with: lexicon add "Ashlr.AI" Ashler, or run \`lexicon setup\`.`, as MCP `list_terms` says it.
    - `GET /export/:format[?cwd=&category=&limit=]` -> the export as text, filtered by `category` (repeated or comma-separated, case-insensitive; an unknown one is a 400) and capped by `limit` (a positive integer of at most `EXPORT_MAX_LIMIT`, else 400), the `ExportOptions` of `lexicon export --category/--limit` and MCP `export_lexicon`, with the exporter's content type by `EXPORT_FORMAT_INFO[format].ext`: csv `text/csv`, json `application/json`, md `text/markdown`, txt `text/plain`, plist `application/xml`, yml `application/yaml` (all `; charset=utf-8`); unknown format -> 404 `unknown export format "<format>" (one of: <EXPORT_FORMATS>)`, as MCP `export_lexicon` words it. When there is something to say the body is unchanged and the `X-Lexicon-Note` header (`EXPORT_NOTE_HEADER`) carries `exportNote(loaded, format, opts): string | undefined`, one line of printable ASCII: the lexicon has no terms yet (naming `lexicon setup`), or only an untrusted project file has terms (naming `lexicon trust`), or no term matched `?category=`, or an alias-row format got no term with an alias (`No exported term has an alias, and <format> writes one row per alias, so the export has no rows; ...`), as `lexicon export` and MCP `export_lexicon` say it, plus the skipped project file's `lexicon trust` sentence when terms were loaded; no header otherwise.
    - `GET /stats[?cwd=]` -> `StatsResponse` = `LexiconStats` plus `projectTrust?` and `skippedProject?` (path only) when a project file exists or was skipped, as `/lexicon` and `/packs` carry them (the counts leave a skipped file out), and `note` (`statsNote(loaded)`, only when there is something to say): the same `lexicon trust` step plus `Its terms are not in the counts.` for a skipped file, else for a lexicon with no terms the `emptyStatsNote` text `lexicon stats --json` gives, as MCP `lexicon_stats` says it.
    - `GET /packs[?cwd=]` -> `PacksResponse { packs, installed, registryMismatch?, projectTrust?, skippedProject? }` (every `PackInfo` plus which are installed; `installed` holds community refs too; `registryMismatch` from `findRegistryRecordMismatch`, as MCP `list_packs`; the skipped project file by path only; no registry index is read, so no network access); `POST /packs/:name` installs one (-> `InstallPackResponse`: `InstallPackResult` with `alreadyInstalled: boolean` always set and a `summary`, `installed <name>: N added, M merged into <path>` or, for a reinstall that changed nothing, `packAlreadyInstalledNote`) and `DELETE /packs/:name` removes it (-> `UninstallPackResponse`: `UninstallPackResult` plus a `summary`, and `notInstalled: true` when no file listed the pack, so nothing was written) (`PACK_ROUTE_RE = /^\/packs\/([a-z0-9-]+)$/`).
    - `GET /aliases?canonical=X` -> `AliasesResponse`, the spellings STT is likely to produce for a name, for an onboarding form. `canonical` is cleaned as the schema cleans a canonical (`stripInvisible`, then trimmed) before the checks: empty -> 400 `canonical query parameter is required (e.g. GET /aliases?canonical=Ashlr.AI)`, over `LIMITS.word` (80) -> 400 `canonical must be at most 80 characters`, a control character left inside it (`hasControlChars`: C0 including tab and newline, DEL, C1, U+2028/U+2029, which `NonEmptyWord` refuses too) -> 400 `canonical must not contain control characters or newlines`, a U+FFFE, U+FFFF or unpaired surrogate (`findUnencodableChar`) -> 400 `canonical must not contain U+XXXX (U+FFFE, U+FFFF and unpaired surrogates fit no file format)`; the response's `canonical` is the cleaned value.

      For example, URL-encode the canonical name `Mason Wyatt` and send the bearer token as described above:

      ```http
      GET /aliases?canonical=Mason%20Wyatt
      ```

      The 200 JSON response contains the cleaned canonical and suggested aliases, not stored terms:

      ```json
      { "canonical": "Mason Wyatt", "aliases": ["Mason Wiatt", "Mason Wyat"] }
      ```
  - Logs one line per request to `opts.log` (default stderr) unless `quiet`: `[serve] <METHOD> <path> <status> <ms>ms` (path sanitized).
- `src/serve/index.ts` re-exports both modules.
- `src/cli/cli-entry.ts` (no imports from the rest of the CLI): `PACKAGE_NAME`, `CLI_ENV_VAR = 'LEXICON_CLI'`, and `PACKAGE_NAME` / `findPackageRoot` re-exported from `src/util/package.ts` (`findPackageRoot(from: string)` walks up to the directory whose package.json is named `@ashlr/lexicon`; undefined otherwise). `whichBin` is gone: use `findOnPathSync` from `src/util/which.ts`, `resolveCliEntry(opts?: { cliPath?; env?; moduleUrl? }): string` = `cliPath` | `$LEXICON_CLI` | `<root>/dist/cli/index.js` when it exists (root from `moduleUrl`, default this module, so it is the same file from src/, dist/ and the plugin/ bundles) | a `lexicon` on PATH resolved through `realpathSync` and accepted only when it ends in `.js`/`.mjs`/`.cjs`, else a readable Error naming `LEXICON_CLI`. Existence is checked for the last two only.
- `src/cli/serve-paths.ts` (no filesystem): `LAUNCH_AGENT_LABEL = 'ai.ashlr.lexicon.serve'`, `SYSTEMD_UNIT_NAME = 'lexicon-serve.service'`, `SERVE_LABEL_ENV_VAR = 'LEXICON_SERVE_LABEL'`, `serveLabel(env?)` (the override when it matches `[A-Za-z0-9._-]+`, else the default), `launchAgentPath(home, env?)` = `~/Library/LaunchAgents/<label>.plist`, `launchAgentLogPath(home)`, `systemdUnitPath(home, env)` (honours `XDG_CONFIG_HOME`), `programPathFromPlist(text)` (second `ProgramArguments` string) and `programPathFromUnit(text)` (second quoted `ExecStart` word, `$$` read as `$`). cmd-serve.ts re-exports the names and paths.
- `src/cli/cmd-serve.ts`: `registerServeCommands(program, io)` adds `serve` (`--port <n>` 0..65535, `--host <host>`, `--json`, `--quiet`, `--show`, `--status`, `--pair`, `--install`, `--uninstall`); the modes are mutually exclusive: two of them are refused with `use only one of --show, --status, --pair, --install, --uninstall` and the `nextStep` `pass only one of <the flags given> (see lexicon serve --help)` (`{ ok: false, error, nextStep }` under `--json`), exit 1. `--quiet` with one of those modes is refused the same way: `--quiet only applies to a running server; <mode> logs no requests`, `nextStep: 'drop --quiet, or run lexicon serve --quiet to start the server without request logs'`. `--quiet` drops only the per-request lines: failures, the listening line and `lexicon serve stopped` are still printed. Every installed service runs `serve --quiet` (`SERVICE_SERVE_ARGS = ['serve', '--quiet']`), so the service log holds failures and start/stop only. `runServe(opts: ServeOptions, io, deps?: ServeDeps { platform?; home?; env?; exec?: ServeExec; cliPath?; nodePath?; nodeVersion?; uid?; fetch?; signal?; onListening?; sleep?: (ms) => Promise<void> })` dispatches to:
  - `runServeForeground`: `createServer` + `start()`, prints `lexicon serve listening on http://127.0.0.1:41733 (token in ~/.config/lexicon/serve.json)` (or a JSON line with `ok: true, url, port, host, token, configPath` under `--json`), then waits for SIGINT/SIGTERM/`deps.signal`, `stop()`s and prints `lexicon serve stopped` (not under `--json`). Before it starts, `capServiceLog(launchAgentLogPath(home), deps.logFd ?? 2, max = SERVE_LOG_MAX_BYTES)` (4 MiB) caps the LaunchAgent log: when that descriptor is `serve.log` itself (same device and inode) and it is over the cap, the file is copied to `serve.log.1` (replacing an older one) and truncated (launchd appends, so later lines land at the new end); any error leaves it alone. Never binds a non-loopback interface unless `--host` is given explicitly, and then prints a red `warning:` on stderr. A busy port returns 1 with `port N is already in use (... lexicon serve --status)`.
  - `runServeShow` (`--show`): `url:`, `token:`, `config:` lines (JSON: `{ ok: true, url, port, token, configPath }`); `--port` overrides the recorded port.
  - `runServeStatus` (`--status`): read-only. `readServeConfig()` (never `ensureServeConfig`, so a missing `serve.json` is not created); with no usable config it probes `--port` or `DEFAULT_PORT` without a token, prints `serve is not configured: <path> does not exist; lexicon serve creates it` (or `has no usable token (<why>); lexicon serve replaces it`, `<why>` as the token-replaced warning gives it) before the status line, and adds `configured: false`, `configPath` and `note` to the JSON. Then `probeHealth(url, { token, timeoutMs: STATUS_TIMEOUT_MS })` (1.5 s); `lexicon serve is up at <url> (version X, N terms)` -> 0; a non-2xx answer prints `lexicon serve is running at <url> but /health failed (HTTP <status>): <server error>` (through `safeError`, every line and the whole path) -> 1 with no start hint; nothing answering prints `lexicon serve is down at <url> (<reason>)` plus a start hint -> 1; a 2xx whose body is not lexicon serve's (`isLexiconHealth(body)`: `ok` true, a non-empty `version` string and a whole-number `terms`, the shape the extension checks; a bare `{ ok: true }` is not enough) prints `something else answers on that port: <url>/health did not answer as lexicon serve does` and the `lexicon serve --port <n>` hint, not the start hint -> 1. JSON leads with `ok` equal to `up` (exit code 0): `{ ok: true, up: true, url, health }`, or `{ ok: false, up: false, url, running?: true, status?, reason, error, hint, nextStep }` (`nextStep` equals `hint`, the key every other failing `--json` document uses), where `error` and `hint` are the keys MCP `serve_status` returns (`error` equals `reason` except for another program on the port, `FOREIGN_HEALTH_ERROR`; `hint` from `SERVE_STATUS_HINTS.failing | down | foreign`).
  - `runServePair` (`--pair`): read-only like `--status`, it reads `serve.json` with `readServeConfig` and never creates it: without a usable one it prints `serve is not configured: <path> does not exist; lexicon serve creates it` (or `has no usable token (<why>); lexicon serve replaces it`, `<why>` as the token-replaced warning gives it) and the `lexicon serve --install` next step on stderr -> 1 (JSON `{ ok: false, error, nextStep, up: false, configured: false, configPath }`), opening nothing. Otherwise it GETs `/health` the same way; when down prints `lexicon serve is down at <url>` and `start it with \`lexicon serve\` or \`lexicon serve --install\` first` on stderr -> 1 (JSON `{ ok: false, error, nextStep, up: false, url, reason }`). A 2xx `/health` whose body is not lexicon serve's (`isLexiconHealth`, as `--status` checks it) prints `FOREIGN_HEALTH_ERROR` with the URL and `SERVE_STATUS_HINTS.foreign` on stderr -> 1 (JSON `{ ok: false, error, nextStep, up: false, url, reason: 'unexpected /health response' }`), opening nothing. When up runs `browserOpenCommand(url, platform)` through `deps.exec` (no shell): darwin `open <url>`, linux/freebsd/openbsd `xdg-open <url>`, win32 `cmd /c start "" <url>`, other platforms none; prints `opening <url> in your browser` -> 0, or `could not open a browser (<why>)` on stderr plus the URL on stdout -> 1 (JSON `{ ok, up: true, url, opened, error?, note? }`, `ok` equal to `opened`, with `error` when the browser did not open). On a port other than `DEFAULT_PORT` the line after it (and JSON `note`) is `pairPortNote(port)`, since the extension's pairing script runs on 41733 only. `pairUrl(port)` = `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/pair`, always 127.0.0.1 (what the extension's match pattern and the server's Host check expect).
  - `runServeInstall` (`--install`) resolves the program with `resolveCliEntry({ env, cliPath: deps.cliPath? })` (a resolver error is printed and returns 1) and, on darwin/linux, refuses with `refusing to install the login service: <path> does not exist (...); nothing was written` (exit 1, no file written, no `bootout`) unless `fs.access` succeeds; then prints exactly what it wrote. The node it runs is `deps.nodePath ?? stableNodePath(process.execPath)`: `stableNodePath(execPath, exists?): string` maps a Homebrew node's versioned `<prefix>/Cellar/node[@N]/<version>/bin/node` (which `brew upgrade` and `brew cleanup` delete) to its `<prefix>/opt/node[@N]/bin/node` symlink when that exists, and returns any other path as is. The plist and the unit are written through a temp file and a rename (`writeFileAtomic`, so a failed write leaves the old file whole) with mode 0644 whatever the umask or the replaced file's mode (launchd refuses a group- or world-writable plist). darwin: `launchAgentPath(home, env)` (`serveLabel(env)`, `launchAgentPlist(nodePath, cliPath, logPath, label?)`: `ProgramArguments = [stableNodePath(process.execPath), <abs dist/cli/index.js>, "serve", "--quiet"]`, `RunAtLoad`, `KeepAlive`, stdout/stderr to `~/Library/Logs/lexicon/serve.log`), then `launchctl bootout gui/$UID/<label>` (a failure is ignored; after a success it polls `launchctl print gui/$UID/<label>` every `BOOTOUT_POLL_MS` (100) ms, at most `BOOTOUT_POLL_TRIES` (50) times, until it fails, since bootout returns before launchd has removed the job and a bootstrap in that window fails; still loaded after that, it warns `<label> was still loaded 5s after launchctl bootout; bootstrapping anyway`), `launchctl bootstrap gui/$UID <plist>`, falling back to `launchctl load <plist>`. linux: `systemdUnitPath(home, env)` (`systemdUnit(nodePath, cliPath)`: each path in double quotes with C escapes and `%` doubled, and a `$` in the CLI path (an argument, which systemd expands) doubled; the node path and `Environment=` values keep a single `$`), then `systemctl --user daemon-reload`, `systemctl --user enable lexicon-serve.service` and `systemctl --user restart lexicon-serve.service` (`enable --now` left a running unit as it was, so a reinstall kept the old process). win32: `schtasks /Create /TN <scheduledTaskName(env)> /XML <file> /F` (a logon-triggered task from `scheduledTaskXml`), then `schtasks /End /TN <name>` (a failure is fine when `schtasksTaskState` finds the task absent or not running, or, as a fallback, the English message says it is not running; any other prints `lexicon: created the Scheduled Task "<name>" but could not end its running instance (schtasks /End failed, see above) ...` and returns 1) and `schtasks /Run /TN <name>` (a failure prints `... but could not start it (schtasks /Run failed, see above); it starts at your next logon ...` and returns 1), since `/Create /F` leaves a running instance as it was and only a logon starts the task. Every command run is echoed as `ran: ...`; a failing final command returns 1 with the file left in place. `runServeUninstall` uses the same label.
  - `serveInstallBlocker(deps?: Pick<ServeDeps, 'cliPath' | 'env' | 'platform'>): Promise<string | undefined>` returns the reason `runServeInstall` would refuse before writing anything (the resolver's error, a path in an npx or Claude Code plugin cache with the `npm i -g @ashlr/lexicon` fix, or a missing path on darwin/linux/win32), undefined when it would go ahead; it runs the same checks.
  - `runServeUninstall` (`--uninstall`) reverses it: `launchctl bootout` (fallback `unload`) and remove the plist; `systemctl --user disable --now`, remove the unit, `daemon-reload`; on Windows `schtasks /End /TN <name>` (deleting a task leaves its running instance serving; a failure other than not running or not found, judged as on install, prints `lexicon: could not end the running instance of the Scheduled Task "<name>" ...`, the delete still runs, and the exit is 1), then `schtasks /Delete /TN <name> /F`: success prints `removed Scheduled Task <name>`, a failure when `schtasksTaskState` finds no task (or, as a fallback, whose English message says it cannot be found) prints `Scheduled Task <name> was not installed` (exit 0), and any other failure prints `lexicon: could not remove the Scheduled Task "<name>": <schtasks error>` on stderr and exits 1. `schtasksTaskState(exec, taskName): Promise<{ exists; running? }>` runs `schtasks /Query /TN <name> /FO CSV /NH /V` only after an `/End` or `/Delete` failure: a non-zero exit is `exists: false`; otherwise `running` is true when a row's Last Result is 267009 (SCHED_S_TASK_RUNNING), false when no row has it, undefined when there is no row, so the decision does not depend on the language schtasks writes its messages in. A plist or unit that is not there is reported as `was not installed` (exit 0); any other failure to remove it prints `lexicon: could not remove <path>: <reason>` on stderr and exits 1.
  - Under `--json`, `--install` and `--uninstall` run into a buffer and print one `ServeServiceJson { ok, action: 'install' | 'uninstall', platform, output, error?, nextStep? }` (`output` the uncoloured transcript they print without it; on a failure `error` is the run's last `lexicon:` stderr line and `nextStep` names the same command again), with the same exit code; the run's stderr still goes to stderr. Two modes together under `--json` are `{ ok: false, error, nextStep }` for these too.
  - `launchAgentPath(home)`, `launchAgentLogPath(home)`, `systemdUnitPath(home, env)`, `defaultServeExec` (spawn without a shell) are exported for tests.
  - Environment passthrough: `SERVE_PASSTHROUGH_ENV = ['LEXICON_PATH', 'XDG_CONFIG_HOME']`. Each one set at install time goes into the service as an absolute path, so the service reads the lexicon and `serve.json` that `serve --show` reads in that shell (a login service does not inherit the shell). `launchAgentPlist(nodePath, cliPath, logPath, label?, env?)` adds an `EnvironmentVariables` dict; `systemdUnit(nodePath, cliPath, env?)` adds one `Environment=` line per entry (`%` escaped); `scheduledTaskXml(nodePath, cliPath, userId?, envFile?)` prefixes the arguments with `--env-file="<envFile>"`, where `envFile` is `SERVE_ENV_FILE_NAME = 'serve.env'` next to `serve.json`, written by `serveEnvFile(env)` (each value single-quoted, or backtick-quoted when it holds a `'`; throws on a line break or both quote kinds) through `writeFileAtomic` with mode 0600, like `serve.json`; `--uninstall` removes it once the task is deleted or was not there (a failure to remove it is reported and exits 1). `nodeSupportsEnvFile(version)` gates it on Node >= 20.6; older Node gets a warning and no env file. With neither variable set, every service file is unchanged. `ServeDeps.nodeVersion?` overrides `process.version` for that gate.

## src/voice (process.ts, devices.ts, models.ts, recorder.ts, transcribe.ts, history.ts, voice.ts) and src/cli/cmd-voice.ts
Local push-to-talk dictation: ffmpeg (16 kHz mono s16 WAV) -> whisper.cpp (`--prompt` = `exportLexicon(merged, 'whisper-prompt')`) -> `normalize()` -> stdout / clipboard / Cmd+V. Every process interaction goes through injectable deps so tests never touch a microphone. See `docs/VOICE.md`.
- `src/voice/process.ts`: `VoiceExec = (cmd, args, opts?: ExecOptions { stdin?; timeoutMs?; signal?: AbortSignal }) => Promise<ExecResult { code: number | null; stdout; stderr; timedOut? }>` (`timedOut: true` when `timeoutMs` ran out and the child was killed; rejects only when the binary cannot start, e.g. `ENOENT`; a non-zero exit is a resolved result; `signal` aborting sends the child SIGTERM and resolves with code null); `VoiceSpawn = (cmd, args, opts: SpawnOptions { detached; logFile? }) => ChildHandle { pid; exited: Promise<number | null>; stderr() }` (a detached child gets its own group, stderr appended to `logFile`, and is unref'd). `defaultExec`, `defaultSpawn`, `defaultIsAlive(pid)` (signal 0; `EPERM` counts as alive), `defaultKill(pid, signal)`. `ProcessDescribe = (pid) => Promise<string | undefined>` and `ProcessList = () => Promise<ReadonlyArray<{ pid; command }> | undefined>`, built by `makeProcessDescribe(exec, platform)` and `makeProcessList(exec, platform)`: `ps -p <pid> -o command=` and `ps -A -o pid=,command=`, or `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process` on win32, where the filter is a literal so no caller path is interpolated into a shell. Both answer undefined when the question cannot be answered, and an empty list means the table was read and held nothing. The distinction is load-bearing: collapsing them made a host without `ps` abandon a live recording and start a second recorder. `findOrphanRecorder` therefore answers `number | 'unknown' | undefined`, and `commandIsRecorder` is true only for an ffmpeg *writing* the capture, so a user's own conversion job reading it is not mistaken for the recorder. `claimStop(globalPath)` renames the state file so two hotkey presses cannot both stop and both deliver, and `sweepVoiceDir(globalPath, keep, nowMs)` removes only zero-byte captures and stale breadcrumbs, never audio. `WELL_KNOWN_BIN_DIRS`, `locateTool`, `locateToolSync` and the `LocateOptions` type are **defined in `src/util/which.ts`** and re-exported here, because this is the import path the voice pipeline and the tests already use; `cmd-doctor.ts` imports `locateToolSync` from `util/which.js` directly. Semantics are in the `src/util/which.ts` section below. `locateWhisperCli(opts?)`: `LEXICON_WHISPER_BIN` (any name, e.g. `main`) else `WHISPER_BIN_NAMES` (`whisper-cli`, `whisper-cpp`); `locateFfmpeg(opts?)`: `LEXICON_FFMPEG_BIN` else `ffmpeg`. `INSTALL_HINTS` / `installHint(platform, missing?: { ffmpeg: boolean; whisper: boolean })`: `brew install ffmpeg whisper-cpp`, apt + build whisper.cpp, winget (`INSTALL_HINTS`); when only one tool is missing, that tool's line alone (`INSTALL_HINTS_BY_TOOL`). `missing` defaults to both.
- `src/voice/devices.ts`: `AudioDevice { index; name; id; backend: 'avfoundation' | 'pulse' | 'alsa' | 'dshow' }`. Pure parsers `parseAvfoundationDevices(stderr)` (only the block after "AVFoundation audio devices:"), `parseDshowDevices(stderr)` (`"Name" (audio)` lines, older header form too), `parsePulseSources(stdout)` (`ffmpeg -sources pulse`; `.monitor` sources skipped), `parseArecordList(stdout)` (`card N: ... device M:` -> `hw:N,M`, numbered by position in the list as pulse and dshow are). `pickDevice(devices, selector)`: index, exact name or id, case-insensitive name, then substring. `listAudioDevices({ platform; ffmpeg; exec })` runs `ffmpeg -f avfoundation -list_devices true -i ""` (darwin), `ffmpeg -f dshow -list_devices true -i dummy` (win32), `ffmpeg -sources pulse` then `arecord -l` (elsewhere); ffmpeg's non-zero exit after listing is ignored. `formatDeviceList(devices)`.
- `src/voice/models.ts`: `DEFAULT_MODEL = 'base.en'`, `MODEL_BASE_URL` (`https://huggingface.co/ggerganov/whisper.cpp/resolve/main`), `REPO_MODELS_DIR: string | undefined` (`<package root>/bench/audio/models`, the root found by `findPackageRoot` from `src/util/package.ts`, so the plugin bundle one level below the root resolves it inside the package too; undefined when no root is found), `modelFileName(name)` = `ggml-<name>.bin`, `modelUrl(name)`, `modelsDir(globalPath, env?)` = `LEXICON_WHISPER_MODELS` with a leading `~/` expanded (`expandTilde`), or `<dirname(globalPath)>/models`; a value that is still not absolute throws (`LEXICON_WHISPER_MODELS must be an absolute path or start with ~/ ...`), so `resolveModel` and `ensureModel` throw it for a name, `lexicon voice` refuses it before recording and `lexicon doctor` warns. `isModelPath(spec)` (ends in `.bin`, contains a separator, starts with `.` or `~`). `KNOWN_MODELS` (the names a refusal suggests) and `isWhisperModelName(name): boolean`: the shape of a name whisper.cpp publishes (`tiny`, `base`, `small` or `medium` with an optional `.en`, or `large-v<N>` with an optional `-turbo`, then an optional `-q<d>_<d>`); `lexicon voice` refuses a `--model` name that fails it and has no file in the models dir or a fallback dir before recording (`there is no whisper model named <name>`), so a model file of the user's own named in the models dir still works. `resolveModel(spec, opts: ResolveModelOptions { globalPath; env?; fallbackDirs?; exists?; inspect? }): ResolvedModel { name; path; present; problem?; foundIn?; url? }`: a path is used as-is; a name is looked up in the models dir, then `fallbackDirs` (default `[REPO_MODELS_DIR]`, or none when it is undefined). Each candidate goes through `inspect` (default `inspectModelFile(p)`, exported: `'missing'` for no regular file, `the file is empty`, `it is not a ggml whisper model (...)` when the first 4 bytes are not `GGML_MAGIC`, undefined for a model; with only `exists` injected, existence alone); a broken fallback is passed over, and a broken file at the models dir or at an explicit path gives `present: false` with `problem`. The default `inspect` then runs `checkModelHashRecord(p)` (exported): beside each model `defaultDownloader` writes `<model>.sha256.json` (`modelHashPath`, `MODEL_HASH_SUFFIX`; `{ version: MODEL_HASH_VERSION (1), sha256, size, mtimeMs }`, written only while the file is still the inode it renamed, `writeModelHashRecord(path, sha256, ino?)`), and a model whose size or mtime differs from its record is hashed again (`sha256OfFile`): a different sha256, or a different size, is `it changed since Lexicon downloaded it (...); delete it to download it again, or delete <record> to keep using it as it is` with `modified: true`, a match under a new mtime rewrites the record, and a file with no record (one Lexicon did not download) is not checked. No table of known-good hashes ships. `defaultDownloader` hashes the stream and refuses a download whose sha256 differs from `claimedSha256(headers)` (exported: the 64-hex `X-Linked-Etag` or `ETag` Hugging Face sends for an LFS file), keeping nothing. A `modified` model is never downloaded over: `ensureModel` throws `whisper model <path> is not usable: <problem>` and `lexicon voice` refuses it before recording. Otherwise `ensureModel` downloads a named model again over such a file (`whisper model <name> at <path> is not usable (<problem>); fetching <url> again`) and throws `whisper model <path> is not usable: <problem>` for an explicit path, which `lexicon voice` reports before recording; `lexicon doctor` warns. `ensureModel(spec, opts: EnsureModelOptions { download?: Downloader; log?; progress? })` downloads a missing named model (`Downloader = (url, dest, onProgress: (DownloadProgress { received; total? }) => void, signal?: AbortSignal) => Promise<void>`; `defaultDownloader` first removes `<dest>`'s part files untouched for `STALE_PART_MS` (10 minutes; `sweepStaleParts(dest, nowMs?)`, exported), then uses `fetch` with `Accept-Encoding: identity`, streams to a part file of its own (`<dest>.<pid>-<time>-<random>.part`, so two downloads of one model never share one), and renames only when `checkDownloadedModel(head, received, total)` (exported) passes: as many bytes as the Content-Length said, when there was one and the response has no `Content-Encoding` other than `identity` (fetch decodes a compressed body while the header counts encoded bytes, so then the length is neither checked nor shown as progress), and a file starting with `GGML_MAGIC` (`lmgg`, exported); otherwise the part file is removed and the reason thrown; a download that fails is rethrown as `could not download whisper model <name>: <why>; ...` naming the network, the URL to save as the destination by hand and `--model <file>`) and throws for a missing explicit path. `formatProgress(name, p)`.
- `src/voice/recorder.ts`: `voiceDir(globalPath)` = `<dirname(globalPath)>/voice`; `stateFilePath(globalPath): string` = `<voiceDir>/recording.json`; `RecordingState { pid; wav; startedAt }` with `readState` (undefined for missing/malformed, including a `pid` that is not a positive safe integer, so a damaged `-1` or `0` never reaches `process.kill`), `writeState` (atomic), `clearState`. `resolveInput({ platform; ffmpeg; exec; device?; devices? }): InputSpec { format; input }`: darwin `avfoundation` `:default` / `:N` / `:<name>`; linux `pulse` `default` when `ffmpeg -devices` lists pulse, else `alsa` `default`; win32 `dshow` `audio=<name>` (first listed device when `--device` is absent; throws when none). On linux and win32 a numeric `--device` that no listed device has throws (naming the range and `--list-devices`) instead of falling back to `default` or passing the number on as a name; on linux with pulse, a device picked from the arecord list goes to the `alsa` demuxer. `ffmpegRecordArgs({ input; wav; seconds? })` = `-nostdin -hide_banner -loglevel error -f <format> -i <input> [-t <s>] -ac 1 -ar 16000 -c:a pcm_s16le -flush_packets 1 -y <wav>`. `-flush_packets 1` is what makes an abruptly terminated capture survive: without it ffmpeg's 256 KB output buffer meant a kill under about eight seconds left a zero-byte file. `startRecorder({ ffmpeg; spawn; input; wav; seconds?; detached; logFile? })`. `stopRecorder({ pid; isAlive; kill; sleep?; graceMs?; alreadySignalled?; platform? }): StopResult { finalized; killed; abrupt }`: SIGINT (skipped when `alreadySignalled`, i.e. Ctrl-C already reached ffmpeg), poll until the pid is gone or `STOP_GRACE_MS` (3000) elapses, then SIGKILL. On win32 it goes straight to SIGKILL and reports `finalized: false, abrupt: true`, because Node maps every signal to `TerminateProcess` and a detached recorder stopped by another process shares no console, so no real interrupt can be delivered. `MAX_TOGGLE_SECONDS = 600` (a toggle recording passes `-t`, so it always self-terminates at ten minutes). `PROVISIONAL_TTL_MS = 5000`, `isProvisional(state)`, `isStaleProvisional(state, nowMs)`. `recorderFingerprint(wav)` is the capture's basename, which carries a timestamp and appears in ffmpeg's own command line; `commandIsRecorder(command, wav)` is true for an ffmpeg writing that capture. `confirmRecorder(pid, wav, describe): Promise<boolean | undefined>` answers whether a live pid really is our recorder, because a pid is unique only while its process lives and a recycled one would otherwise receive our stop signal; undefined means the question could not be answered and the caller assumes it is ours. `findOrphanRecorder(wav, list): Promise<number | undefined>` locates a recorder whose pid was never written down, which is a start that died between spawning ffmpeg and writing the state file. `inspectWav(wav): Promise<WavCheck { ok; reason?; audioBytes; finalized; bytes }>` parses RIFF/WAVE, walks the chunk list to `data` at real file offsets, recovers an odd chunk missing its pad byte, and trusts the bytes on disk when the sizes are still ffmpeg's `0xFFFFFFFF` placeholders, so an unfinalized capture reads back. `CAPTURE_BYTES_PER_SECOND` and `captureSeconds(audioBytes)` give a capture's real length from its bytes, which is what a recording that ended on its own is measured by.
- `src/voice/transcribe.ts`: `cleanTranscript(text)`: drops `[...]` tags always, `*...*` of one to three plain words always (`*laughs*`, `*door slams*`), `(...)` only when one to three words that look like a noise annotation (`(applause)`, `(soft music)`), collapses whitespace, trims. `whisperArgs({ modelPath; wav; outBase; lang; translate; prompt? })` = `-m <model> -f <wav> -l <lang> -nt -np -oj -of <outBase> [-tr] [--prompt <p>]`. `parseWhisperOutput(json?, stdout)` joins `transcription[].text`, falls back to stdout. `buildWhisperPrompt(lexicon)` = `exportLexicon(lexicon, 'whisper-prompt')`. `transcribeFile(wav, opts: TranscribeOptions { lexicon; whisperCli; model?; modelOptions: EnsureModelOptions; lang?; translate?; prompt?; exec?; tmpDir?; log?; platform?; signal?: AbortSignal; timeoutMs? }): Promise<TranscribeResult { raw; output; normalized: NormalizeResult; model: ResolvedModel; prompt?; ms: { transcribe; normalize } }>`: ensures the model, runs whisper-cli (`MissingToolError` on `ENOENT`, its hint `installHint(platform ?? process.platform, { ffmpeg: false, whisper: true })`, and `lexicon voice` passes its platform; a non-zero exit with no JSON throws `WhisperExitError { message; whisperCli; modelPath }` with the stderr tail), has whisper-cli write `<outBase>.json` into a fresh `lexicon-whisper-XXXXXX` directory (mode 0700, from `mkdtemp`) under `tmpDir` (created if absent), reads it and removes that directory with what is in it on every path, cleans, normalizes. whisper-cli runs with `timeoutMs` (default `whisperTimeoutMs(audioSeconds)` = `WHISPER_TIMEOUT_BASE_MS` (120000) + `WHISPER_TIMEOUT_PER_AUDIO_SECOND_MS` (10000) per second of the WAV, read from its size as 16 kHz mono s16); past it `WhisperTimeoutError extends WhisperExitError { timeoutMs; audioSeconds }` (`whisper-cli did not finish within <n> s on <m> s of audio, so it was stopped`), whose next step in `lexicon voice` names the rerun command and a smaller model. `signal` (Ctrl-C) is passed to the model download and to whisper-cli; once it aborts, `TranscribeInterruptedError` (`interrupted while transcribing`) is thrown (a whisper killed by a signal waits up to 200 ms for the abort, since the terminal's SIGINT reaches both at once).
- `src/voice/history.ts`: `HISTORY_MAX_LINES = 1000`, `historyPath(globalPath)` = `voice/history.jsonl`, `HistoryEntry { at; raw; output; model; ms: { record; transcribe; normalize } }`, `HISTORY_MAX_BYTES = 4 * 1024 * 1024`, `HISTORY_READ_MAX_BYTES = VOICE_HISTORY_READ_MAX_BYTES` (16 MiB, the bound `loadVoiceHistory` reads with; both functions read through `readRegularFileTail` only the file's last that many bytes, from the first whole line in them, and only a regular file; a larger file is never loaded whole), `HISTORY_LOCK_TIMEOUT_MS = 2000`, `appendHistory(globalPath, entry, max?)` (under `withFileLock` on the file, at most `HISTORY_LOCK_TIMEOUT_MS` of waiting: a missing file is an empty history, any other read error (or something other than a regular file) drops the entry and keeps the file; an atomic rewrite that drops lines that are not an entry (unparseable, truncated, or JSON without string `raw` and `output`), then keeps the newest `max` lines and, oldest first, at most `HISTORY_MAX_BYTES`, always keeping the new entry; an entry whose line is larger than `HISTORY_READ_MAX_BYTES`, which no reader would see, is not written and the file is kept; never throws). Read history through `loadVoiceHistory(globalPath)` from `src/core/index.js`, the same reader `lexicon suggest` uses.
- `src/voice/voice.ts`: `EXIT_OK = 0`, `EXIT_ERROR = 1`, `EXIT_MISSING_TOOL = 2`, `EXIT_NOTHING_HEARD = 3`. `VoiceOptions { cwd?; globalPath?; seconds?; device?; model?; lang?; translate?; prompt?; history?; copy?; paste?; json?; quiet? }`, `VoiceDeps { exec?; spawn?; platform?; env?; isAlive?; kill?; sleep?; download?; waitForStop?: () => Promise<'enter' | 'sigint'>; waitForInterrupt?: (signal: AbortSignal) => Promise<'sigint'>; waitForTranscribeInterrupt?: (signal: AbortSignal) => Promise<'sigint'>; clipboardWrite?; sendPaste?; exists?; extraBinDirs?; modelFallbackDirs?; tmpDir?; now?; clock? }`, `VoiceIO { stdout; stderr }`, `VoiceJsonResult { ok; raw; output; replacements; summary; model; seconds; ms: { record; transcribe; normalize } }` (`ok: true` when delivered), `VoiceJsonFailure { ok: false; error; nextStep?; code }` (`code` is the exit code). Under `json`, `runVoice`, `runVoiceToggle` and `runVoiceListDevices` leave exactly one document on stdout on every path: a non-zero exit with no other document prints `VoiceJsonFailure`, and an exception escaping the run is reported on stderr and as `VoiceJsonFailure` with code 1 instead of being thrown. The stderr lines and exit codes do not depend on `json`.
  - `runVoice(opts, io, deps?)`: locates both tools (exit 2 with `installHint` on stderr when either is missing, adding `(or set <VAR> to the binary)` only for a variable the hint does not already name; a `LEXICON_FFMPEG_BIN` or `LEXICON_WHISPER_BIN` that points nowhere is reported as `<VAR>=<value> does not exist` with `point <VAR> at the <tool> binary, or unset it to look on PATH (to install <tool>: <installHint for that tool>)`, not as missing from PATH), refuses an explicit `--model` path that is not there (`whisper model not found: <path>`, exit 1, nothing recorded; the toggle start does the same), loads the merged lexicon, resolves the input, records to `<tmpDir>/lexicon-voice-<pid>-<t>-<12 hex>.wav`, created new with mode 0600 (`createPrivateFile`, `O_CREAT | O_EXCL`, so a file or symlink already at the name is never used; another name is tried on EEXIST) (attached child; `--seconds` waits for ffmpeg to exit or for `waitForInterrupt` (default: SIGINT; Enter does not stop it, and the listener is removed once ffmpeg exits), and on Ctrl-C runs `stopRecorder` with `alreadySignalled` and transcribes what was recorded, otherwise `waitForStop` (default: Enter on stdin or SIGINT) then `stopRecorder`), fails with a Microphone-permission hint when no audio was written, transcribes, deletes the WAV and delivers. A whisper failure keeps the WAV and names it. A whisper-cli that exits non-zero gets the rerun command as its next step, quoted by core's `commandLine(words, platform)` (re-exported from voice.ts): POSIX single quotes, or on win32 PowerShell's (single quotes, `'` doubled, `& ` before a quoted command, introduced as `run in PowerShell`). Ctrl-C while transcribing (`waitForTranscribeInterrupt`, default SIGINT; the toggle's stop listens too) stops the download or whisper-cli and exits 1 with `interrupted while transcribing; the recording is kept at <wav>`.
  - `runVoiceToggle(opts, io, deps?)`: state file present and pid alive -> stop: `stopRecorder`, clear the state file, transcribe the WAV, deliver, delete the WAV (`ffmpeg` is not required for the stop half). A live pid is confirmed with `confirmRecorder` before it is signalled, and one that now belongs to another process is treated as a recorder that is gone. A stale provisional record is searched for an orphaned recorder with `findOrphanRecorder`, and when one is found it is stopped and its audio transcribed rather than left running to the end of its `-t`. Otherwise, if the state names a recorder that is gone, `recoverDeadRecorder` inspects the capture before anything is discarded: a ten-minute self-termination or a crash partway is transcribed and delivered like any other recording, measured by `captureSeconds` rather than by wall clock since `startedAt`. Only when nothing usable is there does it report the parser's reason, the tail of `voice/recorder.log` and the microphone-permission hint, and exit 1 rather than silently starting another doomed recording. `discardOrKeep` deletes only a 0-byte capture, which is the placeholder a start writes before ffmpeg runs; any file holding bytes is kept and named with its size. Otherwise -> start: both tools are located, ffmpeg is spawned detached with `-t min(seconds, 600)` and stderr to `voice/recorder.log`, `{ pid, wav: voice/recording-<iso>.wav, startedAt }` is written, `recording` is printed, exit 0. A press that only starts (or meets a start or stop already in flight) prints `recording` or `stopping`, or under `json` `{"status":"recording"}` / `{"status":"stopping"}`, so `--toggle --json` stdout is JSON on every path. Each press reads its own `opts`: the start records with `device` and `seconds` (and checks an explicit `model` path), the stop transcribes and delivers with `model`, `lang`, `translate`, `prompt`, `history`, `copy` and `paste`, and `json` shapes each press's output; nothing from the start press is stored in the state file.
  - `deliver(result, recordMs, seconds, opts, io, deps, r, loaded?)`: empty raw -> `(nothing heard)` (or the JSON result with the `VoiceJsonFailure` keys over it, `code: 3`) and exit 3, nothing recorded; otherwise history append (unless `history: false`), `recordHits` for the changed canonicals (best-effort, awaited; given `loaded`, the `parseSkipped: false` load the lexicon came from, which both modes pass, so neither lexicon file is read again for the count), a `N corrections: ...` status line on stderr (or, when nothing changed and `loaded` has no terms, `lexicon voice: nothing was corrected: <emptyLexiconHint(loaded)>`), then the text (or `jsonResult(...)`) on stdout; `copy`/`paste` write the clipboard via the daemon backend (`detectClipboardBackend` over the voice exec) or `clipboardWrite`; `paste` on darwin runs `osascript -e PASTE_APPLESCRIPT` (or `sendPaste`) with an Accessibility hint on failure; off darwin it prints "not supported yet" and behaves as `copy`. Clipboard or paste failures exit 1 after the text was already printed; under `json` the result is printed after the clipboard and paste steps instead, with the `VoiceJsonFailure` keys (`code: 1`) over it when one of them failed.
  - `runVoiceStatus(opts, io, deps?)`: `recording since <startedAt>` / `idle` (`--json`: `{ ok, recording, since?, pid? }`, `ok` equal to `recording`, so it follows the exit code), exit 0 when recording else 1. `runVoiceListDevices(opts, io, deps?)`: `[N] Name` lines (or JSON), exit 2 without ffmpeg.
- `src/cli/cmd-voice.ts`: `registerVoiceCommands(program, io)` adds `voice` (`--toggle`, `--status`, `--list-devices`, `--seconds <n>`, `--device <name|index>`, `--model <name|path>` default `base.en`, `--lang <code>` default `en`, `--translate`, `--no-prompt`, `--no-history`, `--copy`, `--paste`, `--json`, `--quiet`) and dispatches to the four runners above; the exit code becomes `process.exitCode`. Two of `--toggle`, `--status` and `--list-devices` are refused before any runner (`<flags> cannot be combined`, exit 1, `VoiceJsonFailure` under `--json`), and so is a recording-only flag given with `--status` or `--list-devices` (`--seconds`, `--device`, `--model`, `--lang`, `--translate`, `--no-prompt`, `--no-history`, `--copy`, `--paste`, read with `getOptionValueSource(...) === 'cli'` so the defaults do not count): `<flags> only applies to a recording; <mode> records nothing`, `nextStep: 'drop <flags>, or run lexicon voice without <mode> to record'`, exit 1, `VoiceJsonFailure` under `--json`; and a commander usage error under `voice --json` prints `{ ok: false, error, nextStep, code }`.
- `runDoctor` (commands.ts) adds warnings, never failures: `whisper-cli found: <path>` / `whisper-cli not found (...)`, `ffmpeg found` / `not found`, an info line `whisper model base.en present: <path>` or `absent (... downloads it to <path> on first run)`, and on darwin an info line about Microphone permission for the terminal or launcher.

## src/cli/prompt.ts
Dependency-free interactive helper over `node:readline`. No ANSI beyond bold/dim, and only when the output is a TTY.
- `Prompter { ask(question, opts?: { default? }): Promise<string>; confirm(question, def?): Promise<boolean>; choose<T>(question, choices: PromptChoice<T>[], opts?: { multi? }): Promise<T[]>; close(): void }`, `PromptChoice<T> = { label; value }`.
  - `ask`: Enter returns `opts.default` (or `''`). `confirm`: Enter returns `def` (default false), accepts y/yes/n/no.
  - `choose` single: numbered list, a number (Enter = first). Multi: every item starts selected, numbers toggle, `a` all, `n` none, Enter accepts. Empty choices resolve to `[]`.
  - A prompt whose input closes (EOF) rejects with `PromptClosedError`.
- `createPrompter(io?: PrompterIO { input?; output? }): Prompter` (defaults to process stdin/stdout; readline is created lazily).
- `isInteractive(): boolean` (`process.stdin.isTTY && process.stdout.isTTY`).
- `onlyStdoutRedirected(streams = process): boolean`: stdin is a terminal and stdout is not (`| tee log`, `> file`), so a person is there but nothing prompts; a command taking the defaults then says that stdout is why.
- `askKey(prompter, question, keys: readonly string[], def?, onUnknown?: (answer: string) => void): Promise<string>` first character of the line, lower-cased, repeated until it is one of `keys`; empty line returns `def` when given; `onUnknown` runs with the trimmed answer before each re-ask (`review`, `harvest -i` and `suggest --apply` reprint their key list with it).
- `splitList(input): string[]` comma-separated, trimmed, empties dropped, case-insensitive dedupe (first spelling wins).
- `PromptClosedError`. `styler` is imported from `./io.js`, not defined or re-exported here.

## src/cli/commands.ts
`commands.ts` is now mostly a facade. It still owns the term/normalize/harvest/stats handlers below, and re-exports the rest from the modules they were split into so that `commands.js` (the path `index.ts`, the MCP server and the tests already import) keeps working:

| re-exported from `commands.ts` | actually defined in |
| --- | --- |
| `IO`, `processIO`, `CommonOptions`, `renderTable`, `safe`, `safeLines`, `safeMessage`, `safePath`, `safeError`, `ERROR_LINE_MAX_CHARS`, `ERROR_MAX_LINES` | `src/cli/io.ts` |
| `HOOK_EVENTS`, `ClaudeSettings`, `HookCommand`, `HookGroup`, `IntegrationPaths`, `hookConfigFor`, `mergeHookIntoSettings`, `isInstalledLexiconHook`, `resolveIntegrationPaths`, `settingsHasLexiconHook`, `findInstalledLexiconPlugin` | `src/cli/claude-settings.ts` |
| `DoctorAudience`, `DoctorCheck`, `DoctorCliOptions`, `DoctorDeps`, `DoctorLevel`, `DoctorReport`, `DoctorReportOptions`, `checkLoginService`, `renderDoctorReport`, `runDoctor`, `runDoctorReport` | `src/cli/cmd-doctor.ts` |
| `CLAUDE_MD_SNIPPET`, `InstallClaudeDeps`, `InstallClaudeOptions`, `InstallOutcome`, `runInstallClaude` | `src/cli/cmd-install.ts` |

- Still defined here: `readStdin()` (all of stdin as UTF-8, unbounded; `normalize` reads it) and the handlers below. `renderTable(rows, header?, opts?)` takes an optional `header` (singular) and `opts.pathColumns` (0-based column indexes whose cells are file paths: `safePath(p)`, sanitized like `safe`, line breaks included, but capped at `ERROR_LINE_MAX_CHARS` instead of 200, so a deep path keeps its file name; `lexicon stats` files, `harvest` evidence and `trust --list` paths use it).
- Write previews: `init`, `add`, `remove`, `learn`, `untrust`, built-in `pack add`, `harvest`, `export`, `review` and `edit` accept `--dry-run`. Mutating previews keep the normal validation and project trust gates and report planned changes without persisting them. `loadLexicon({ dryRun: true })` reads current content even with `staleGlobal`, and neither writes parse caches or cached failures nor promotes project spill entries. The `init`, `add`, `remove`, `learn`, `untrust` and built-in `pack add` previews preserve existing files and create no lexicon, trust or cache files. `export --dry-run --out <path>` reports the destination without creating it. `review --dry-run` still asks about terms; `edit --dry-run` reports the target before launching any editor, creating files or updating trust/caches. Project `init`, including a dry run, refuses an unusable trust registry before creating a new project file. JSON success results carry `dryRun: true` when supported; `review` and `edit` keep their existing JSON refusal.

  | Command | Dry-run support and limits |
  | --- | --- |
  | `init` | Supported for global and `--project`: reports whether the file would be created. A new project reports the trust it would receive; an existing file reports its current trust and is not newly trusted. |
  | `add` | Supported for global and `--project`. For a change, text output says `would create` or `would merge`, gives the canonical, scope and path, states `nothing was written`, and lists the resulting aliases; an unchanged term instead uses the usual unchanged note and aliases line. Project text output also says a real run re-pins trust. With `--json`, the usual result contains the planned `term`, `path`, `created`, optional `unchanged`, `scope`, `suggested` and any conflict or dropped-alias details, plus `dryRun: true`. No lexicon, trust registry, cache or other persistent state is changed. `--interactive` still prompts, and an untrusted existing project file is refused. |
  | `remove` | Supported for whole terms and `--alias`, including `--global` and `--project`. A missing target still fails; project trust restrictions and warnings about copies in other files still apply. |
  | `learn` | Supported for positional corrections and `--from`, with the usual scope selection or `--project`: reports the term and whether an alias would be added. Invalid corrections and untrusted project writes are still refused. |
  | `untrust` | Supported: reports the proposed trust change, whether a registry entry would be removed or there is nothing to remove. The change is pending only: any existing pin remains in the registry and no change is persisted; a real `lexicon untrust` is needed to apply it. JSON reports `removed` as the proposed result and includes `dryRun: true`. |
  | `pack add` (built-in) | Supported for global and `--project`: reports added and merged counts or an already-installed result. Each name in a multi-pack preview is computed against the current files independently, so it does not include effects of earlier names in that preview; a real run installs names in order. |

  These are previews of the current state, not reservations: another change before a real run can change its result, and a preview cannot establish that a later filesystem write will succeed. JSON successes include `dryRun: true` (built-in pack results are an array); some refusal paths return the usual error without that field.
- Handlers: `runInit(opts: InitOptions { project?; json?; dryRun? }, io)` (with `json`, prints `{ ok: true, scope, path, created }` plus `trust` (`isTrusted` of the file) for `project`, `created` false when the file was already there, and `nextStep` when an existing project file is `untrusted` or `changed` (`it is not trusted yet` or `it has changed since it was trusted`, then `, so its terms are not used; review it and run: lexicon trust`, also printed under the text `already exists` line); a failure throws to the CLI entry, which prints `{ ok: false, error, nextStep }` under `--json`), `runAdd(canonical, aliases, opts: AddOptions, io, prompter?)` (`--json` prints `{ ok: true, term, path, created, unchanged?, scope, suggested, conflicts?, note?, droppedAliases? }`, `droppedAliases: { alias, reason }[]` only when an alias given was the canonical in another case (`add Yod yod`), which `addTerm` drops since matching ignores case, with a `note: <alias> was not added as an alias of <canonical>: it differs from the canonical only in case, and matching already ignores case` line in text; `suggested` the generated aliases this call applied: with `--suggest`, or with no aliases when it created the term, else `[]`; after `addTerm`, `spellingConflicts` of the reloaded lexicon: one `warn:` line per alias another term also holds, with the `lexicon remove --alias` command, and `conflicts` plus `note` (`conflictNote`: one sentence per conflict, naming `lexicon remove <canonical> --alias <alias> --<scope>` (each word shell-quoted, `conflictRemoveCommand`) for an alias) under `--json` only when there is one, as MCP `add_term` reports it; when `addTerm` reports `unchanged` the line is `unchangedTermNote(canonical)` with `(<scope>: <path>)` in place of `merged ...`, and `--json` adds `unchanged: true` and that `note` when there is no conflict; the `warn:` line names the same command), `runRemove(canonical, opts: RemoveOptions { project?; global?; json?; alias? }, io)` (`project` and `global` each limit the edit to that file, and together exit 1; without `alias`: `removeTerm`, then `removed <canonical> from <path>` plus one `warn:` line per copy still in the other file (`termHolders` read before the edit) naming the `lexicon remove ... --project` or `--global` command that removes only it, and one `warn:` line when the skipped project file holds the term (`untrustedTermHolder`, path only; that file is never edited); `--json` prints `{ ok: true, canonical, removed, stillIn?, untrustedProject? }` (`canonical`, and the `removed` line, the spelling the file held, not the one typed) (a miss, exit 1, leads with `ok: false` and `error: 'term "<canonical>" not found[ in <scope> lexicon]'`, then `nextStep`: the flag for the other file when it holds the term, else, when the merged lexicon has no terms, `the lexicon has no terms yet, so there is nothing to remove; run lexicon setup, or add one with: lexicon add <canonical> <alias>` (or, when a skipped project file is the only one with terms, a `lexicon trust` step), else `run lexicon list to see the exact canonical spellings` unless only the untrusted project file has it; the same line goes to stderr), `stillIn: { scope, path }[]` only when a copy is left and `untrustedProject: { path, trust }` only when the skipped file has one; `--project` or `--global` on a term only the other file holds adds a stderr line naming the flag that reaches it; a `ProjectTrustError` (the untrusted project copy comes first without a scope) prints the message, a `lexicon remove <canonical> --global` hint when the global file holds the term too, and under `--json` `{ ok: false, error, canonical, removed: false, untrustedProject? }`, and returns 1; with `alias`, `remove <canonical> --alias <alias>`, it calls `removeAlias` and prints the aliases kept, or under `--json` `{ ok: true, canonical, alias, removed: true, aliases, paths, untrustedProject? }`; `untrustedProject` (and a `warn:` line) when the skipped project file's copy of the term holds the alias (`untrustedAliasHolder`, read before the edit, path only; that file is never edited); an unknown term or alias prints `lexicon: <message>` to stderr (plus a line naming the untrusted copy when there is one), plus `{ ok: false, error, nextStep, canonical, alias, removed: false, untrustedProject? }` under `--json` (`nextStep` the generic `DEFAULT_NEXT_STEP` when nothing more specific applies), and returns 1; with `--project` or `--global` naming a file whose copy lacks the term or the alias while the other loaded file's copy has it, a second stderr line and `nextStep` name that file and the flag (`it is an alias of "<canonical>" in the <scope> lexicon (<path>); run with --<scope> ...`); otherwise, when the merged lexicon has no terms, the same empty-lexicon step as without `alias` (`lexicon setup` / `lexicon add`, or the `lexicon trust` step) is that second line and `nextStep`; otherwise that second line and `nextStep` name the term's aliases (`the aliases of "<canonical>" are: <a>, <b>; remove one with lexicon remove <canonical> --alias <alias>`, or that it has none and the command that removes the term), or, when no file in reach holds the term, `run lexicon list --query <canonical> to see the exact spellings of the terms`; a `ProjectTrustError` (the untrusted project copy holds the alias and comes first without a scope) is reported the same way, plus a `lexicon remove <canonical> --alias <alias> --global` hint when the global copy holds the alias, and returns 1), `runList(opts: ListOptions { json?; full?; category?; query?; offset?; limit? }, io)` (`offset` (`--offset`, a whole number, default 0) and `limit` (`--limit`, a positive integer, default every term) take a page of the matching terms with `pageTermList`, in every output mode, and are checked before the lexicon is read; `--full` then adds `page`, as `list_terms` does, the text table closes with `terms <first> to <last> of <matched>[; the next page is --offset <n>]`, and an offset at or past the last match prints `offsetPastEndNote` (as `note` under `--full`); `--json` prints the filtered `Term[]` as a bare array, unchanged; `--full` (implies `--json`) prints `ok: true` and then the object MCP `list_terms` returns, `{ ok: true, terms, counts: { matched, total, global, project }, paths: { global, project? }, projectTrust?, skippedProject?, note? }`, with the CLI's `skippedProjectJson` note; with no project file skipped and nothing matched, `note` says why, as `list_terms` does: the lexicon has no terms yet (naming `lexicon add` and `lexicon setup`), or `No term matches --category <c> [--query "<q>"]; the lexicon has N terms in all, which lexicon list without it shows.` (`noTermMatchesNote`); the text output says the same, `no terms yet. Try: ...` for an empty lexicon whatever the filters, the same `noTermMatchesNote` sentence otherwise), `runNormalize` (`buildNormalizeOptions`; `--json` prints `ok: true` and the `NormalizeResult` plus the additive `example: true` when the built-in example terms stood in for an empty lexicon and `projectTrust`, `skippedProject`, `note` (`skippedProjectJson`) when a project file was skipped; the `diffSummary` goes to stderr under `diff` (with or without `json`) and under `dryRun` without `json`; on a lexicon load error it passes stdout through byte-exactly, warns twice on stderr and returns 1; under `json` stdout is `{ ok: false, error, nextStep, input, output, replacements: [], changed: false }`, the input unchanged, `nextStep` the `unreadableLexiconHint` or `lexicon doctor`; a `--min-confidence` that `buildNormalizeOptions` rejects is refused before stdin is read, exit 1, with `lexicon: <error>` and a nextStep line on stderr and, under `json`, `{ ok: false, error, nextStep }` on stdout; empty text (empty stdin, or an empty argument) exits 0 with empty stdout, or `{ ok: true, input: '', output: '', replacements: [], changed: false }` under `json`, and a `lexicon: no text to correct (stdin was empty)` line on stderr naming both ways to pass text), `runHarvest(root, opts: HarvestCliOptions, io, deps?: HarvestDeps { isInteractive?; createPrompter? })`, `runExport`, `runPath(opts: PathOptions { cwd?; json? }, io)` (with `json`, prints `{ ok: true, global, project }`, `project` null when the walk found none), `runDoctor(opts: DoctorCliOptions { cwd?; json? }, io, deps?: DoctorDeps)` = `renderDoctorReport(await runDoctorReport(opts, deps), io)` (with `json`, prints the report as `JSON.stringify(report, null, 2)` instead, the object MCP `lexicon_doctor` returns, with the same exit code: 1 when any check fails), `runDoctorReport(opts: DoctorReportOptions { cwd?; audience?: DoctorAudience }, deps?): Promise<DoctorReport>` (the checks as data, no output; `audience` `'cli'` (default) or `'mcp'` picks how `nextStep` is phrased: `'mcp'` names `setup_lexicon`, `install_client` and `trust_project`, and rewrites every check's `lexicon ...` hint (`(run: lexicon ...)`, `then run: lexicon trust`, `run lexicon trust again`, `keeps the local API up: lexicon serve --install`) to the tool (`install_client`, `setup_lexicon`, `trust_project`) or the `cliCommand` npx form, in `checks` as in `nextStep` and `summary`; `checks` holds the same checks in the same order either way, and `'cli'` leaves their messages as `lexicon doctor` prints them), `renderDoctorReport(report, io): number` (one line per check, blank line, verdict; returns the exit code), `runInstallClaude(opts: InstallClaudeOptions, io, deps?: InstallClaudeDeps)`.
- `DoctorLevel = 'ok'|'fail'|'warn'|'info'`, `DoctorCheck { level, message }` (message unsanitized; renderers apply `safeMessage`), `DoctorReport { ok (no `fail`), ready (terms plus declared integration), summary, nextStep, error? (the first `fail` check's message, present exactly when `ok` is false), checks: DoctorCheck[], paths: { global, project?, trust (getTrustPath), settings, projectSettings? (`<cwd>/.claude/settings.json`, always set by `runDoctorReport`), projectSettingsFiles? (every repo-level settings file read for the hooks, `projectSettingsCandidates(cwd)` or `[DoctorDeps.projectSettingsPath]`), installedPlugins }, versions: { lexicon (package.json two levels up, else '0.0.0'), node (process.version), platform } }`. `DoctorDeps { platform?; env?; exec?; settingsPath?; projectSettingsPath?; installedPluginsPath?; home?; uid? }`. Doctor also reads the user-level MCP config of codex, cursor, windsurf, gemini, claude-desktop and vscode (`configPathFor` under `home`); inspection is limited to stable regular files of at most 1 MiB, with file symlinks, unsafe replacements, invalid UTF-8 and oversized files reported as unconfirmed. Missing configs stay normal absence. Errors report fixed reasons, not file contents or private home paths; `registersLexicon(client, text)` (exported from cmd-doctor.ts) requires a non-disabled `lexicon` transport declaration: a nonblank stdio command with optional string arguments, or a nonblank remote URL. Malformed maps/entries and conflicting transport fields are not registration evidence. A registration found there marks the setup wired, and the `claude mcp list` miss becomes `warn` instead of `fail`. `claude mcp list` counts as registered only when a line names a server called exactly `lexicon` (`lexicon: ...`, or a plugin's `plugin:<plugin>:lexicon: ...`), not any output holding the word. The state files beside the global lexicon each add one `warn` check (never a `fail`) when they exist but cannot be used, naming the problem, its cost, the fix and the path: `serve.json` (`readServeConfigResult` status `unusable`, its `reason`), `hits.json` (`hitsRegistryProblem`), `pending-aliases.yaml` (`readPending` throws) and `index-cache.json` (`indexCacheProblem`); a missing file adds nothing. The trust registry is read with the doctor's own `cwd`, `env` and `home` (`paths.trust` and the project file's `isTrusted` alike); one that cannot be used (`trustRegistryProblem`) is a `fail` check, `trust registry <problem>, so every project lexicon reads as untrusted and trusting one fails (fix the JSON in it, or move it aside and trust each project again): <path>`, and with an untrusted project file the verdict says so (`The trust registry <problem>, so this repo's project lexicon reads as untrusted and is not merged.`) and its `nextStep` is to fix or move that file, then `lexicon trust` (`trust_project` for `mcp`), in place of a trust step that would be refused. `list`, `normalize` and `doctor` warn on stderr when a project lexicon was skipped.
- `checkLoginService({ platform, env, exec, home, uid? }): Promise<DoctorCheck>` (one check, appended by `runDoctorReport` before the clipboard check): darwin runs `launchctl print gui/<uid>/<serveLabel(env)>` (loaded when it does not throw) and reads `launchAgentPath(home, env)` for `programPathFromPlist`; linux runs `systemctl --user is-active lexicon-serve.service` (loaded when stdout is `active`) and reads `systemdUnitPath(home, env)` for `programPathFromUnit`. Neither loaded nor a file -> `info` `no login service installed (optional; keeps the local API up: lexicon serve --install)`; a program path that does not exist -> `fail` `login service <name> points at a missing file: <path> (run: lexicon serve --uninstall && lexicon serve --install)` (checked before the loaded state: a crash-looping service is the actionable problem); a service file that is there but cannot be read (EACCES, EISDIR; anything but ENOENT or ENOTDIR) -> `warn` `login service <name> file cannot be read (<code>), so the program it runs was not checked (run: lexicon serve --uninstall && lexicon serve --install): <file>`, loaded or not; file present but not loaded -> `warn` `... is installed at <file> but not loaded (...)`; loaded -> `ok` `login service <name> loaded (<path> exists)`. Other platforms -> `info` `login service is not automated on <platform> (...)`.
- `runAdd` with `-i` (`AddOptions.interactive`): when no aliases were given the suggestions become a multi-select checklist followed by `more aliases (comma-separated, Enter for none)`; then `phonetic hint` (default `--phonetic`, empty = none) and `category` (default `--category` or `other`, re-asked until valid). ctrl-D at any of these prompts (`PromptClosedError`) writes nothing, prints `input closed before <canonical> was saved; nothing was added` and returns 0. Without a prompter and not on a TTY throws `add --interactive needs a terminal ...`. `AddOptions.globalPath` is a test hook.
- `runHarvest`: a root that does not exist, or exists and is not a directory (`harvest: not a directory: <path>`, next step `pass a directory (the repository root, not a file in it), or leave the path out ...`), is refused through `refuse` before anything is scanned, exit 1. `--interactive`, or `--add` on a TTY without `--yes`/`--json`, hands the candidates to `runHarvestInteractive` (cmd-review.ts); otherwise prints the table and, with `--add`, adds every candidate. `--interactive` off a TTY throws `harvest --interactive needs a terminal ...`; `--interactive` + `--json` is rejected. `--add` without the walk checks the trust gate before scanning: an untrusted (or changed) project `.lexicon.yaml` prints the `ProjectTrustError` message on stderr and, under `--json`, `{ ok: false, error, added: false, path, projectTrust }` on stdout, and returns 1 with no candidates printed. The add honours `globalPath` like the walk does. `--json` prints the bare candidate array (with `--add`, after the writes) and the `added N new terms, merged M in <path>` summary on stderr; stdout carries exactly one JSON document in every outcome, so a write that throws prints only `{ ok: false, error, nextStep?, added, created, merged, path? }` (the candidates go in one `addTerms` batch, which writes all or none, so the counts are 0), the error and `nothing was added` on stderr, and returns 1 (under `--full` too, instead of the object). Without `--json` a write that throws prints the same stderr lines (the error, its hint, `nothing was added`) and returns 1; a `ProjectTrustError` from the write is rethrown for `fail` to print with its next step. A scan cut at `HARVEST_MAX_FILES` adds a `warn: <harvestTruncatedNote(root)>` line (on stderr as `lexicon: ...` under `--json`). `--full` (implies `--json`) instead prints one object after the add: `{ ok: true, root, candidates, count, truncated?, note? }` (`truncated: true` and `note`, `harvestTruncatedNote(root)`, only for a cut scan), `ok` first and then the keys MCP `harvest_repo` returns, plus with `--add` `added` (created + merged, as `harvest_repo` counts it), `created`, `merged` and `path` (absent when nothing was written); the stderr summary is printed too.
- `runDoctor`: three tiers, `✓` ok, `!` warn, `✗` fail (exit 1), `·` info. Checks both lexicon files, trust status, term conflicts, the Claude Code wiring and the clipboard backend. `DoctorDeps` gains `settingsPath?` (default `~/.claude/settings.json`) and `installedPluginsPath?` (default `~/.claude/plugins/installed_plugins.json`). `findInstalledLexiconPlugin(settings, installedPlugins): string | undefined` (a `lexicon@<marketplace>` key under `installed_plugins.json` `plugins`, or a true `enabledPlugins` entry) -> `✓ lexicon plugin installed as <id>`; otherwise per event `settingsHasLexiconHook(settings, event)` (a hook command matching `user-prompt-submit.js`, `plugin/hook.mjs` or `lexicon`) over the user settings.json and the repo-level files `projectSettingsCandidates(cwd): string[]` names (exported from cmd-doctor.ts): `.claude/settings.json` and `.claude/settings.local.json` in `<cwd>` and, when `<cwd>` is below it, at the git root (`DoctorDeps.projectSettingsPath`, when set, is the only one read; `install claude --scope project` writes the shared settings.json; a file that resolves to the user settings.json is not read twice, and each one's `enabledPlugins` also counts for the plugin check) -> `✓ <event> hook found in <path> (user scope)`, `(project scope)` for a settings.json or `(local scope)` for a settings.local.json, or the warning `! <event> hook not found in <settings> (user scope), <file> (project scope), ... or <file> (local scope) (fine if you use the plugin; otherwise run: lexicon install claude --apply)`, naming every file read. An unparseable settings.json is a warning. When the plugin is installed, a missing `claude mcp list` entry is a warning instead of a failure. A missing clipboard backend is a warning.
- Claude Code integration helpers: `HOOK_EVENTS: readonly string[] = ['UserPromptSubmit', 'SessionStart']`; `resolveIntegrationPaths(cliDir?): IntegrationPaths { server; hook; bundled }` returns `<root>/plugin/mcp-server.mjs` + `<root>/plugin/hook.mjs` when both exist (root = `cliDir/../..`; without `cliDir`, `cliDir` = `<findPackageRoot(import.meta.url)>/dist/cli`, falling back to this module's directory), else `dist/mcp/server.js` + `dist/hooks/user-prompt-submit.js`; `InstallOutcome = 'created' | 'updated' | 'unchanged'`; `InstallClaudeDeps { cliDir?; settingsPath?; exec?; onWritten?: (file, outcome) => void; env?; installedPluginsPath? }` (`onWritten` reports settings.json under `--apply`; `installedPluginsPath` defaults to `plugins/installed_plugins.json` beside `settingsPath`); `hookConfigFor(command, timeout = 5, events = ['UserPromptSubmit'])`, `mergeHookIntoSettings(settings, command, timeout = 5, events = ['UserPromptSubmit'])` (one group per event; an event that already has the identical command is skipped; `changed` is true when any event was added); `CLAUDE_MD_SNIPPET`. `runInstallClaude` at user scope, when `findInstalledLexiconPlugin` finds the plugin in settings.json or installed_plugins.json, prints one line (the plugin already provides the MCP server and hooks, nothing was written or planned, `claude plugin uninstall <id>` to wire it by hand instead) and returns 0 without running `claude` or touching settings.json; otherwise it prints the three steps and, with `--apply`, runs `claude mcp add --scope <user|project> lexicon -- node "<server>"` and merges hooks for every `HOOK_EVENTS` entry (paths quoted) into `claudeSettingsPathFor(scope, cwd, env = process.env): string` unless `InstallClaudeDeps.settingsPath` is given: `<claudeConfigDir(env)>/settings.json` at user scope, `<cwd>/.claude/settings.json` (the repo's shared settings) at project scope, which the step 2 line names as project scope. Under `--apply`, before step 1 runs `claude`, a settings.json that does not parse throws `could not read <file>: ...` and one that parses to something other than an object (`[]`, `null`, a string) throws a `CliRefusal` (`<file> holds <kind>, not a JSON object, so the hooks cannot be added to it; nothing was changed`); nothing is written. `env` is `InstallClaudeDeps.env` when given. `runInstall('claude', ...)` passes the `--home` settings path (`<claudeConfigDir(env, home)>/settings.json`, `env` being `InstallDeps.env` when given, as `lexicon uninstall` reads it) at user scope only. When `claude mcp add` answers that the server already exists, it runs `claude mcp get lexicon` and reads it with `parseClaudeMcpGet(out): ClaudeMcpRegistration { scope?: 'user'|'project'|'local'; command?; args? (joined by spaces) }`: the same launch prints `already registered with claude, nothing changed`; one at the same scope that `isInstalledLexiconServer(command, args): boolean` recognises (`node <dir>/plugin/mcp-server.mjs`, `node <dir>/mcp/server.js`, `npx -y @ashlr/lexicon[@v] mcp`) but with another command or path is replaced (`claude mcp remove --scope <scope> lexicon`, then the add again) and reported `repointed lexicon: <old> -> <new>`; one it does not recognise (left as it is, with the remove command to replace it) and one `claude mcp get` cannot show are left alone. `claude mcp get` has no `--scope` and shows the registration that takes precedence (local, then project, then user), so when it shows another scope the one at this scope is read from its own file with `readClaudeRegistrationAt(scope, files: ClaudeRegistrationFiles { userConfig; cwd }): Promise<ClaudeMcpRegistration | null | undefined>` (user: `mcpServers.lexicon` of `claudeUserConfigPath(env?, home?)`, `$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR/.claude.json` or `~/.claude.json`; project: `<cwd>/.mcp.json`, the file `claudeRegistrationFile(scope, files)` names; null when there is none, undefined when the file cannot be read) and compared, repointed or kept as above, with a note that the other scope's registration takes precedence; `InstallClaudeDeps.claudeUserConfig` and `UninstallDeps.claudeUserConfig` override the user file. `lexicon uninstall claude` removes the one at its scope the same way, and an unreadable file is a failure (exit 1) naming the `claude mcp remove` to run. `isInstalledLexiconServer` and `parseClaudeMcpGet` live in claude-settings.ts. `editJsonText` (src/util/jsonc.ts) deletes a removed key or array element in place (with its line, or its comma on a shared line), keeping the comments around it. A failed remove or re-add exits 1 and, after a remove, names the add to run.

## src/cli/io.ts
Everything the CLI prints goes through here, so a handler is testable by passing a sink and terminal-safety is applied in one place.
- `IO { stdout(s); stderr(s) }`, `processIO`, `line(io, s?)`, `CommonOptions { cwd? }`, `resolveCwd(opts)`.
- `Styler { bold; dim; red; green; yellow }`, `styler(output = process.stdout, env = process.env): Styler` (colour off when not a TTY, or under `NO_COLOR`), and the ready-made `const { bold, dim, red, green, yellow } = styler(process.stdout)`.
- `shownCommand(words, platform = process.platform): string`: a command composed for the user to copy, `commandLine(words, platform)` sanitized like `safe` but never cut (a command shortened with an ellipsis cannot be run). `installApplyCommand`, `uninstallCommand`, `setupApplyCommand` and the `pack add` / `pack update` `--yes` command use it.
- `safe = sanitizeForDisplay` and `safeLines(s)`: everything derived from a lexicon file, a path or another tool's output is printed through one of these, so an ANSI escape or a bidi override in a repo's `.lexicon.yaml` cannot rewrite the terminal. `safeError(s)` prints another process's error message: non-blank lines, each sanitized with `sanitizeForDisplayMax(line, ERROR_LINE_MAX_CHARS)` (4096, so a file path is never cut), at most `ERROR_MAX_LINES` (12) then `  ... and N more lines`. `safeMessage(s)` sanitizes each line the same way and caps it at `ERROR_LINE_MAX_CHARS` too, keeping every line: `fail`, `refuse`, `pack add`'s failure line and the `lexicon doctor` renderer (checks, summary, next step) print through it, because those messages name the command to run after a path, and the 200-character cap of `safe` cut that command off in a deep directory.
- `plural(n, word)`, `indent(s, prefix)`, `tildify(p, home)`, `ensureNewline(s)`, `renderTable(rows, header?, opts?: { pathColumns? })`.
- `unknownCommandHint(name): string | undefined` the hint for an unknown command that is another tool's name for a lexicon command (`status` and `check` name `doctor`, `version` names `--version`, `config` names `path` and `edit`, `show` names `list`, `delete` names `remove`), or undefined to keep commander's spelling suggestion. The CLI entry prints it in place of commander's `(Did you mean ...?)`; the exit code and the `--json` usage document are unchanged.
- `positiveIntOption(flag, example, max?): (value: string) => number` is the commander parser of every positive integer flag (`harvest --limit/--min-count`, `export --limit`, `suggest --limit`, `daemon --interval`): a value that is not a positive whole number written in digits (so `5abc` and `1.5` too, which `parseInt` read as 5 and 1) throws `InvalidArgumentError('expected a positive integer, e.g. <flag> <example>')`, and one over `max` throws `expected a positive integer of at most <max>, e.g. <flag> <example>` (`harvest --limit` takes `HARVEST_MAX_LIMIT`, `export --limit` `EXPORT_MAX_LIMIT`, `suggest --limit` `SUGGEST_MAX_LIMIT`), so commander prints `option '--limit <n>' argument '0' is invalid. expected a positive integer, e.g. --limit 50`. The digits rule is `wholeNumber(value: string): number | undefined` (a string of digits, surrounding spaces aside, as a safe integer), which `runExport` and `runHarvest` also apply to a string `limit` / `minCount` handed to them directly (`<flag> must be a positive integer` otherwise, so `0` is refused there too; a number must be a positive safe integer; `--limit must be at most <max>` over the same maxima).
- `exampleAfterMissingArgument(cmd, example): Command` makes a commander command print `example` after a missing-argument usage error and `(use <command path> --help for usage)` after any other (an unknown option), in place of one fixed `showHelpAfterError` text. index.ts gives `add` and `remove` theirs, cmd-pack.ts `pack add`, `pack remove` and `pack show`; the stderr error line, the exit code and the `--json` usage document are unchanged.
