- `fail(io, err, json?: boolean): number` the one error reporter (prints `errorMessage(err)`, then the `unreadableLexiconHint(message)` line when there is one, returns 1; with `json` true it first prints `{ ok: false, error, nextStep }` on stdout, `nextStep` being that hint, else the error's own (a `CliRefusal`'s `nextStep`, such as an unknown `--category` naming the categories, or `PACK_NOT_FOUND_NEXT_STEP` for a `PackNotFoundError`; also printed as a second stderr line), `PROJECT_TRUST_NEXT_STEP` for a refused project write (`isProjectTrustError`), else `DEFAULT_NEXT_STEP`; `jsonError(error, nextStep?): JsonError { ok: false; error; nextStep: string }` fills an absent or empty `nextStep` with `DEFAULT_NEXT_STEP` (`'fix what error names, then run the command again (its --help lists the flags)'`), so every CLI `--json` failure document (from `fail`, `refuse`, `printJsonError` and the documents built on `jsonError`) carries all three keys; the generic step is not repeated on stderr; index.ts sends errors a handler throws through it too, with `json` set from the running command's `--json`, or its `--full` on `list` and `harvest`, which implies it (`optsRequestJson(opts)`), recorded by a `preAction` hook). `JsonError { ok: false; error; nextStep? }`, `jsonError(error, nextStep?): JsonError`, `printJsonError(io, error, nextStep?)` (stdout, pretty-printed), `jsonOk(doc): { ok: true } & doc` (the object-shaped `--json` success document, `ok` first, printed by `add`, `remove`, `list --full`, `path`, `learn`, `stats`, `import` and `import --guided`, `harvest --full`, `serve --show`, `--pair` and its listening line, `trust` (and `--list`) and `untrust`, `pending` (and `pending list`), and `pack list`, `remove`, `show`, `search` and a community `pack add` (preview and install); `pack update`, `pack validate`, `pending accept` and `pending reject` carry `ok` equal to exit code 0; array-shaped successes such as `list --json` and `harvest --json` stay bare arrays), and `refuse(io, json, error, nextStep?): number` for a refusal that is not an exception (the `lexicon: <error>` and `lexicon: <nextStep>` stderr lines, the `JsonError` on stdout under `json`, returns 1). A `--json` command whose failure already has a document keeps it: `remove` (`{ ok: false, error, canonical, removed: false, ... }`), `harvest --add` (`{ ok: false, error, added: false, ... }`), `normalize` (`{ ok: false, error, nextStep }` and the unchanged `NormalizeResult`), `doctor` (the report), `serve --status` (`{ ok: false, up: false, ... }`), `trust`/`untrust` (`{ ok: false, error, nextStep?, action, path? }`), `pack add` (a single ref's `{ ok: false, ref, error, nextStep? }`; for a list, the results array of the refs before the failure with `{ ref, ok: false, error, nextStep? }` appended), `pack update` (the report, `{ ok: false, updates }`) and `pack validate` (`{ ok: false, file, pack, errors, warnings }` for a pack that parses but fails the publish bar; a file that is missing or does not parse as a pack is `{ ok: false, error, nextStep }` from `fail`). `argvRequestsJson(args, flags = ['--json']): boolean` (one of `flags` before any `--`; `jsonFlagsOf(cmd)` is `['--json', '--full']` for a command that defines `--full`, else `['--json']`) and `usageErrorJson(message, commandPath): JsonError` (commander's message without `error: `, `nextStep: 'see the usage: <commandPath> --help'`): index.ts sets `exitOverride` on every command, remembers which one threw, and for a commander usage error (not help or version output) with `--json` (or that command's `--full`) on the command line prints that document on stdout, next to commander's unchanged stderr text and exit code. `unreadableLexiconEdit(message): string | undefined` the `lexicon edit` command for the file such a message names (`lexicon edit --project` for a `.lexicon.yaml`), which `unreadableLexiconHint` and the MCP server's `withLexiconFix` word around; `unreadableLexiconHint(message): string | undefined` names `lexicon edit` (`--project` for a `.lexicon.yaml`) and `lexicon doctor` for a store `Failed to parse lexicon YAML at` / `Invalid lexicon at` message; `warnSkippedProject(loaded, io)` the one "an untrusted project lexicon was not merged" notice. `skippedProjectJson(loaded, consequence?)` the matching additive `--json` keys `{ projectTrust, skippedProject, note }` (empty when nothing was skipped).

## src/cli/claude-settings.ts
Everything the CLI knows about Claude Code's own files, extracted from `commands.ts` and re-exported by it.
- `HOOK_EVENTS = ['UserPromptSubmit', 'SessionStart']`.
- `ClaudeSettings`, `HookCommand`, `HookGroup`, `hookConfigFor(...)`, `mergeHookIntoSettings(...)` (idempotent: a second install does not duplicate a hook, from the same location or another: a hook an earlier install wrote, `isInstalledLexiconHook(command)` (`node <dir>/plugin/hook.mjs` or `node <dir>/hooks/user-prompt-submit.js`, quoted or not, either separator, or `npx -y @ashlr/lexicon[@<version>] hook`; matched on the command's words, parsed as `launchCommandLine` quotes them, so a path holding an escaped `"` is recognised), is pointed at the new command with its timeout instead, any further copy of it is removed with its group when nothing else is left there, and an exact match keeps its timeout; a user's own hook is never touched), `settingsHasLexiconHook(settings, event)`.
- `findInstalledLexiconPlugin(settings, installedPlugins): string | undefined` so the CLI does not add a hook that the installed plugin already provides.
- `IntegrationPaths`, `resolveIntegrationPaths(cliDir?)`, `defaultExec(file, args, opts?: { cwd? })` (`lexicon install claude` and `lexicon uninstall claude` run `claude` with `cwd` = their `--cwd` directory, whose `.mcp.json` and local scope are the project's).

## src/cli/cmd-doctor.ts
`lexicon doctor`, extracted from `commands.ts` and re-exported by it.
- `DoctorLevel = 'ok' | 'fail' | 'warn' | 'info'`, `DoctorCheck`, `DoctorReport`, `DoctorDeps`.
- `DoctorAudience = 'cli' | 'mcp'`, `DoctorReportOptions`.
- `runDoctorReport(opts, deps?): Promise<DoctorReport>` (what the MCP `lexicon_doctor` tool calls, with `audience: 'mcp'`), `renderDoctorReport(report, io): number`, `runDoctor(opts, io, deps?): Promise<number>`, `checkLoginService(probe)`.
- Imports `locateToolSync` from `src/util/which.ts` directly, which is why doctor and `lexicon voice` now name the same binary.
- `LoginServiceProbe`, the parameter type of `checkLoginService`, is not exported; call it with an object literal.
- `DoctorDeps.home` supplies the default home for lexicon, client and service paths, including Claude settings and the plugin registry; explicit environment/path overrides remain effective. Tests inject complete scratch paths.
- Codex registration detection scans limited supported table/transport syntax, including conventional quoted keys, basic/literal strings and JSON-compatible argument arrays, while ignoring comments and multiline-string contents. It is not a full TOML document validator. `DoctorReport.ready` reports configured terms plus a declared integration; it does not prove a live client connection. Verify one dictated sentence in the client.

## src/cli/cmd-pack.ts
`lexicon pack`, the CLI over `src/core/packs.ts`.
- `PackCliOptions extends CommonOptions { json?; project?; globalPath? }`, `PackDeps` (the core functions, injectable for tests). `globalPath` names the global lexicon file every pack command reads and writes, vendored and community alike (`pack add` and `pack remove` of a vendored pack included).
- `runPackList`, `runPackAdd(names, ...)`, `runPackRemove(name, ...)`, `runPackShow(name, ...)`, each `(..., opts, io, deps?) => Promise<number>`. `runPackRemove --json` prints `{ ok: true, ...UninstallPackResult }`; when no file listed the pack (`files: []`, nothing written, exit 0 as in text mode, which prints `<name> is not installed`) it adds `notInstalled: true` and `summary: '<name> is not installed; nothing was removed'` (` in the project lexicon` before `;` with `--project`). `runPackList` and `runPackSearch(query, ...)` mark packs installed from the trusted lexicon files only: a skipped project lexicon is reported on stderr (`warnSkippedProject`) and, under `--json`, as the additive keys `projectTrust`, `skippedProject` (path only) and `note` (`skippedProjectJson`). Under `--json`, each `registry` entry of `pack list` and each `packs` hit of `pack search` carries `installed`. Every `installed` flag (vendored and community, table and JSON) is `installedPacks(loaded)`, `settings.packs` of the trusted files, the same source as MCP `list_packs` and `GET /packs`; `pack list`'s `communityInstalled` is the registry records of the listed scope (`installedRegistryPacks`), and when those disagree with that scope's file `pack list` prints one `lexicon: warn:` line per ref on stderr and `--json` adds `registryMismatch: RegistryRecordMismatch { fileOnly: string[]; recordOnly: string[] }` (`registryRecordMismatch(loaded, scope, recorded)` from core; the type is re-exported here; absent when they agree). `pack search` reports the same over every loaded scope (`findRegistryRecordMismatch`): the `lexicon: warn:` lines on stderr and `registryMismatch` under `--json`.
- `registerPackCommands(program, io, deps?)` adds `pack` with `list` (alias `ls`), `add <name...>`, `remove <name>` (alias `rm`), `show <name>`, `search <query>`, `update [ref]` and `validate <file>`. List/update carry `--project` through to scoped community state; update JSON without `--yes` reports complete previews and writes nothing. Every object-shaped `pack` JSON document leads with `ok` (see `jsonOk` under io.ts); the vendored `pack add --json` results stay a bare array. `pack add --json` prints one document: a single community ref's install object (`{ ok: true, preview: false, ref, added, merged, path, scope }`) or, without `--yes`, its preview (`{ ok: true, preview: true, scope, ref, author, homepage, version, checksum, terms }`), or for any other list one array in argument order holding every ref's outcome (vendored `InstallPackResult`s, community install records and community previews, all without `ok`). A failure (an unknown ref, a failed install, a missing `--registry`, a community fetch or checksum failure) stops the run with exit 1: the failing ref is in the document, as `{ ok: false, ref, error, nextStep? }` for a single ref or as a `{ ref, ok: false, error, nextStep? }` entry after the refs before it in a list (even when it is the first), the refs after it are not attempted and left out, and stderr says `lexicon: <ref>: <error>`, then `lexicon: <nextStep>` when there is a specific one (an unknown pack: `PACK_NOT_FOUND_NEXT_STEP`; a community ref without `--registry`: `run: lexicon pack add <ref> --registry <index> ...`), and, in a list, `lexicon: not attempted: <refs>`. A previewed or declined community ref writes nothing and does not stop the refs after it; without `--json`, a declined ref in a list prints `declined <ref>; nothing written for it.` and the run ends with `N packs installed (<names>); declined: <refs>`. Community add/show JSON previews include every applied term field; update previews expose alias-only and note changes as well as canonical additions/removals.

## src/cli/cmd-pending.ts
`lexicon pending`, the CLI over `src/core/pending.ts`.
- `PendingOptions extends CommonOptions { json?; all?; globalPath? }`; `runPendingList(opts, io)`, `runPendingAccept(ids, opts, io)` and `runPendingReject(ids, opts, io)`, each `Promise<number>`; `registerPendingCommands(program, io)` adds `pending` (the list) with `list`, `accept [ids...]` and `reject [ids...]` (`--all` on both). `pending` and each subcommand take `--json`; a subcommand's `--json` is honoured wherever it is written (commander hands a flag both levels define to `pending`, so the subcommands read it from there too).
- Under `--json`: the list prints `{ ok: true, file, pending: (PendingAlias & { alreadyKnown })[] }`; `accept` prints `{ ok, file, accepted: { id, heard, meant, canonical, path }[], failed: { id, error }[], unknown: string[] }` and `reject` `{ ok, file, rejected: string[], unknown: string[] }`, `ok` false and exit 1 when an id is unknown (or, for `accept`, an entry was not accepted; it stays pending), and then `error` (`no pending alias with id <id>` and `<id> not accepted: <error>`, joined with `; `) and `nextStep` (`run lexicon pending list to see the ids`, or, for failed accepts only, that they stay pending) follow `ok`. Each unknown id and failed accept is also a `lexicon:` line on stderr, under `--json` too. No ids and no `--all` is `{ ok: false, error, nextStep }` (`nothing to <verb>: name one or more ids, or pass --all`, `run: lexicon pending (to list ids)`), exit 1; an unreadable pending file is `{ ok: false, error, nextStep }` naming the file fix. In text mode, `accept --all` or `reject --all` on an empty queue prints `no pending aliases to <accept|reject> (<file>); corrections the prompt hook notices are queued here, and lexicon pending lists them` and exits 0 (it printed nothing).

## src/cli/cmd-import.ts
- `MAX_IMPORT_BYTES` (8 MB; files and stdin over it are refused before parsing).
- `registerImportCommands(program, io)` adds `import [file]` (`-` reads stdin) with `--format <f>` (default auto), `--project`, `--dry-run`, `--source <s>`, `--category <c>` (applied to terms lacking one), `--json`, plus `--guided`, `--sources`, `--limit`, `--yes`, `--preview-id`, `--accept` and `--home` for the guided flow.
- `runImport(file, opts: ImportCliOptions, io, readInput?): Promise<number>` the testable handler. Prints a canonical/aliases/status table and `imported N terms (M new, K merged, S skipped)`; skipped reasons go to stderr; unknown format lists `IMPORT_FORMAT_INFO` and returns 1 (under `json`, after printing `{ ok: false, error, nextStep }` on stdout). `--dry-run` reads the target file to label new vs merged without writing, except under `leaveUntrusted` with an existing project file: that file is not read, the report carries `untrustedTarget: true` and each row leaves out `created` (`ImportedTermReport.created?: boolean`; both `counts.created` and `counts.merged` count only rows that have it, and the table's status column reads `untrusted file`). Project-scope writes go through `addTerm` and therefore the trust gate. `ImportCliOptions.leaveUntrusted` (not a CLI flag; MCP `import_dictionary` sets it for a project file that is not trusted) writes with `addTerm`'s `leaveUntrusted`, and each report row is then the term as imported rather than the merged copy, so nothing already in that untrusted file is read back out.
- `importOptionConflict(formatArg, opts: ImportCliOptions & ImportGuidedOptions): string | undefined`: why the `import` command line cannot run, checked before anything is read: a positional format and a different `--format`, a file import with an option only `--guided` reads (`--sources`, `--limit`, `--yes`, `--preview-id`, `--accept`, `--home`), or `--guided` with one only a file import reads (`--source`, `--category`, `--format` or the format argument). The command refuses it (exit 1, `{ ok: false, error, nextStep }` under `--json`); shared options (`--dry-run`, `--project`, `--json`) are fine in either mode.

## src/cli/cmd-import-guided.ts
- `runImportGuided(opts: ImportGuidedOptions, io, deps?: ImportGuidedDeps): Promise<number>` handles `import --guided`. Options include sources/limit/dryRun/project/yes/home/json/globalPath plus previewId/accept. Dependencies add an injected prompter and interactive probe to ImportSourceDeps.
- Interactive mode lists source privacy descriptions without probing local profiles or providers, asks for source selection, then probes and harvests only those choices and reviews candidates before writing. Cancellation probes nothing. Noninteractive reads require explicit `--sources`, including JSON/dry-run previews; rejected defaults cause no availability probes or source reads. Only named sources are probed.
- `--json --sources <list>` returns `ImportGuidedReport { sources; candidates: { id; canonical; category; source; aliases; evidence; count; accepted? }[]; previewId?; dryRun; added; merged; skipped; path? }` without writing. Noninteractive apply needs `--yes --sources <list> --preview-id <digest> --accept <ids>`; it rechecks the complete source/destination digest and refuses drift or unknown candidate ids before writing. An empty explicit acceptance imports nothing. Project writes remain subject to trust.

## src/cli/cmd-install.ts
`lexicon install [client]` (`install-claude` stays in index.ts). Registered via `registerInstallCommands(program, io)`.
- `INSTALL_CLIENTS: readonly ['claude','codex','cursor','windsurf','gemini','claude-desktop','vscode','generic']`, `InstallClient` union.
- `runInstall(client: string | undefined, opts: InstallOptions, io, deps?: InstallDeps): Promise<number>`
  - `InstallOptions extends CommonOptions { apply?; scope?: 'user'|'project' (string, validated); project?; home?; json? }`; with `json`, runInstall runs the text install into a buffer and prints one document instead: `InstallJsonResult { ok: true, client, scope, applied, written: { file, outcome: InstallOutcome }[], output (the text, ANSI stripped) }`, or `{ ok: false, error, nextStep }` (exit 1) when it throws (an unknown client names the clients, a bad scope names `--scope user|project`), with `lexicon: <error>` and `lexicon: <nextStep>` on stderr as every other `--json` refusal has them, and `{ ok: false, error, nextStep, output }` when it returns non-zero; `InstallDeps { platform?; env?; cliDir? }` (test hooks, like `DoctorDeps`).
  - `claude` delegates to `runInstallClaude` (scope passed through; `--home` maps to `settingsPath`). `generic` / no client prints the `mcpServers` snippet and the client list. Unknown client throws.
  - Server path from `resolveServerPath(cliDir?)`, which delegates to `resolveIntegrationPaths` (bundle preferred, `dist/mcp/server.js` fallback). `InstallDeps { platform?; env?; cliDir?; onWritten?: (file, outcome: InstallOutcome) => void }`; `onWritten` is called once per config file under `--apply` (forwarded to `runInstallClaude` for `claude`).
  - Without `--apply` prints the file and the launch block/JSON that would be merged (or, when the file already holds a matching entry so `--apply` would change nothing, `already installed in <file>: the lexicon entry there matches, nothing to write` and no apply line), and ends `to write step 1, run: <installApplyCommand(client, opts)>` (claude: `to perform steps 1 and 2, run: <InstallClaudeOptions.applyCommand>`, which runInstall sets the same way; default `lexicon install claude [--scope project] --apply`). `installApplyCommand(client, opts): string` (rendered by `shownCommand` from io.ts) is `lexicon [--cwd <dir>] install <client> [--scope <s> | --project] [--home <dir>] --apply` with the flags this run was given, each word quoted by core's `shellWord` only when it needs it (POSIX, or PowerShell on win32), never cut however long its paths, `--cwd` only when it is not the current directory. The printed `claude mcp add ...` line quotes an argument as `powerShellWord` does on win32 (a backslash path bare), else in double quotes when it holds whitespace, a quote, `$`, a backtick or a backslash. With `--apply` reads the file (missing => `{}` / empty), merges, writes 2-space JSON + trailing newline (or TOML text), reports `created` / `updated` / `nothing changed`, then `undo: delete the "lexicon" entry under "<key>" in <file>` (plus `, and "<key>" itself, which this run added` when the file existed without that key) (Codex: `undo: delete the [mcp_servers.lexicon] table from <file>`, `table and its [mcp_servers.lexicon.env] table` when the env keys went into that sub-table); a file the run created ends ` (this run created the file, so deleting it undoes this)`. `runInstallClaude --apply` adds `undo: claude mcp remove --scope <scope> lexicon` after step 1 when the server is registered (or already was) and `undo: delete the lexicon hook entries under hooks.UserPromptSubmit and hooks.SessionStart in <settings>` after step 2 (with ` (this run created the file, so deleting it undoes step 2)` when it did). Never clobbers unrelated keys or other servers. For JSON clients a stale `lexicon` launch is refreshed while existing client options (`env`, disabled state, timeouts and allowlists) are kept; matching launch fields are a no-op. Invalid JSON, a non-object config/server map/entry, or a conflicting remote transport throws without writing. Codex retains its existing table replacement behavior; nested subtables survive.
  - `reloadStepFor(client: InstallClient): string | undefined`: what the user does so the client loads the new server (`restart Claude Code`, `restart Codex`, `restart Gemini CLI`, `restart Claude Desktop` (quit completely), `reload the Cursor window`, `reload the VS Code window`, `refresh the MCP servers in Windsurf`, each with its reason or menu path; undefined for `generic`). Printed after step 1 as `then: <step>` with `--apply` and `after --apply: <step>` without (`claude`: after step 3, only when no step failed); MCP `install_client` returns it as `reload`.
  - Always ends with the hint `lexicon export claude-md >> <rules file>` (CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md, .cursor/rules/lexicon.mdc, .windsurfrules, GEMINI.md, copilot-instructions.md).
  - `lexicon uninstall [client]` (src/cli/cmd-uninstall.ts, `registerUninstallCommands(program, io)`, wired by index.ts right after `registerInstallCommands`): `runUninstall(client, opts: UninstallOptions, io, deps?: UninstallDeps): Promise<number>`; `UninstallOptions extends CommonOptions { dryRun?; scope?; project?; home?; json? }`, `UninstallDeps { platform?; env?; exec?; settingsPath? }`. Clients are the install clients but `generic`; none or an unknown one, a bad `--scope`, or `--scope user` with `--project`, throws a `CliRefusal`. Targets are `targetFor`'s files (claude: `claude mcp get lexicon`, then `claude mcp remove --scope <scope> lexicon`, and the hooks in `claudeSettingsPathFor(scope, cwd)` or the `--home` one at user scope). Removes only what install writes: `removeServerFromJson(current, key): { next, outcome: UninstallOutcome }` (`isInstalledServerEntry(entry)`: `isInstalledLexiconServer` over `command` and `args`), `removeServerFromToml(text): { next, outcome }` (the `[mcp_servers.lexicon]` table and every `[mcp_servers.lexicon.*]` sub-table, matched by key however spelled, when its `command`/`args` (bare or quoted keys, `"command"`, `'args'`) are an install's), `removeHooksFromSettings(settings, events = HOOK_EVENTS): { settings, removed }` (`isInstalledLexiconHook` commands; emptied groups, events and `hooks` go). `UninstallOutcome = 'removed' | 'absent' | 'kept'` (`kept`: a `lexicon` entry install did not write, named and left alone). JSON writes go through `writeJsonFile(file, next, text)`, so `editJsonText` deletes the entry in place. `--dry-run` writes nothing, prints `would remove <entry> from <file>` and `to remove it, run: <uninstallCommand(client, opts)>`. Exit 1 when `claude mcp get` or `remove` fails for a reason other than no server. `--json`: `UninstallJsonResult { ok: true, client, scope, dryRun, changes: UninstallChange { file, entry, outcome }[], output }`, or `{ ok: false, error, nextStep }` (also on stderr).
  - `PASSTHROUGH_ENV = ['LEXICON_PATH', 'LEXICON_CWD']`; `lexiconServerEnv(env, cwd): Record<string, string> | undefined` returns the ones set (non-blank) in `env`, resolved to absolute paths against `cwd`. When any is set the entry carries `env` with them (JSON clients: added to the entry's existing `env`, where a variable the user already set wins; Codex: `env = { ... }` inline in `[mcp_servers.lexicon]`, or the missing keys appended to an existing `[mcp_servers.lexicon.env]` sub-table via `upsertTomlKeys(text, header, values)`, which never rewrites a key already there; claude: `-e KEY=value` pairs before `--scope` in `claude mcp add`, read from `InstallClaudeDeps.env`, default `process.env`; generic: in the printed snippet). A GUI client does not inherit the shell, so without this the installed server reads the default lexicon instead of the one the user installed from.
- `configPathFor(client, ctx: { home, cwd, platform, env, project }): string`: codex `~/.codex/config.toml` | `./.codex/config.toml`; cursor `~/.cursor/mcp.json` | `./.cursor/mcp.json`; windsurf `~/.codeium/windsurf/mcp_config.json`; gemini `~/.gemini/settings.json` | `./.gemini/settings.json`; claude-desktop macOS `~/Library/Application Support/Claude/claude_desktop_config.json`, win32 `%APPDATA%/Claude/...` (`%APPDATA%` unset or blank: `<home>/AppData/Roaming`, for vscode too), else `~/.config/Claude/...`; vscode macOS `~/Library/Application Support/Code/User/mcp.json`, win32 `%APPDATA%/Code/User/mcp.json`, linux `~/.config/Code/User/mcp.json`, project `./.vscode/mcp.json`. windsurf and claude-desktop throw on `--project`. `targetFor(client, ctx)` bundles path, key and entry.
- `mergeServerIntoJson(current: unknown, key: 'mcpServers'|'servers', entry): MergeResult { next, changed }` pure, never mutates input. vscode uses `servers` + `{ type: 'stdio', command, args }` (`VsCodeServerEntry`); everyone else `mcpServers` + `{ command: 'node', args: [serverPath] }` (`StdioServerEntry { command; args; env? }`).
- `codexBlock(entry): string` => `[mcp_servers.lexicon]\ncommand = "node"\nargs = ["<abs server path>"]` (`CODEX_TABLE`; strings escaped with JSON.stringify, valid TOML basic strings). `upsertTomlTable(text, header, block): { next, changed }` replaces the table from its header line up to the next `[` header (sub-tables such as `[mcp_servers.lexicon.env]` survive), else appends after a blank line. `upsertTomlTable` and `upsertTomlKeys` find the header by its key, however it is spelled (`[mcp_servers."lexicon"]`, `[ mcp_servers . 'lexicon' ]`, a trailing comment), and the replaced table gets the canonical `[mcp_servers.lexicon]` header. Before writing, `lexicon install codex --apply` refuses (`CliRefusal` `<file> already defines the lexicon server as an inline table under [mcp_servers] on line <n>; nothing was changed`, or `dotted keys under [...]` / `in the root table`, `an array of tables`, or `defines [mcp_servers] as an inline table`; next step: replace it with the table shown) when the server is defined other than by its own table, since an appended table would define it twice (`codexServerElsewhere(text): string | undefined` names the form and line). `isTomlTableHeader(line, header, { orSubTable? }): boolean` is the shared key-based header match (never an array-of-tables header); `lexicon uninstall codex` uses it and `codexServerElsewhere` too, so it removes a quoted or spaced header and refuses the same inline, dotted and array forms (`CliRefusal` `<file> defines the lexicon server as ...; nothing was changed`). No TOML library.

## src/cli/cmd-trust.ts
- `registerTrustCommands(program, io)` adds `trust [path]` (`--list`) and `untrust [path]`. `path` resolves against `--cwd`; a directory means the `.lexicon.yaml` inside it; no path means `resolvePaths({ cwd }).project`.
- `runTrust(target, opts: TrustOptions { list?; json?; yes? }, io, deps?: TrustDeps { isInteractive?; createPrompter? })`: resolves the project file for cwd (or the given path), refuses a missing or invalid file, takes the hash of the bytes it parsed (`parsedContentHash`), prints a preview table (canonical, first alias, capped rows), then on a terminal (`isInteractive()`, both stdin and stdout TTYs) and without `yes` asks `trust <path>? ...` through the `Prompter` (default no; `PromptClosedError` is a no). A no prints `declined; nothing was trusted.` and returns 0 with nothing written. A file whose hash changed while the question was open, or that no longer holds the parsed bytes when it is pinned (`trustProject` with `expectedSha256`: nothing is written), is refused with exit 1. Off a terminal without `yes` (and for a file not already `trusted`) it refuses after the preview: `no terminal to confirm trusting <path>; nothing was trusted`, next step `review the preview, then run: lexicon trust "<path>" --yes`, exit 1, nothing written (no environment-variable bypass; see docs/TRUST.md). A file that already reads `trusted` is not asked about. Then `trustProject` and reports `trusted` / `updated` / `re-pinned` with the registry path, or, when the registry already pinned these bytes (`alreadyPinned`), `already trusted: <path> (sha256 <12>, since <trustedAt>) in <registry>; nothing changed` and `result: 'already-trusted'` under `--json`. `--json` prints `ok: true` and then MCP `trust_project`'s shapes, with the full preview where MCP has the model summary: `{ ok: true, action: 'trust', path, previousStatus, status: 'trusted', result, sha256 (12 chars), trustedAt, registry, termCount, preview, more, note }` (`trustPreview(file)`), or `{ ok: true, action, path, status, result: 'declined', summary }` (a no exits 0, so `ok` is true); the preview table and the question go to stderr then, and only when it asks. Every refusal (no project file, a missing or invalid file, a file that changed during the question or the pin) under `--json` also prints `{ ok: false, error, nextStep?, action: 'trust', path? }` on stdout (`path` absent when no project file was found; `nextStep` names `lexicon edit --project` for a broken `.lexicon.yaml`), with the stderr lines and exit 1 as without it. `--list` prints every registry entry with its status (`trusted`, `changed`, `missing`) and notes when `LEXICON_TRUST_ALL` is set; `--list --json` prints `{ ok: true, registry, trustAll, trusted: TrustListEntry[] }`, `ok` and then the registry fields of `trust_project` `status`. A `trust.json` that exists but cannot be used (`trustRegistryProblem`) is not listed as empty: `--list` names it and the fix on stderr and exits 1, and `--list --json` prints `{ ok: false, error, nextStep, registry }`.
- `runUntrust(target, opts: CommonOptions & { json?; dryRun? }, io)`: `untrustProject`; reports when the file was not trusted. `--json` prints `{ ok: true, action: 'untrust', path, removed, registry, summary, dryRun? }` (exit 0 either way); `dryRun: true` marks a proposed, pending removal rather than a persisted change. No project file found: exit 1, and under `--json` `{ ok: false, error, nextStep, action: 'untrust' }` on stdout.

## src/cli/cmd-learn.ts
- `registerLearnCommands(program, io)` adds `learn [words...]` (`--from <sentence>`, `--project`, `--json`) and `stats` (`--json`).
- `correctionFromArgs(words, from): Correction | undefined`: `--from` is parsed with `parseCorrection`; exactly two words are `<heard> <meant>`; any other word list is joined and parsed as a sentence.
- `optionLikeWord(words): string | undefined`: the first word that reads as a command-line option (a dash or two followed by a letter: `--dry-run`, `-n`, `--scope=project`; `->` and `=>` do not match). `learn` allows unknown options so a bare `->` is a word; `runLearn` refuses such a word before anything else (`unknown option '<word>'; nothing was learned`, `nextStep` naming `lexicon learn --help`; the `JsonError` on stdout under `--json`, exit 1), with or without `--from`, so a mistyped flag is never saved as the heard spelling.
- `runLearn(words, opts: LearnOptions, io)`: refuses words given together with `from` (`--from and the words <words> were both given; learn takes one correction, so nothing was learned`, `nextStep: 'pass either <heard> <meant> or --from "<sentence>", not both'`, the `JsonError` under `--json`, exit 1; `correctionFromArgs` itself still prefers `from`); prints the usage examples (`CORRECTION_EXAMPLES`) and returns 1 when nothing parses; a sentence that parses only with `allowUnrelated` is refused instead with `unrelatedCorrectionError(heard, meant)` (`"<heard>" and "<meant>" neither look nor sound alike, so this reads as a change of meaning, not a spelling speech-to-text got wrong; nothing was learned`) and `nextStep` `if speech-to-text really writes "<heard>" for "<meant>", run: lexicon learn <heard> <meant>` (both on stderr, the `JsonError` under `--json`, exit 1); heard and meant that are the same spelling after trimming (`learn X X`) are refused before `learnCorrection` with its error and `nextStep: 'run: lexicon learn <heard> <meant>, where <heard> is the misspelling STT wrote'` (every command `runLearn` and `conflictLine` print has its known words quoted by `shownCommand`, bare when they need no quotes, and its placeholders as written) (both on stderr, the `JsonError` on stdout under `--json`, exit 1); otherwise `learnCorrection` and reports the alias added and the file, or, when nothing was written, `learnUnchangedNote(heard, canonical)` followed by `(<scope>: <path>)`, as `lexicon add` reports an unchanged term, plus a `warn:` line for `conflict` and one naming the file and `lexicon trust` for `untrustedProject`. `--project` forces `scope: 'project'`. `--json` prints `{ ok: true, term, created, aliasAdded, conflict?, untrustedProject?, path, file: { path, scope }, heard, meant, unchanged?, note? }`: the keys `learn_correction` returns, with `file` cut to its path and scope (never the file's terms), `unchanged: true` when nothing was written, and `note`: `learnUnchangedNote` with `unchanged`, then `conflictNote([conflict])` (naming `lexicon remove <canonical> --alias <alias> --<scope>` (each word shell-quoted, `conflictRemoveCommand`) for an alias) with `conflict`, as serve `POST /learn` gives them; no `note` when neither applies.
- `runStats(opts: StatsOptions, io)`: `computeStats(await loadLexicon({ cwd }))`, rendered by `renderStats(stats, note?)` or as JSON. A skipped project lexicon is reported on stderr (`warnSkippedProject`) and, under `--json`, as the additive keys `projectTrust`, `skippedProject` (path only) and `note`, as `lexicon_stats` does. With no terms at all (and no skipped project file), core `emptyStatsNote(loaded): string | undefined` (`The lexicon has no terms yet, so nothing is corrected or counted; run \`lexicon setup\`, or add one with: lexicon learn <heard> <meant>.`) is the line under the counts and the `--json` `note`; MCP `lexicon_stats` returns the same `note` naming `setup_lexicon` and `add_term`. `runExport(format, opts: ExportCliOptions { out?; category?; limit?; json? }, io)` reports a skipped project lexicon on stderr too; stdout stays the export. `out` is written with `writeFileAtomic` (a temp file and a rename, the existing file's mode kept), and refused (exit 1, nothing written, `{ ok: false, error, nextStep }` under `json`) when it is a directory (`--out <path> is a directory, so nothing was exported`) or resolves (through links; case-folded on win32 and darwin) to the global, project or skipped project lexicon file the export read (`--out <path> is the <scope> lexicon file the export reads, so nothing was written (...)`), next step `name another file with --out, or leave --out off to print the export`. With `json`, stdout is one document instead: `{ ok: true, format, terms, bytes, path }` after writing `out` (`terms` the terms written after `category` and `limit`, `bytes` the UTF-8 size of the file; an alias-row format adds `rows`, the alias rows written, and the human line reads `wrote csv export (N rows from M terms; K terms without an alias wrote no row)`), `{ ok: true, format, terms, bytes, content }` without `out` (`content` the export, exactly what stdout would have held), and `{ ok: true, formats: ExportFormatJson[] }` (`{ name, description, ext }`) with no format; both export shapes add `projectTrust`, `skippedProject` and `note` (`skippedProjectJson`) when a project lexicon was skipped. An export with no terms says why, as MCP `export_lexicon` does: core `emptyExportNote(emptyExportReason(loaded, format, opts), { format, filter: '--category <c...>' })` (the lexicon has no terms yet, naming `lexicon setup` and `lexicon learn`; its only terms are in the skipped project lexicon, naming `lexicon trust`; no term matched `--category`, naming `lexicon list`; or, for an alias-row format (`EXPORT_ALIAS_ROW_FORMATS`: csv, wispr, superwhisper, macos, espanso) whose exported `terms` all lack an alias, or whose matched terms all lack one so `limit` exported none, the export has no rows, naming `lexicon add`) goes to stderr as a `lexicon:` line and, under `json`, leads the `note` (stdout and `--out` are still the export, exit 0). A failure under `json` is `{ ok: false, error, nextStep }`. Without a format it prints the usage and format list -> 0, unless `--out`, `--category` or `--limit` was given: then `<flags> need(s) a format; nothing was exported`, `run: lexicon export <format> (formats below)` and the format list on stderr -> 1, nothing written.

## src/cli/cmd-review.ts
Interactive workflows. Every handler takes a `Prompter` (or an injectable spawner) so tests script the answers. `InteractiveOptions extends CommonOptions { globalPath?: string }` is a test hook (no CLI flag) forwarded to the store.
- `runHarvestInteractive(candidates: readonly HarvestCandidate[], opts: HarvestInteractiveOptions, io, prompter: Prompter): Promise<number>` (`HarvestInteractiveOptions extends InteractiveOptions { harvest?: HarvestScanContext { root; minCount; filesScanned; belowMinCount; symbolsOnly } }`, the scan `runHarvest` ran): `opts.cwd` is the harvested root (the project lexicon written to). With no candidates it prints `noHarvestCandidatesLine(opts.harvest)` (without `harvest`, `no candidates to review`) and returns 0: `no candidates found in <root>: left out N names seen fewer than <minCount> times (to include them: --min-count 1) and M names found only as code symbols (to include them: --symbols)`, or, with nothing left out, that the directory holds no manifest, README or source file, or that N files were scanned with no name in them, each naming `lexicon add <canonical> <alias>`; plain `lexicon harvest` prints the same line. When every candidate is already a term with all its suggested aliases it prints `nothing to review: all N candidates are already in the lexicon with their suggested aliases. To see them: lexicon list; to go over existing terms: lexicon review` and returns 0 before any prompt. Checks the trust gate up front: an existing untrusted/changed `.lexicon.yaml` prints the `ProjectTrustError` message to stderr and returns 1 before any prompt; a `ProjectTrustError` thrown mid-walk does the same. Per candidate shows `[i/n] canonical  category, seen Nx`, up to three evidence entries and the suggested aliases, then asks `add? [y/n/e/c/a/q]` (Enter = y): `y` `addTerm(scope: 'project')`, `n` skip, `e` replace aliases with a comma-separated list (the canonical itself is dropped), `c` change category (re-asked until valid), `a` add this and every remaining candidate without asking, in one `addTerms` call (one read and one write; a `ProjectTrustError` there writes none of them), `q` stop (the rest counts as skipped); a `PromptClosedError` (ctrl-D) stops the same way and prints `input closed at candidate i of n; the terms added before it stay in the project lexicon` (or `; nothing was added`) before the summary. A write that fails for any reason but trust (a full disk, an unreadable file) stops the walk the same way: stderr gets `lexicon: could not write the project lexicon at candidate i of n (<error>); the terms added before it stay in the project lexicon` (or `; nothing was added`), the summary is printed, and it returns 1. Candidates are copied before editing. Ends with `added N new terms, merged M, skipped S in <path>` and the trusted note, then `next: run lexicon list to see the terms` when anything was added or merged.
- `runReview(opts: ReviewOptions, io, prompter?): Promise<number>`: `ReviewOptions extends InteractiveOptions { neverHit?; project?; global?; category?; json? }`. `json` is refused before anything is read: `{ ok: false, error: 'review is interactive and has no --json output', nextStep: REVIEW_NON_INTERACTIVE_NEXT_STEP }` (`list --json`, `remove`, `add` and the MCP tools `list_terms`, `remove_term`, `add_term`), returns 1. Global file by default, `--project` the project file found for cwd (error when none; `--project` + `--global` is an error). Without a prompter and not on a TTY throws a `CliRefusal`, `review needs a terminal ...; nothing was changed`, with the same `nextStep`. A missing file or an empty selection prints a note naming the next step and returns 0: `no global lexicon at <path> yet; run lexicon setup to create one`; `--project` with no project file throws `no project lexicon found for <cwd>, so nothing to review yet (run: lexicon harvest -i to pull names from this repo, or lexicon add "Ashlr.AI" Ashler --project)` (not `lexicon init --project`, whose empty file leaves nothing to review), `no terms to review in <path> yet. Try: lexicon add "Ashlr.AI" Ashler` for an empty file (or no filters), and `no terms to review in <path>: N terms there, none matching <filters> (run lexicon review without it to see them)` when `--never-hit` or `--category` matched none. An untrusted project file returns 1 with the `ProjectTrustError` message. Per term shows `[i/n] canonical  category, N hits[, source]`, aliases, phonetic and notes, then `[k/d/e/p/n/q]` (Enter = k): `k` keep, `d` delete (printing `removed <canonical> from <path>`, as `lexicon remove` does), `e` edit aliases (comma-separated), `p` phonetic hint (empty clears), `n` notes (empty clears), `q` stop; a `PromptClosedError` (ctrl-D) stops the same way, keeps the answers given and prints `input closed at term i of n; keeping the answers given before it`. A final write that fails prints `lexicon: could not write <path> (<error>); nothing was written, so this review's N removals and M edits were not saved` on stderr and returns 1. Writes via `writeLexiconFile` once at the end only when something changed, then `refreshTrust` for a project file. A project file that changed on disk during the review and is no longer trusted (rewritten by a git pull or a hand edit, not by a lexicon writer, which re-pins it) is not merged or written: `lexicon: <path> changed during review and is not trusted (<status>), so nothing was written and this review's N removals and M edits were not saved; run lexicon trust to look at the new version, then lexicon review --project again` on stderr, return 1. Ends with `kept K, removed D, edited E[, U not reviewed (left as they were)]. Wrote <path>` or `... (nothing written)`, `U` the terms after a stop that were never answered (the term on screen at the stop counts only when no `e`, `p` or `n` changed it), then `next: run lexicon list to see the terms` when the file was written.
- `runEdit(opts: EditOptions, io, spawnEditor?: SpawnEditor, env?, interactive?: () => boolean): Promise<number>`: `EditOptions extends InteractiveOptions { project?; json? }` (`json` is refused before any file is created or editor started: `{ ok: false, error: 'edit opens an editor and has no --json output', nextStep: EDIT_NON_INTERACTIVE_NEXT_STEP }`, naming `add`, `remove`, `lexicon path` and the MCP tools `add_term`, `remove_term`, returns 1); `SpawnEditor = (command, args, filePath) => Promise<number>` (exit code), default `spawnEditorInherit` (`spawn` with `stdio: 'inherit'`, no shell, of what `resolveEditorSpawn(command, args, filePath, platform?, env?, isFile?): EditorSpawn { file; args; windowsVerbatimArguments }` gives: the command itself on POSIX; on win32 an editor that resolves through PATHEXT (in the directory it names, else on PATH, never the current directory) to a `.cmd` or `.bat` shim, such as `code`, runs as `%ComSpec% /d /s /c "<line>"` with verbatim arguments, each argument quoted and every cmd metacharacter `^`-escaped so a file path with spaces, `&` or `%` arrives whole; anything else, a `.exe` or a name it cannot resolve, is spawned directly as before). A terminal editor (`isTerminalEditor(command, args)`: vi, vim, nvim, nano, micro, hx, kak and other editors that run in the terminal, `emacs` with `-nw`, `-t` or `--no-window-system`, `emacsclient` with `-t`, `-nw` or `--tty`; not `vim -g`, `gvim`, plain `emacs` or GUI editors) when `interactive()` (default `isInteractive`, stdin and stdout both a TTY) is false is refused before anything is created or started: `$EDITOR (<command>) is a terminal editor, and stdin and stdout are not both a terminal, so it would wait for input that never comes` (or `$VISUAL`) and a next step naming a terminal, `VISUAL="code --wait"` and the file's path, returning 1; it used to hang. A missing global file is created empty first, inside the file's lock so a concurrent write cannot be renamed away by that create. Editor from `$VISUAL` then `$EDITOR`, split on whitespace with a single- or double-quoted part kept whole and its quotes removed, backslashes kept as written (`editorCommand(env)`, so `"/Applications/Sublime Text.app/.../subl" -w` runs that path); an editor that cannot be started (`spawnEditor` rejects, e.g. ENOENT) prints `could not start the editor $VISUAL names (<command>): <reason>` (or `$EDITOR`) and a next step naming the variable and the file's path, and returns 1; none set prints the path and a next step (`after saving, check it with: lexicon doctor`, or for a project file `after saving, run: lexicon trust (it shows the terms and trusts the file; until then its terms are not used)`) and returns 0. An editor that exits non-zero (vim `:cq`) is an aborted edit: `<editor> exited with code <n>, so the edit is treated as aborted` (plus ` and the project lexicon was not re-trusted` for a trusted project file, and a line saying it changed anyway and needs `lexicon trust` when its bytes no longer match the pin) and a `lexicon edit` next step on stderr, nothing checked or re-trusted, and returns 1. After an editor that exits 0 the file is re-read with `readLexiconFile` (which uses `parseLexicon`): on error prints the message plus `your edits are still in <path>` and returns 1 without touching the file. A project file that was trusted before the edit is re-pinned (`refreshTrust`); an untrusted one stays untrusted with a `lexicon trust` hint. `lexicon edit <anything>` is a usage error (exit 1, and `{ ok: false, error, nextStep }` under `--json`) whose message says edit takes no term because it opens the whole file, naming `lexicon add <canonical> <alias...>` and `lexicon remove <term>`; commander said only `Expected 0 arguments but got 1`.
- `registerReviewCommands(program, io)` adds `review` (`--never-hit`, `--project`, `--global`, `--category <c>`) and `edit` (`--project`).

## src/cli/cmd-suggest.ts
- `registerSuggestCommands(program, io)` adds `suggest` (`--json`, `--apply`, `--yes`, `--harvest [dir]`, `--limit <n>`, `--project`). The global `--cwd` is accepted after the subcommand too; giving it explicitly (or `--harvest`) turns the repo harvest on, a bare `lexicon suggest` only reads the history.
- `runSuggest(opts: SuggestCliOptions, io, deps?: SuggestDeps): Promise<number>`: `SuggestCliOptions extends CommonOptions { json?; apply?; yes?; harvest?: string | boolean; limit?; project?; globalPath? (test hook) }`; `SuggestDeps { suggest?; loadLexicon?; addTerms?; removeTerm?; prompter?; now? }` default to the core (`addTerms` replaced `addTerm`: every add goes through it, and a run of consecutive alias, never and term suggestions bound for the same file is one call, so one read and one write; a suggestion for the other file or a stale removal ends the run, so the writes keep the suggestions' order, and a refused run writes none of its suggestions). Loads the lexicon with `parseSkipped: false`, as MCP `suggest_terms` does (it reads only the merged terms, the global file and a trusted project file; an untrusted or changed project file is reported on stderr by path as elsewhere and never parsed), calls `suggestTerms({ loaded, cwd?, limit?, now? })`; with `--project` every `term` suggestion's `scope` and `path` become `project` and the project lexicon (the loaded or skipped one, else `defaultProjectPath(cwd)`), the file `--yes` and `--apply` write it to; then: `--json` prints the array and applies nothing; `--json --yes` applies as `--yes` does and prints one document, `{ ok: true, threshold, applied: SuggestApplied[], skipped: TermSuggestion[] }` (`SuggestApplied { kind; canonical; alias?; scope; action: 'added' | 'merged' | 'removed' | 'already-gone' | 'skipped'; path?; stillIn? }`, `path` the file written), or `{ ok: false, error, nextStep, applied }` when a write is refused partway, exit 1; `--json --apply` is refused (`--apply asks about each suggestion and cannot be combined with --json`, `nextStep` naming `--yes --json`), exit 1, before anything is loaded or asked; no suggestions prints a note and returns 0; the default prints `renderSuggestions(suggestions)` (a `renderTable` with kind, canonical, alias, count, confidence, reason) plus a footer; `--yes` applies every suggestion with `confidence >= AUTO_APPLY_CONFIDENCE` without asking and reports `applied N suggestions, skipped M below 0.80`; `--apply` walks them with a Prompter (`deps.prompter`, else a TTY is required and the error suggests `--yes`), showing kind, confidence, count, reason and up to three evidence lines, then `apply? [y/n/a/q]` (Enter = y, except a 'stale' removal defaults to n and is never swept up by `a`). `--apply` with `--yes` is an error (exit 1, `nextStep` `pass --apply to choose one by one, or --yes to apply those at or above 0.80`).
- Applying: 'alias' -> `addTerm({ canonical, aliases: [alias] }, { scope })` (merge); 'never' -> `addTerm({ canonical, aliases: [], never: [alias] }, { scope })`; 'term' -> `addTerm({ canonical, aliases: suggestion.aliases ?? suggestAliases(canonical), category?, source: 'learned' }, { scope: --project ? 'project' : 'global' })`; 'stale' -> `removeTerm(canonical, { scope })`, reported as `removed <canonical> from <path>` plus one `warn:` line per copy still in the other file (`termHolders` of the loaded lexicon) with the `lexicon remove` command that removes it. For existing terms `scope` is the term's own (`term.scope`, default global). A `ProjectTrustError` prints the message and returns 1; other store errors propagate.

## src/cli/cmd-setup.ts
`lexicon setup`, the guided first run. `cmd-setup.ts` owns `registerSetupCommands`, `runSetup` and `setupApplyCommand` and re-exports the rest from `./setup/types.js` (every interface and constant below, plus `Ctx`, the step context) and `./setup/detect.js` (`captureIO`, `lastLine`, `isSetupClient`, `isSetupApp`, `parseClientList`, `parsePackList`, `setupNameProblem`, `setupHintProblem`, `findGitRoot`, `suggestCompany`, `readPackageIdentity`, `defaultSetupExec`, `tryExec`, `configDirFor`, `detectClients`). The seven numbered steps themselves are `stepLexicon`, `stepPacks`, `stepHarvest`, `stepClients`, `stepServe`, `stepExport` and `stepDemo`, in `./setup/steps.js`; the summary card that follows them is `printSummary` in `cmd-setup.ts` and carries no number. Registered via `registerSetupCommands(program, io)`: `setup` with `-y/--yes`, `--clients <list>`, `--company <name>`, `--person <name>`, `--phonetic <hint>`, `--app <wispr|superwhisper|macos|none>`, `--export-dir <dir>`, `--harvest` / `--no-harvest`, `--serve` / `--no-serve`, `--reseed`, `--home <dir>`, `--json`, `--dry-run`. Under `--json`, `runSetup` prints `{ ok, ...SetupSummary }` (or `{ ok, ...SetupPlan }` under `--dry-run`) on stdout, `ok` first and false exactly when the exit code is 1 (a client or the serve install failed); the returned `summary` and `plan` do not carry `ok`. Before any step runs, `runSetup` refuses a `--company` or `--person` that is blank, holds a control character (a newline included) or is over `LIMITS.word` (80) characters once trimmed, in a dry run too: `<flag> must ...; nothing was written. ...`, the problem from `setupNameProblem(value: string): string | undefined` (in `./setup/detect.js`, which MCP `setup_lexicon` uses for the same check), instead of creating the lexicon and failing at the write on the generated aliases. A name or `--phonetic` holding U+FFFE, U+FFFF or an unpaired surrogate is refused the same way (`must not contain U+XXXX (...)`). A `--phonetic` that holds a control character or is over `LIMITS.text` (200) characters once trimmed is refused the same way (`--phonetic must ...; nothing was written. ...`, the problem from `setupHintProblem(value: string): string | undefined`, also in `./setup/detect.js`; blank is no hint). On a terminal, a company, extra term name or phonetic hint typed at the prompt is checked with the same function and asked again (`the name|hint must ...; type it again (Enter to skip)`, the default dropped so Enter skips) before anything is written for it. An error `runSetup` throws (an unknown `--app` or client, a refused name, a step that failed outright) prints `{ ok: false, error, nextStep }` on stdout as well as the `lexicon: <message>` stderr line (`fail`), exit 1; without `--json` it propagates as before.
- `SETUP_CLIENTS` (= `INSTALL_CLIENTS` without `generic`), `SetupClient`; `SETUP_APPS = ['wispr','superwhisper','macos','none']`, `SetupApp`; `HARVEST_LIMIT = 10`, `HARVEST_MIN_COUNT = 2`, `SEEDED_TERMS = 3`.
- `SetupOptions extends CommonOptions { yes?; json?; reseed?; clients?: string; packs?: string; company?; person?; phonetic?; harvest?; harvestUntrusted?; forAgent?; serve?; app?: string; exportDir?; home?; dryRun? }`. `serve` and `harvest` are three-valued: `true` does it, `false` skips it without a prompt, `undefined` asks on a terminal (`serve` defaults to yes, `harvest` to no) and is treated as `false` without one (`--yes`, no TTY, `--json`), so a non-interactive run never creates a service, or writes and trusts a project lexicon, that was not asked for. A dry run still lists the harvest candidates in `wouldHarvest` unless `harvest === false`. `harvestUntrusted` (not a CLI flag; MCP `setup_lexicon` sets it) writes the harvest with `addTerm`'s `leaveUntrusted` unless `projectWriteTrusted` says the project file is already trusted: a trusted file takes the names and stays trusted, while a new, untrusted or changed one is not trusted by the run and `SetupSummary.untrustedProject` names it.
- `SetupPlan { plan: true; interactive: boolean; wouldAsk: string[]; lexiconPath; lexiconExists; wouldSeed: string[]; wouldInstallPacks: string[]; packsOnOffer: string[]; packsToInstall: string[]; alreadyInstalledPacks?: string[]; wouldHarvest: string[]; detectedClients: string[]; wouldInstallClients: string[]; wouldInstallServe: boolean; serveBlocked?: string; wouldExport: { format; path }[] }`: what this same command would do without `--dry-run`, computed without writing. `interactive` is true when that run would find a terminal and prompt; `wouldAsk` names the steps that are a question (`packs`, `harvest`, `clients`, `serve`, `export`) and the `would*` fields are the outcome of taking every default, so `wouldInstallServe` and `wouldExport` are filled for a run that would be asked and says yes. When the login service is asked for (`--serve`, or a terminal run that would ask) and `serveBlocker` reports a reason, `serveBlocked` carries it, `wouldInstallServe` is false, `serve` is not in `wouldAsk`, and the plan card prints `login service: would not install (<reason>)`.
- `SetupPackResult { name; added; merged; status }` and `SetupSummary.packs: SetupPackResult[]`; `SetupPlan.wouldInstallPacks: string[]` (never a pack the global lexicon already lists; those are `SetupPlan.alreadyInstalledPacks?: string[]`, absent when none, and the terminal checklist leaves them out too); it is the set on offer (the `--packs` list, else the defaults a terminal would show checked), not what will be installed, and `SetupPlan.packsOnOffer: string[]` is the same list under a name that says so. `SetupPlan.packsToInstall: string[]` is what the same call without the dry run installs: the `--packs` list, or the checklist's checked defaults when the plan `wouldAsk` `packs` (Enter installs them); empty under `--yes`, off a terminal and for MCP `setup_lexicon` without `packs`, matching the step's `would install: none` line; `SetupDeps.listPacks?`.
- `SetupDemo { heard; corrected; terms: string[]; usedExample }` is what step 7 printed, on `SetupSummary.demo?` and `SetupPlan.demo?` (a dry run computes it without writing). `usedExample` is true when the user's own lexicon could not demonstrate anything and the built-in example terms stood in. Absent only if the step threw.
- `SetupSummary { lexiconPath; termsAdded: string[]; clients: SetupClientResult { name; status: 'installed' | 'skipped' | 'failed'; detail? }[]; serve: 'installed' | 'skipped' | 'failed'; exports: { format; path }[]; demo?; untrustedProject?; nextStep? }`. `nextStep` is the text output's `Next:` line without the label (`add a name you dictate: lexicon add "Your Co" --suggest (then open <client> and say it in a sentence)` or `open <client> and dictate a sentence with "<name>" in it`), so `--json` carries it; with `forAgent` (MCP `setup_lexicon`, not a CLI flag) it names `add_term` instead, the progress lines (whose hints name CLI flags) are not written at all, and a failed client's or login service's retry hint names `install_client` / `setup_lexicon` with its arguments. `untrustedProject` is the project lexicon a `harvestUntrusted` harvest wrote without pinning (absent when the file was already trusted; printed `(not trusted: review it and run lexicon trust)` instead of `(trusted)`). `termsAdded` lists only terms created (merges into existing terms are not counted), global seed first, then harvested project terms. `detail` for `installed` is the tildified config file(s) the installer reported through `onWritten`, comma separated, `(unchanged)` appended to one that already had the entry (the installer's last output line when nothing was reported); for `failed` the last error line.
- `SetupDeps { platform?; env?; home?; exists?: (p) => boolean; exec?: SetupExec ((file, args) => string, trimmed stdout, throws on failure; default execFileSync with a 5 s timeout); cliDir?; isInteractive?; createPrompter?; installClient?: (client, opts: InstallOptions, io, onWritten: (file, outcome: InstallOutcome) => void) => Promise<number> (default runInstall with `{ platform, env, onWritten, cliDir? }`); installServe?: (opts: ServeOptions, io, deps: ServeDeps) => Promise<number> (default runServeInstall; `deps` = `{ platform, env, home? (when overridden), cliPath? = <cliDir>/index.js when cliDir is set }`); serveBlocker?: (deps: ServeDeps) => Promise<string | undefined> (default serveInstallBlocker, called by the plan with the same `deps`); harvest?: (root, opts) => Promise<HarvestCandidate[]> (default harvestRepo) }`. Every process spawn and filesystem probe goes through these.
- `runSetup(opts: SetupOptions, io: IO, deps?: SetupDeps): Promise<{ code: number; summary: SetupSummary; plan?: SetupPlan }>`. Interactive (prompt.ts) when none of `yes`, `json`, `dryRun` is set and `isInteractive()`; otherwise every default is taken (off a TTY without `--yes` it says so; when only stdout is redirected, `onlyStdoutRedirected()`, it also says on stderr `lexicon setup: stdout is not a terminal (piped or redirected), so no questions were asked and the defaults were taken, as with --yes.` and how to answer them: without the pipe, or with flags). Under `dryRun` every step records in `plan` what this same command would do without it, nothing is written and nothing prompts, and the summary card becomes a plan card ending in `to apply, run: <setupApplyCommand(opts)>`. `setupApplyCommand(opts: SetupOptions): string` is `lexicon [--cwd <dir>] setup` followed by every flag this run was given except `--dry-run` (`--yes`, `--clients`, `--company`, `--person`, `--phonetic`, `--app`, `--export-dir`, `--packs` / `--no-packs`, `--harvest` / `--no-harvest`, `--serve` / `--no-serve`, `--reseed`, `--home`, `--json`), rendered by `shownCommand` from io.ts (each word quoted by core's `shellWord` only when it needs it, POSIX or PowerShell on win32, never cut), `--cwd` only when it is not the current directory. The plan reasons about the run it describes rather than about itself: `isInteractive()` decides `plan.interactive`, the steps that would prompt are listed in `plan.wouldAsk` and printed as `asks` with the answer Enter gives, so a step a real run would stop at is never reported as skipped. Under `--json` progress goes to stderr and stdout carries only the summary JSON. `code` is 1 when any client or the serve install failed; every step still runs. A prompt whose input closes (ctrl-C or ctrl-D, `PromptClosedError`) stops the run at that step instead: `summary.stoppedAt` is the step's title, a `Stopped at "<title>"` card lists what the finished steps wrote, `nextStep` says to run `lexicon setup` again (every step skips what is done), and `code` is 1. A step that throws (an export directory that cannot be written, a pack that will not install) stops the run the same way outside a dry run: `lexicon setup: <title>: <error>` on stderr, `summary.failedAt` and `summary.error`, a `Failed at "<title>": <error>` card of what the finished steps wrote, a `nextStep` to fix the error and run setup again, and `code` 1; under `dryRun` it still throws. Steps, each one line of output:
  1. Global lexicon: `runInit` when missing. Seed unless the file already has `SEEDED_TERMS` terms (or `--reseed`); in that case an explicit `--person` / `--company` is still seeded, without the guessed defaults, the company prompt or the `add another term?` loop: the person term from `--person`, else `git config --global user.name`, else `git -C <cwd> config user.name` (repo-local, system or `includeIf` config), else `$GIT_AUTHOR_NAME` / `$GIT_COMMITTER_NAME` (confirmed on a terminal; category `person`, `suggestAliases`), then the company term from `--company`, else the prompt with a default from `suggestCompany(packageName, remoteUrl, author?)` (cwd `package.json` scope `@ashlr/x` with author `Ashlr.AI` -> `Ashlr.AI`, without a matching dotted author -> `Ashlr`, else the git remote org `github.com/ashlrai/x` -> `ashlrai`; `users`/`orgs` path segments are rejected), category `brand`, `suggestAliases`, `--phonetic` or the prompt. Non-interactive with neither `--company` nor a suggestion: no company term. On a terminal `add another term?` loops (name, phonetic, category). Writes go through `addTerm(scope: 'global')`, so reruns merge instead of duplicating; a person or company whose canonical is already in the global lexicon (case-insensitive) is reported as `already present: <existing spelling>` and not written or counted (the plan leaves it out of `wouldSeed` too).
  2. Starter packs (`stepPacks`; skip with `--no-packs`, choose with `--packs a,b` validated by `parsePackList`): on a terminal a checklist with `DEFAULT_PACKS` (developer, ai, voice-tools) checked and the other packs (`business`, `cloud-devops`, `profession-q8`) offered unchecked, one yes/no each; without one, nothing is installed unless `--packs` names them, because this writes a hundred-odd terms into the global lexicon. Each chosen pack goes through `installPack`, reported as `installed <name>: N added, M merged`.
  3. Repo harvest (skip with `--no-harvest`; without a terminal only with `--harvest`, else `skipped (not requested)`; a dry run always lists): when cwd is inside a git repo, `harvest(root, { limit: 10, minCount: 2 })`, candidates whose canonical the global lexicon or this run's seed already covers are dropped (`already in the global lexicon: ...`), a table plus a confirm on a terminal **whose default is no** (the names are a write inside the user's repo plus a trust decision, offered before they can judge the list), then `addTerm(scope: 'project', cwd: root)` for every candidate (auto-trusted by the store, or with `leaveUntrusted` under `harvestUntrusted`); a `ProjectTrustError` is reported as skipped.
  4. Agent clients: `--clients a,b` (validated by `parseClientList`; `none` skips) or `detectClients(deps, cwd): DetectedClient { name; detected; evidence? }[]` (config dir from `configPathFor` / `~/.claude`, the app bundle under `/Applications` on darwin for Claude, Cursor, VS Code and Windsurf, or the CLI on PATH via `findOnPath` with the injected `exists`; never spawns). On a terminal the detected clients are a multi-select with all pre-selected. Each chosen client runs `installClient(client, { apply: true, cwd, home? }, capturedIO, onWritten)`; the files reported through `onWritten` become `detail` (the last output line when none was reported), a non-zero code or a throw is `failed` with a retry hint on stderr.
  5. Local API (skip with `--no-serve`; without a terminal only with `--serve`): on darwin/linux (confirm on a terminal, default yes) `installServe({ cwd }, capturedIO, serveDeps)` with `serveDeps.cliPath = <cliDir>/index.js` when `cliDir` is set -> `serve: 'installed' | 'failed'` (the installer's refusal of a missing program path is a `failed` with its stderr line); other platforms are `skipped` with a pointer to `lexicon serve --install`.
  6. Dictation app: `--app` or a single choice (Wispr Flow, Superwhisper, macOS Text Replacement, none; non-interactive default none). Exports the merged lexicon to `<--export-dir | ~/Desktop | dirname(global)>/lexicon-<format>.<ext>` (`exportLexiconWithNotes`), prints `wrote <file> (N rows)`, the rows the app imports. A file already there that differs from this export (edited by hand) is kept: on a terminal a confirm (default no) asks whether to replace it, and otherwise (or on a no) it says `kept <file> as it was: it differs from this export` and writes the first `lexicon-<format>-<n>.<ext>` (n from 2) that is free or already holds this export; past 99 it writes nothing and says so. The plan names that file in `wouldExport`. each note on what the format left out on stderr, and where to import it.
  7. Does it work? (`stepDemo`): `demonstrate(merged, ctx.seeded)` over the terms this run just seeded (a dry run adds the terms step 1 would seed to `merged` and prefers `wouldSeed`, so the plan shows the same correction as the real run), printed as `you dictate:` / `your agent sees:` plus the replacements. Falls back to the built-in example terms when the user's own lexicon cannot demonstrate anything yet, and says so, so the run never ends in "no changes". Recorded as `SetupSummary.demo` (or `SetupPlan.demo`).

  Then the summary card, which is not numbered: lexicon path, terms added, packs, clients, local API, exports, and one next action (dictate a sentence with the company name in the client that was installed), followed by a dim `later:` line naming `lexicon suggest`, `lexicon stats` and `lexicon voice`. Under `--dry-run` the plan card replaces it and ends in `to apply, run: <setupApplyCommand(opts)>`.

Company guessing uses `suggestCompany(packageName, remoteUrl, author?)`: a dotted
package author whose full name or first dot-separated component matches the
scope (case-insensitive) supplies the canonical spelling. For example,
`@ashlr/lexicon` with author `Ashlr.AI` suggests `Ashlr.AI`. Otherwise the scope
keeps its spelling with its first letter capitalized, or the remote organization
is used for an unscoped package. No suffix is invented.
`readPackageIdentity(cwd): Promise<{ name?: string; author?: string }>` reads the
package name and the string author or author object's `name`; missing, malformed
or non-object manifests return an empty object. Setup validates the resulting
name before seeding it, including in previews.

### Dotted brand in setup (issue #218)

For this scoped `package.json`:

```json
{ "name": "@ashlr.ai/lexicon", "author": "Ashlr.AI" }
```

Setup previously truncated the company suggestion to `Ashlr`. The requested
behavior is to preserve the dot and suggest `Ashlr.AI`; the dry-run preview and
the seeded brand must agree. The matching author supplies the capitalization.
Without it, the dotted scope still produces `Ashlr.ai`, with the dot intact.

## src/cli/index.ts  (bin: lexicon)
A lone `--version`, `-V` or `-v` prints the package version before commander or the core loads. Other invocations load `run.ts` for the handshake, dispatch and error handling. `program.ts` exports `buildProgram(io): Command` and wires commander; actions dynamically import handlers in `commands.ts` and `cmd-*.ts`. Lightweight argument and IO helpers live in `args.ts` and are re-exported from `io.ts`. Twelve registrars run in this order: `registerImportCommands`, `registerInstallCommands`, `registerUninstallCommands`, `registerTrustCommands`, `registerLearnCommands`, `registerPendingCommands`, `registerServeCommands`, `registerVoiceCommands`, `registerSetupCommands`, `registerSuggestCommands`, `registerReviewCommands`, `registerPackCommands`. Global `--cwd <dir>`: the `preAction` hook refuses one that is not an existing directory before any command but `hook` and `mcp` runs (`cwdOptionProblem(cwd): CliRefusal | undefined` in io.ts; the `CliRefusal { message, nextStep? }` it throws is reported through `refuse`: `lexicon: --cwd "<dir>" is not an existing directory`, then `pass a directory that exists, or leave --cwd out to use the current directory`, both also as `{ ok: false, error, nextStep }` under `--json` or `--full`, exit 1). Commands wired in `program.ts`: `init` (`--project`, `--json`), `add <canonical> [aliases...]` (`--phonetic`, `--category`, `--notes`, `--project`, `--suggest`, `-i/--interactive`, `--never <word...>`), `remove|rm <canonical>` (`--project`), `list|ls` (`--json`, `--category`, `--query`), `normalize [text...]` (stdin if omitted; `--json`, `--diff`, `--dry-run`, `--min-confidence`, `--no-phonetic`, `--no-fuzzy`, `--include-untrusted`), `harvest [path]` (`--limit`, `--min-count`, `--add`, `-i/--interactive`, `--yes`, `--json`, `--full`), `export [format]` (`--out`, `--category <c...>`, `--limit`), `path` (`--json`), `doctor` (`--json`), `mcp`, `hook`, `daemon` (`--once`, `--paste`, `--interval`, `--dry-run`, `--quiet`, `--backend`, `--which`), `install-claude` (`--apply`, `--scope`, `--project`, `--home`, `--json`: the flags of `install claude`, forwarded to `runInstall("claude", ...)`, with no `--scope` default of its own). Then `registerImportCommands`, `registerInstallCommands`, `registerUninstallCommands`, `registerTrustCommands`, `registerLearnCommands`, `registerReviewCommands`, `registerSetupCommands` add `import`, `install`, `uninstall`, `trust`, `untrust`, `learn`, `stats`, `review`, `edit`, `setup`. `docs/CLI.md` is generated from their `--help`.

## src/hooks/user-prompt-submit.ts  (bin via `lexicon hook`; bundled as plugin/hook-main.mjs, run through plugin/hook.mjs)
Claude Code hook entry for two events, dispatched on the payload's `hook_event_name`. Exit 0 always; every error path prints nothing (message to stderr). Must finish < 200ms for a 1KB prompt.
- `HookEventName = 'UserPromptSubmit' | 'SessionStart'`, `HookOptions { cwd?; matchBudgetMs?; elapsedMs?: () => number }`, `SESSION_CONTEXT_MAX_CHARS = 4000` (the whole SessionStart context: the table is truncated to leave room for the skipped-project note), `HOOK_MATCH_BUDGET_MS = 100` (UserPromptSubmit gets the index from `loadMatcherIndex(loaded)`, then passes `normalizeWithIndex` a `shouldStop` that turns true this many ms after matching starts (in the real hook, `main()` passes `elapsedMs` (the process uptime) and the budget is `hookMatchBudget(elapsedMs(), checkedChars)`, exported: what is left of `HOOK_TOTAL_BUDGET_MS = 160` from process start, at most `HOOK_MATCH_BUDGET_MS` and at least `HOOK_MATCH_FLOOR_MS = 20`, and the whole `HOOK_MATCH_BUDGET_MS` for a prompt of at most `HOOK_SHORT_PROMPT_CHARS = 2000` characters (a slow start under load cut a two-word prompt short at the floor), so Node's start and the load no longer come on top of a whole 100 ms scan; `matchBudgetMs` overrides both), so a huge paste against a large lexicon reports the corrections from its start instead of running past the hook timeout; before it, `parseCorrections(text, lexicon?, limits?: { shouldStop? })` reads each distinct sentence once, always reads the first `CORRECTION_SENTENCES_MIN = 8` (a wall-clock budget spent on a slow runner must not drop a short prompt's second correction), and past them stops reading sentences when `shouldStop` is true, which the hook sets `HOOK_CORRECTION_BUDGET_MS = 25` ms after it starts; when it does stop, the budget note `formatBudgetNote(budgetMs)` is added to the parts, even when nothing changed), `HOOK_PROMPT_MAX_CHARS = 50_000` (UserPromptSubmit checks only this many characters of the prompt, cut after the last whitespace in the final 200 and never inside a surrogate pair; correction parsing and matching see only that head, the unchecked tail is appended to the result's `output` unchanged, and the length note `formatLengthNote(checkedChars, totalChars)`, exported, is added after the corrections note even when nothing changed: the work before the match budget is linear in the whole prompt, so a 10 MB paste took 49 s), `HOOK_LOCK_TIMEOUT_MS = 50` (passed as `lockTimeoutMs` to `queueCorrections` and to the background `recordHits`: with the store's ten second default, a lock another lexicon process held kept the prompt, or the hook's process, waiting past the plugin's five second hook timeout; a correction not queued in time is one stderr line, a hit not counted is silent), `CORRECTED_PROMPT_MAX_CHARS = 8000` (a longer corrected prompt is not repeated back: the note is the diff summary alone), `CORRECTION_LIST_MAX_CHARS = 4000` (`capCorrectionList(summary, max?)`, exported: a diff summary longer than this has identical lines merged with a count, `<line> (N times)`, keeps lines from the start while they fit and ends with `... N more corrections not listed; call the lexicon normalize_transcript tool on the prompt for the full list.`; a shorter one is returned unchanged).
- `runHook(input: string, opts?: HookOptions): Promise<string>` is the dispatcher `main()` uses: `SessionStart` -> `runSessionStartHook`; `UserPromptSubmit` or a missing event name -> `runUserPromptSubmitHook`. Returns the JSON to print or `''` (print nothing). Any other event name (a non-string included) writes one stderr line (`[lexicon hook] unknown hook_event_name "<name>"; nothing to do ...`, the name through `sanitizeForDisplayMax(_, 60)`) and returns `''`, even when the payload carries a `prompt`; a payload that is valid JSON but not an object (array, string, number, boolean, null), in any of the three entry points, writes `[lexicon hook] the payload is <kind>, not a JSON object; nothing to do` and is handled as `{}`; input that is not JSON at all writes `[lexicon hook] stdin is not valid JSON (<n> bytes); expected the Claude Code hook payload object, nothing to do` (the input itself is never quoted) and is handled as `{}` too, where it used to throw. A UserPromptSubmit payload whose `prompt` is present but not a string writes `[lexicon hook] the payload's prompt is <kind>, not a string; nothing to do` (the type only, never the value) and prints nothing; a missing or blank prompt stays silent. Stdout is unchanged by any of them.
- Both events resolve the project lexicon from `opts.cwd`, else the payload's `cwd` when it is a non-empty string, else `process.cwd()`; a `cwd` of any other type (or blank) is ignored with one stderr line. When `loadLexicon` throws, the event's only part is a note: for a store parse/validation error (`Failed to parse lexicon YAML at` / `Invalid lexicon at`) it says the file does not parse and names `lexicon edit`; for any other error it says the lexicon could not be read and names `lexicon doctor`. The error is quoted with the file a parse error names through `pathForModel` and cut to `LOAD_ERROR_NOTE_MAX_CHARS` (1500, exported), so the note stays within `SESSION_CONTEXT_MAX_CHARS` on SessionStart.
- `runUserPromptSubmitHook(input, opts?)` reads `{ prompt, cwd, ... }`, runs normalize, and prints `{"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"UserPromptSubmit","additionalContext":"<parts joined by \n>"}}` when any part exists. Parts, in order: the corrections note (`formatAdditionalContext(result)`: diff summary (through `capCorrectionList`) + corrected prompt, the latter only up to `CORRECTED_PROMPT_MAX_CHARS`; every replacement and matched original first goes through `neutralizeForModel`, a replacement that starts a line of the corrected prompt with a markdown block marker (`#`, a backtick, `~`, `|`, or three of `-`, `=`, `*`, `_`) gets a backslash before it there, and the user's own text between replacements is echoed as typed) when normalize changed something; the length note (`formatLengthNote`) when the prompt is longer than `HOOK_PROMPT_MAX_CHARS`; the budget note (`formatBudgetNote(budgetMs)`, exported: matching stopped at the budget so later parts were not checked, call `normalize_transcript` on a part that looks garbled) when `shouldStop` returned true; the correction notes (`formatCorrectionNotes(corrections)`, exported: one `formatCorrectionNote(correction)` line per correction up to `CORRECTION_NOTES_MAX` (5), then `... N more spelling corrections in this message not listed; ask the user which to record before calling learn_correction for them.`) for the corrections `parseCorrections(prompt, lexicon?): Correction[]` finds (exported: `parseCorrection` of the whole prompt, with `lexicon` (the hook passes the merged lexicon) as its `opts.lexicon`, then of each sentence (split after `.`, `!` or `?` and whitespace), both sides non-empty, duplicates by case-insensitive heard and meant dropped, and a contrast of two ordinary words dropped: one where neither side is quoted (`"x"`, U+201C/U+201D or backticks) in the sentence the correction was read from or holds an uppercase letter, digit, `.` or `-`, and the sides (letters and digits, lowercased) are less similar than `CORRECTION_MIN_SIMILARITY` (0.45) and do not share a phonetic key, so "it's flaky not broken" gets no learn_correction line and is not queued while "it's vercel not versel" still is; the prompt is read with the ranges `collectRanges` returns (fenced and indented code, HTML code elements, URLs, emails, paths) blanked to spaces, an inline code span only when its own text parses as a correction (`` it's `Vercel` not versel `` keeps its quoted side), and a side that reads as code (a call such as `toBe(`, a brace, `;` or `=`) is dropped; quoted material is blanked the same way: Markdown blockquote lines (up to three spaces, then `>`) and the text after a verb of showing or saying and a colon (`says:`, `reads:`, `shows:`, `printed:` and the like, not `I said:`) up to the end of its sentence or line, so `> it's Vercel not versel` and `The banner says: it's Vercel not versel.` are not corrections), each line reading: `The user is correcting a spelling: "<heard>" should be "<meant>". Call the lexicon learn_correction tool with heard: "<heard>", meant: "<meant>". If "<heard>" contains words that are not part of the misspelled name, pass only the name. Then continue with the rest of the message.` (each `"<heard>"` / `"<meant>"` is `JSON.stringify` of the trimmed spelling, so a `"` or `\` inside it is escaped; a plain spelling reads exactly as shown) (the hook never writes to the lexicon).
