For each correction detected the normalize result first passes through `dropCorrectionSpans(result, correction): NormalizeResult` (exported, pure): every replacement whose span overlaps a case-insensitive occurrence of `heard` or `meant` in the prompt, or whose original text equals one of them, is dropped, `output` and `changed` are rebuilt from the rest, and hits are recorded only for the remaining canonicals; the same object is returned when nothing overlaps; after the correction pass the result goes through `dropDataBlockSpans(result)` (exported, pure): every replacement inside a span from `dataBlockSpans(input)` is dropped the same way and records no hit. `dataBlockSpans(text): [start, end)[]` (exported) marks lines that read as pasted dictionary or CSV data: a header found by `DATA_HEADER_RE` (`word,` / `canonical,` / `original,` / `shortcut,` followed by a non-space, at the line start or after whitespace, `:`, `;` or `(`; the span starts at the token so a prose prefix such as `import this: word,replacement` stays correctable) together with the following consecutive rows with the same comma count, or any run of at least `DATA_BLOCK_MIN_ROWS` (3) consecutive rows with the same comma count; a row is a line with one or more commas, none with whitespace on either side and none at either end (`versel,Vercel`, `a,b,brand`), so prose commas (`ping X, then`) never count. The skipped-project note (`formatSkippedProjectNote(loaded)`, one line naming the path through `pathForModel` (at most 120 characters, its last three components), never the contents, telling the model to review through `trust_project` `status` or `lexicon trust` and not to open the file with Read or cat, and to trust only after the user says yes to the preview) when `loadLexicon` reports `skippedProject`.
- `runSessionStartHook(input, opts?)` when the merged lexicon has >= 1 term prints `{"hookSpecificOutput":{"hookEventName":"SessionStart","additionalContext":"<claude-md export>[\n<skipped-project note>]"}}`, the export through `neutralizeMarkup`. With 0 terms it prints the onboarding note `ONBOARD_NOTE` (one line: the lexicon is empty; offer setup, do not run it unasked; ask for (1) company/product names spelled exactly and their pronunciation, (2) the user's own name, (3) which of Claude Code, Claude Desktop, Codex, Cursor, Windsurf, Gemini CLI, VS Code they use; then call `setup_lexicon` with company, person and clients, or the onboard prompt; followed by the skipped-project note when there is one) at most once per `ONBOARD_NOTE_INTERVAL_MS` (24 h); between notes it prints the skipped-project note alone when there is one, else `''`. The gate is `shouldEmitOnboardNote(globalPath, now?)`: reads `onboardNotePath(globalPath)` = `<dirname(globalPath)>/onboard-note.json` (`{ lastNotedAt: ISO }`), returns false inside the interval, otherwise writes the new timestamp (mkdir -p) and returns true, or false when it cannot be written (a read-only config directory; the error goes to stderr), so the note is not repeated every session; a missing or corrupt state file counts as never noted. The note is never emitted when terms exist. The export is capped by `exportClaudeMdWithin(merged, room, more)`, `room` being `SESSION_CONTEXT_MAX_CHARS` less the trailing notes: rows are taken most used first (`sortByImportance`, as `lexicon://me` keeps them; a row too long to fit is skipped and later rows still count) and written in the export's order, and a line `... N more terms; read the lexicon://me resource for more, or call the list_terms tool to look any term up.` is inserted before the footer. `truncateSessionContext(text, max = SESSION_CONTEXT_MAX_CHARS)` stays exported: it keeps the rows that fit in the order given, with the same line (text without a table, or a header and footer over the cap, is hard-cut with `...` to at most `max` characters, never inside a surrogate pair). Untrusted content never appears: the skipped file contributes its path only.
- `decodeStdin(bytes: Buffer): string` (exported; `main()` decodes stdin with it before `runHook`): a UTF-16LE (FF FE) or UTF-16BE (FE FF) byte order mark picks that encoding; bytes without one that hold a NUL (UTF-8 JSON never does) are UTF-16, little-endian when more NULs in the first 4 KB sit at odd offsets, else big-endian; an odd trailing byte is dropped; a UTF-8 byte order mark is dropped; anything else is UTF-8. Text that still reads as UTF-16 decoded as UTF-8 (two leading U+FFFD, or a NUL) and is not JSON gets `[lexicon hook] stdin looks like UTF-16 read as UTF-8 (<n> bytes, NUL bytes between the characters); send the hook payload as UTF-8, nothing to do` instead of the not-JSON line.
- `HookOptions.startRefresh?: (cwd) => Promise<void>` (`main()` passes `spawnCacheRefresh`; `runHook` and the other entry points without it parse as before). With it, UserPromptSubmit loads with `staleGlobal: true`; on `globalStale` it calls `claimCacheRefresh(globalPath, now?)` (exported): `'claimed'` when it created `<dirname(global)>/index-refresh.lock` (`REFRESH_LOCK_FILE`, `wx`, mode 0600), then `startRefresh(cwd)`; `'running'` when the lock is younger than `REFRESH_LOCK_STALE_MS` (30000); `'unavailable'` when the lock cannot be created or is older (it is removed). Claimed or running, the prompt is matched against the previous parse (and its cached index) and `STALE_LEXICON_NOTE` is the last part of the context when there is any other part; SessionStart takes the same path and lists the previous parse, with `STALE_SESSION_NOTE` after the table (inside `SESSION_CONTEXT_MAX_CHARS`, the table gives up room for it as for the skipped-project note); when the load carries `globalStaleProblem`, both events put `formatStaleProblemNote(problem)` (exported: `the last edit to the lexicon file looks broken (<problem>), so the previous version is in use`, the `lexicon edit` step, and that the next prompt reports the full check) in place of the stale note, and UserPromptSubmit emits it even when nothing else is said; unavailable, or a `startRefresh` that throws (one stderr line, the lock released), loads again without `staleGlobal`. `spawnCacheRefresh(cwd)` starts `node [execArgv without --inspect*] <this module> --refresh-cache <cwd>` (`REFRESH_CACHE_FLAG`) detached with stdio ignored and returns; `main(['--refresh-cache', cwd])` runs `refreshHookCaches(cwd)` (exported: `loadLexicon({ cwd, parseSkipped: false })`, which rewrites the parse cache or records the failure, then `loadMatcherIndex` when there are terms, then removes the lock; never throws), reads no stdin and prints nothing.
- `HOOK_STDIN_MAX_BYTES = 16 * 1024 * 1024` and `readStdin(max?, source?: StdinSource { readSync(buffer); stream() }): Promise<StdinRead>` (exported; `main()` uses it with fd 0 and `process.stdin`): reads synchronously first, then from the stream, and stops as soon as the total passes `max`, returning `{ overflow: <bytes read> }` instead of `{ bytes }`. `main()` then writes `formatStdinOverflow(read, max?)` (`[lexicon hook] stdin is over <max> bytes (stopped reading at <read>); a Claude Code hook payload is far smaller, nothing to do`) to stderr, prints nothing and exits 0.
- `writeStdout(text, sink?: StdoutSink { writeSync; stream; stderr })` (exported; `main()` uses it with process stdout): `writeSync(1, ...)` first; on `EAGAIN` the rest goes through the stream with a one-shot `'error'` listener; any other sync error (`EPIPE`: the reader is gone) and any later stream error print one `[lexicon hook] could not write the hook output (<code>); the prompt goes through without it` line on stderr and nothing more, so the process still exits 0.
- `main(argv = process.argv.slice(2))` and `hookFlagOutput(argv): string | undefined`: `--version`/`-v` prints the package version (`packageVersion(import.meta.url)`) and `--help`/`-h` a one-line usage, on stdout with exit 0 and without reading stdin, the same flags `lexicon-mcp` answers; any other argv (Claude Code passes none) reads the payload. `lexicon hook` calls `main([])`.
- `src/hooks/plugin-entry.ts` (bundled as `plugin/hook.mjs`, the file Claude Code runs): calls `module.enableCompileCache(<dirname(globalLexiconPath())>/node-compile-cache)` when Node has it (>= 22.1; `COMPILE_CACHE_DIR_NAME = 'node-compile-cache'`; never the shared temp directory; failures ignored; `NODE_DISABLE_COMPILE_CACHE=1` is Node's own off switch), then imports `./hook-main.mjs` and awaits its `main()`. A bundle that is missing or fails to load prints `lexicon hook: <error>` and the next step (`claude plugin update lexicon@ashlrai`, or `npm install -g @ashlr/lexicon` then `lexicon install claude --apply`) on stderr; every path exits 0.
- `hooks/hooks.json` registers `plugin/hook.mjs` for both events; `lexicon install-claude --apply` merges both into `~/.claude/settings.json`.

## src/mcp (server.ts, shared.ts, tools/)  (bin: lexicon-mcp; bundled as plugin/mcp-server.mjs)

`server.ts` exports only `ServerOptions`, `ROOTS_TIMEOUT_MS`, `cwdFromRoots`, `dirsFromRoots` (every `file:` root naming an existing directory, in order; `cwdFromRoots` is its first), `createServer`, `main` and `cliFlagOutput(argv): string | undefined` (the `--version`/`-v` package version or the `--help`/`-h` one-line usage, printed to stdout with exit 0 instead of starting the stdio server; undefined means run the server); it owns the server object, the three resources, the two prompts and the stdio wiring. The twenty-one tools live in `tools/*.ts`, one registrar per module, collected in registration order by `TOOL_REGISTRARS` in `tools/index.ts`:

| module | registrar | tools |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `tools/terms.ts` | `registerTermTools` | `normalize_transcript`, `add_term`, `remove_term`, `list_terms`, `export_lexicon`, `learn_correction`, `suggest_canonical`, `lexicon_stats` |
| `tools/harvest.ts` | `registerHarvestTools` | `harvest_repo`, `suggest_terms`, `apply_suggestion` |
| `tools/trust.ts` | `registerTrustTools` | `trust_project` |
| `tools/setup.ts` | `registerSetupTools` | `lexicon_doctor`, `install_client`, `uninstall_client`, `setup_lexicon`, `serve_status` |
| `tools/packs.ts` | `registerPackTools` | `list_packs`, `add_pack`, `import_vocabulary`, `import_dictionary` |

`scripts/check-facts.mjs` asks the built server for this list over stdio and fails if a doc states a different count, so add the row here when a tool is added.

`shared.ts` is what every tool module imports: `ToolContext { readonly cwd; readonly roots; load(); readonly normalizeBudgetMs; readonly normalizeMaxChars }` (`cwd` and `roots` are live getters: read them inside a handler, never at registration; `roots` is every directory from the client's last `roots/list` answer, empty without one), `HARVEST_ROOTS_ENV = 'LEXICON_HARVEST_ROOTS'` (absolute directories a human lists in the MCP server's env, separated by the platform path delimiter), `readableRoots(ctx, env?): string[]` (the server directory, the git root of its project file, `ctx.roots` and `$LEXICON_HARVEST_ROOTS`, each realpath'd, or resolved when missing), `containedPath(ctx, path, cliArgs, env?): string` (the realpath of `path` resolved against `ctx.cwd` when it lies inside one of `readableRoots`; otherwise throws `"<path>" is outside the directories this server may read (...), so nothing was read.` naming `cliCommand(cliArgs(realpath))`, a client root and `LEXICON_HARVEST_ROOTS` as the ways out, or `"<path>" does not exist ...`; symlinks are resolved before the check), `shellWord(dir)` (one shell word for a path in such a command), `mcpWriteOpts(ctx): Promise<{ leaveUntrusted?: true }>` (`{}` when `projectWriteTrusted({ cwd })`, else `{ leaveUntrusted: true }`; every tool that writes a term spreads it into its store options, so a model never creates or trusts a project file, while a trusted one keeps its pin refreshed), `untrustedWriteFields(path, what, plural?): { trusted: false; nextStep }` (the `nextStep` every such write returns: `<what> was|were written to <path>, which is not trusted, so it does|they do not apply yet. Ask the user to review the file and run \`lexicon trust\` ... or call trust_project with action "trust" ...`, with `cliCommand('trust')`), `ToolRegistrar = (server, ctx) => void`, `SERVER_NAME = 'lexicon'`, `SERVE_HEALTH_URL`, `bufferIO()` (an `IO` that collects, so a CLI handler's multi-line report becomes one tool result), `textResult(payload)`, `guarded(fn)`, `withLexiconFix(message): string` (a store `Failed to parse lexicon YAML at` / `Invalid lexicon at` message, which names the file, plus a second line: nothing was read or changed, ask the user to fix the file with `unreadableLexiconEdit(message)` from cli/lexicon-edit-hint.ts (also re-exported by cli/io.ts; `lexicon edit`, or `lexicon edit --project` for a `.lexicon.yaml`), then check it with `lexicon doctor` or `lexicon_doctor`; any other message unchanged), `trustPreview(file)` (delegates to core/trust.ts after `loadCore()` resolves), `filePathsInUse(loaded)`, `log(...)`, `readPackageVersion()`, `cliDirForInstall()`, and the zod enums `INSTALL_CLIENT_VALUES`, `INSTALL_SCOPES`, `IMPORT_FORMAT_VALUES`, `SUGGESTION_KINDS`. The package lookup helper `findPackage()` is internal to this module.

- `createServer(opts?: ServerOptions { cwd?; rootsTimeoutMs?; normalizeBudgetMs?; normalizeMaxChars? }): McpServer`, `main()`. `withAnnotations(name, config)` gives every tool its `annotations` from `TOOL_ANNOTATIONS` (src/mcp/annotations.ts: `readOnlyHint`, `destructiveHint`, `idempotentHint` and `openWorldHint`, all four stated, covering the tool's most far-reaching call) unless the config names its own, and throws for a tool the table does not list (`toolAnnotations(name)`), so a new tool cannot register without them. `strictInput(config)` replaces a tool's raw `inputSchema` shape with `z.object(shape).strict()` at registration, so an undeclared argument is a validation error and `tools/list` shows `additionalProperties: false`; `nonBlank()` (shared.ts) is `z.string().min(1).regex(NOT_BLANK_RE)` (a character other than whitespace and the BMP format characters, spelled out without `\p{Cf}` so a client compiling the published pattern without the `u` flag reads it the same; whitespace, U+200B and the BOM alone are blank, as the lexicon schema trims them) for required strings that must not be whitespace, and for the optional `harvest_repo` `path`, `suggest_terms` `cwd`, `trust_project` `path` and `list_packs` / `add_pack` `registry`, where a blank value was read as omitted (`remove_term` `canonical` and `alias`, `suggest_canonical` `heard`, among others; `learn_correction` `heard` and `meant` make the same check after their missing-spelling message), each refusal `must not be blank at <argument>`. Project directory: `opts.cwd`, else a non-blank `$LEXICON_CWD`, else the client's MCP roots, else `process.cwd()`. With neither explicit, a client that advertises the `roots` capability is asked `roots/list` once initialized and again on every `notifications/roots/list_changed`; `cwdFromRoots(roots): string | undefined` takes the first `file:` root that names an existing directory. The request is bounded by `rootsTimeoutMs` (`ROOTS_TIMEOUT_MS`, 2000); a failure, a timeout or no usable root keeps the current directory and logs to stderr. `load()` and every tool handler (registration is wrapped) wait for an in-flight roots request, so the first resource or tool call after initialize already sees the root and one call never mixes two directories. Clients launch servers in their own directory (Claude Desktop, Cursor), which is why the roots matter. Server name `lexicon`, stdio transport, version from `readPackageVersion()` (`findPackage()` looks for `package.json` two levels up for dist/src, then one level up for plugin/, accepting only `@ashlr/lexicon`; the same lookup gives `cliDirForInstall()` = `<package root>/dist/cli`, which the install helpers derive the package root from). Instantiated once; reads the lexicon fresh on every call; logs to stderr only. Sends `SERVER_INSTRUCTIONS` at initialize (read lexicon://me first, normalize dictated input, learn corrections, suggest before guessing, never rewrite code, offer setup_lexicon when the lexicon is empty after asking for the company spelling, the user's name and the clients, preview then apply with `clients`, `harvest: true` and `serve: true` only for what was agreed, lexicon_doctor when corrections stop, suggest_terms + apply_suggestion to improve, preview install_client before applying, never trust a project file unseen). The `trust_project` description adds that it replaces reading `.lexicon.yaml` (sanitized preview, notes never quoted, do not open the file with Read or cat).
- The agent-native tools dynamically import CLI handlers inside their tool bodies. `shared.ts` exports `loadCore(): Promise<typeof import('../core/index.js')>`, memoized across calls; `core()` returns that module only after loading and throws otherwise. `coreFn(name)` binds a core export without loading it at registration. Keep the core barrel and CLI handlers out of the static MCP graph: mixing static and dynamic imports of the heavy graph can make esbuild wrap shared zod classes and break `plugin/mcp-server.mjs`. Dependency-free shell quoting is shared through `util/shell.ts` and remains synchronous.
- Every tool body runs inside `guarded()`, which awaits `loadCore()` before the body and turns a thrown error into an `isError` result (its text through `withLexiconFix`). The resources and the `voice-context` prompt load the core and lexicon through `loadForContext()`, which rethrows a broken-file error as `new Error(withLexiconFix(message))`, so the JSON-RPC error names the file and the fix too. Tools:
  - `normalize_transcript({ text, dryRun?, minConfidence? })` -> `{ output, changed, replacements[], summary, incomplete?, projectTrust?, skippedProject?, note? }` (the last three, as in `list_terms`, only when a project lexicon was skipped: its path, never its contents); on a real change calls `recordHits` for the matched canonicals (best effort). `text` over `MCP_NORMALIZE_INPUT_MAX_CHARS` (1000000, exported; serve's /normalize body limit is 1 MB) is refused by the schema, nothing checked, with an error saying to call again in pieces. Matches against `loadMatcherIndex(loaded)` (the on-disk index cache the hook and serve share), only the first `normalizeMaxChars` characters (`MCP_NORMALIZE_MAX_CHARS`, 100000; the cut backs up to whitespace within 64 characters, `normalizeCut(text, max)`) and for at most `normalizeBudgetMs` (`MCP_NORMALIZE_BUDGET_MS`, 1000, the `shouldStop` budget serve uses). The unchecked rest is appended unchanged. When either limit is hit, `incomplete: { reasons: ('length' | 'time')[], checkedChars?, totalChars, note }` says so; otherwise the field is absent. `tools/terms.ts` also exports `MCP_NORMALIZE_BUDGET_MS`, `MCP_NORMALIZE_MAX_CHARS` and `normalizeCut`.
  - `add_term({ canonical (nonBlank), aliases?, phonetic?, category?, notes?, never?, scope? })` -> `{ term, path, created, unchanged?, conflicts?, note?, trusted?, nextStep? }` (`unchanged: true` and `note: unchangedTermNote(canonical)`, `Nothing was added: "<canonical>" already has every alias and field given.`, when `addTerm` reports `unchanged`, the note only when there is no conflict; never for a project file left untrusted); if aliases omitted, uses `suggestAliases`; an explicit `aliases: []` passes no `defaultAliases`, so the term gets none. Writes with `mcpWriteOpts(ctx)`: when that left a project file untrusted the result adds `trusted: false` and `nextStep` (`untrustedWriteFields`), and when the term already existed in that file `term` (and the `conflicts` check) is the term this call passed, not the file's copy. `conflicts` = `spellingConflicts(merged, term.aliases, term.canonical)` after the write, present only when non-empty, with a `note` naming each `remove_term` call (JSON string literals); the other term is never edited.
  - `remove_term({ canonical, alias?, scope? })`. Without `alias`: `removeTerm` -> `{ canonical, removed }` (after a removal `canonical` is the spelling the file held, `Yod` for a call with `yod`, as `lexicon remove` reports it); when nothing was removed (not an error, `isError: false`, unlike `lexicon remove`, which exits 1; the tool description says so) the result adds `note`, and `foundIn: 'project' | 'global'` when the term is in the other file than the requested `scope` (the note then says to call again with that scope, or, for a term in an untrusted project file, that the user must `lexicon trust` it first; only presence of the passed name is reported, never that file's contents). When it was removed, `termHolders` is read before and after: the result adds `path` (the file it was removed from) and, when another trusted file still holds the term, `stillIn: { scope, path }[]` and a `note` naming the `scope` to pass to remove that copy too. When the skipped project file holds the term (`untrustedTermHolder`), the result adds `untrustedProject: { path, trust }` and the `note` says that file was neither loaded nor edited and its copy corrects again once trusted. Without `scope`, a `ProjectTrustError` (the untrusted project copy comes first) is an error whose text adds the `scope: "global"` call when the global file holds the term too. With `alias`: `removeAlias` -> `{ canonical, alias, removed: true, aliases, paths, untrustedProject?, note? }`, where `canonical` is the stored spelling, `aliases` what the term keeps and `paths` every file edited; `untrustedProject` and a `note` when the skipped project file's copy of the term holds that alias (`untrustedAliasHolder`); an unknown term or alias is an error (`isError: true`) with the `removeAlias` message (with a `scope` whose file lacks the term or the alias while the other loaded file's copy has it, the text adds `It is an alias of "<canonical>" in the <scope> lexicon (<path>); call remove_term again with scope "<scope>"...`; the untrusted project file is never consulted for this), and without `scope` a `ProjectTrustError` is an error whose text adds the `alias` and `scope: "global"` call when the global file holds the term.
  - `list_terms({ query?, category?, offset?, limit? })` -> `{ terms, counts: { matched, total, global, project }, paths, page?, projectTrust?, skippedProject?, note? }` (skipped file reported by path only; `category` counts a term without one as `other`, as `lexicon list --category` does). Every match is returned unless `offset` (an integer >= 0) or `limit` (>= 1) is given; then `terms` is that slice of the matches and `page: TermPage { offset, limit?, returned, nextOffset? }` is added, `nextOffset` only while matches remain past the page; `counts.matched` still counts every match. An `offset` at or past the last match gives empty `terms` and `offsetPastEndNote` as `note`. `lexicon list --full --offset --limit` prints the same object after `ok`.
  - `harvest_repo({ path?, limit?, minCount?, add? })` -> `{ root, candidates, count, added?, failures?, path?, trusted?, nextStep?, truncated?, note? }` (`truncated: true` when the scan stopped at `HARVEST_MAX_FILES`, with `harvestTruncatedNote(root)` in `note`, after the failures sentence when both apply); with `add: true` the file it would write (`resolvePaths({ cwd: root }).project ?? defaultProjectPath(root)`) must pass `writableProjectFile(ctx, file, env?): boolean` (exported from `shared.ts`: the server's own project file, or inside `readableRoots`, symlinks resolved), else it throws `add: true would write <file>, which is outside the directories this server may write (...), so nothing was read or written.` before scanning; an explicit `path` goes through `containedPath` (the error names `lexicon harvest <dir>` and its npx form) before anything is read. With `add: true` writes to the harvested repo's own project lexicon (`cwd: root`) with `leaveUntrusted`, so the model's write never trusts the file; once a candidate was written the result adds `path` (that file), `trusted: false` and a `nextStep` asking the user to review it and run `lexicon trust` (`cliCommand('trust')`) or answer `trust_project` `'trust'`; a `ProjectTrustError` is surfaced once as the tool error instead of per candidate; any other failed write is a `failures` row plus a `note` counting them, and when no candidate was written at all the same payload comes back with `isError: true`.
  - `export_lexicon({ format, categories?, limit? })` (`limit` at most `EXPORT_MAX_LIMIT`; `harvest_repo` takes `limit` up to `HARVEST_MAX_LIMIT` and `suggest_terms` up to `SUGGEST_MAX_LIMIT`, refused at the argument above them) -> the export as text in the first content block. A second text block, `Note (not part of the export): ...`, is added only when the export is empty (no terms, none matched `categories`, or an `EXPORT_ALIAS_ROW_FORMATS` format where no exported term has an alias: `No exported term has an alias, and <format> writes one row per alias, ...`) or an untrusted or changed project `.lexicon.yaml` was left out (the `skippedProjectFields` note plus `Its terms are not in this export.`).
  - `learn_correction({ heard, meant, scope? })` -> `{ term, path, created, aliasAdded, reversed?, conflict?, untrustedProject?, trusted?, nextStep?, summary }` (`reversed: true` and the `'mcp'` `reversedCorrectionNote` as `summary` for `LearnResult.reversed`, except in a project file left untrusted). Calls `learnCorrection` with `mcpWriteOpts(ctx)`; a project file left untrusted adds `trusted: false` and `nextStep` (also appended to `summary`), and a merge into a term that file already had reports `term` as `{ canonical, aliases: [heard] }` (or `[]` when no alias was added). With `conflict` the summary adds that the other term may still win and, for `as: 'alias'`, the `remove_term` call (canonical, alias, scope; canonical and alias as `JSON.stringify` literals) that lets the correction take effect. With `untrustedProject` it adds that the untrusted project file (by path) also claims `heard` and would win once trusted, and points at `lexicon trust` / `trust_project` `status`, not Read or cat.
  - `suggest_canonical({ heard })` -> `{ heard, suggestions: { canonical, confidence, aliases, category? }[], projectTrust?, skippedProject?, note? }` (the last three as in `list_terms`, path only, when a project lexicon was skipped). A `heard` longer than `MAX_CORRECTION_LENGTH` (60) after whitespace is squashed is an error, before the lexicon is read: scoring is linear in its length for every alias, and `learn_correction` would refuse it.
  - `lexicon_stats({})` -> `LexiconStats & { projectTrust?, skippedProject?, note? }` (the last three as in `list_terms`, path only, when a project lexicon was skipped).
  - Agent-native (see `docs/AGENT-NATIVE.md`):
  - `lexicon_doctor({})` -> `DoctorReport` from `runDoctorReport({ cwd, audience: 'mcp' })` (src/cli/commands.ts).
  - `install_client({ client: 'claude'|'codex'|'cursor'|'windsurf'|'gemini'|'vscode'|'claude-desktop', apply?, scope?: 'user'|'project' })` -> `{ client, scope, applied, ok, written, output, reload?, stderr?, next? }` (`reload` is `reloadStepFor(client)` when `ok`); runs `runInstall` (cmd-install.ts) with a buffered `IO`, `cliDir` from `cliDirForInstall()` and an `onWritten` that collects `written: { file, outcome }[]` (the list `lexicon install --json` returns; empty for a preview); `apply` omitted or false is a preview that writes nothing (`next` tells the model to confirm with the user first).
  - `uninstall_client({ client: 'claude'|'codex'|'cursor'|'windsurf'|'gemini'|'vscode'|'claude-desktop', apply?, scope?: 'user'|'project' })` -> `{ client, scope, applied, ok, changes: UninstallChange[], output, stderr?, next?, nextStep? }`; runs `runUninstall` (cmd-uninstall.ts) with `json: true`, `dryRun: apply !== true` and a buffered `IO`, and returns its `changes` and `output`. `apply` omitted or false is a dry run that writes nothing (the agent-facing safety default; `next` says to confirm with the user). A non-zero exit with `changes` is `ok: false` plus `nextStep`; a refusal (`{ ok: false, error }` with no `changes`) is a tool error, the CLI's `drop --project` rephrased as calling again without scope.
  - `trust_project({ action: 'status'|'trust'|'untrust', path? })`; `path` resolves against `cwd`, default `resolvePaths({ cwd }).project`, and a directory means the `.lexicon.yaml` inside it. For `status` and `trust` an explicit `path` goes through `containedPath` (the error names `lexicon trust <path>` and its npx form) before anything is read: an existing path is checked at its realpath, the `.lexicon.yaml` inside a directory again when it exists, and a missing file by its directory, so `status` can still answer `'missing'` (when that directory is missing too, the error names the `path` given, `"<path>" does not exist (resolved from <cwd>); nothing was read.`, as `harvest_repo` does); `untrust` only removes a registry entry and takes any path. A `trust.json` that cannot be used (`trustRegistryProblem`) makes `status` an error naming the registry, its problem and the fix (fix the JSON or move it aside), not an empty `trusted` list. `status` -> `{ action, registry, trustAll, trusted: TrustListEntry[], path?, status: TrustStatus|'none'|'missing'|'invalid', termCount?, aliasCount?, termsWithNotes?, sample?, more?, fullPreview?, error?, nextStep? }` (the counts, `sample`, `more` and `fullPreview` are `trustSummaryForModel(file)`; every path and error string through `sanitizeForDisplay` and `neutralizeMarkup`) (`nextStep` with `status: 'invalid'`: ask the user to fix the file, `lexicon edit --project` for a `.lexicon.yaml`, check it with `lexicon doctor`, and not to open it yourself); `trust` parses the file (invalid -> error `refusing to trust an invalid lexicon: ...` followed by that same next step, missing -> error), refuses a `trust.json` that cannot be used (error naming it, nothing asked or written), takes the hash of the parsed bytes (`parsedContentHash`), and unless it is already trusted asks the user through MCP elicitation (`elicitInput`, message naming the path, term count and sanitized canonicals; boolean `trust` field). A client without the elicitation capability gets an error telling the user to run `lexicon trust`; decline, cancel or `trust: false` -> `{ action, path, status, result: 'declined', summary }` with nothing written; a file whose hash changed after the prompt -> error, nothing trusted; a file that no longer holds the parsed bytes when it is pinned (`trustProject` with `expectedSha256`, on a re-pin too) errors with nothing written, and a pin whose sha256 still differs is revoked (`untrustProject`). Then `trustProject` -> `{ action, path, previousStatus, status: 'trusted', result: 'trusted'|'updated'|'re-pinned'|'already-trusted'` (`already-trusted` when `trustProject` found the same pin and wrote nothing), `sha256 (12 chars), trustedAt, registry, termCount, aliasCount, termsWithNotes, sample, more, fullPreview, note }` (`trustSummaryForModel(file)`); `untrust` -> `{ action, path, removed, registry, summary }`. No alias or note is ever quoted to the model; the elicitation message, which the user answers, names the first 25 sanitized canonicals from `trustPreview(file)`.
  - `list_packs({ registry? })` lists vendored packs and optionally community index entries with installed flags, read from the trusted files only; a skipped project lexicon adds `projectTrust`, `skippedProject` (path only) and a `note` saying a pack only that file records shows as not installed; `registryMismatch { fileOnly, recordOnly }` (`findRegistryRecordMismatch`) when a file's `settings.packs` and the registry records disagree, as `lexicon pack list --json` reports it. `add_pack({ name (nonBlank); scope?; registry?; confirm?; previewDigest? })` retains the starter-pack flow and an install answers `{ name, title, added, merged, alreadyInstalled, path, scope, summary }` (`alreadyInstalled` true and `summary` = `packAlreadyInstalledNote` when the file was left as it was); a vendored name with `registry`, `previewDigest` or `confirm: false` is refused before any write. A community ref requires its registry: preview returns every term field (including notes, phonetic, never and caseSensitive) plus a digest binding raw bytes, entry metadata and destination. Confirmation requires that same digest; changed metadata/bytes/scope or an absent digest is rejected before installation. The installer receives the exact checksum-verified bytes from that approved preview, preventing a second download from changing what is written. With `scope: 'project'` both installs pass `mcpWriteOpts(ctx)`; when that is `leaveUntrusted` the result adds `trusted: false` and `nextStep` (`untrustedWriteFields`).
  - `import_vocabulary({ sources?, apply?, scope?, previewToken?, approvedCandidateIds? })`: omitted sources returns source descriptions without availability probes or harvesting. Explicit chosen sources produce candidate data/ids and a previewToken in process memory (five-minute lifetime, maximum20 pending previews). Applying requires the same sources/scope/destination and an explicit approved id list. Unknown ids, expired tokens and destination changes fail before writes. `previewToken` or `approvedCandidateIds` without `apply: true` fails before any probe or harvest and leaves the saved preview in place. Only the saved approved candidates are written; apply never re-harvests. A token is consumed before writing; after a failure or restart, preview again. No preview names/tokens are persisted. A project-scope apply passes `mcpWriteOpts(ctx)` to `applyImportCandidates`; when that is `leaveUntrusted` and a file was written the result adds `trusted: false` and `nextStep`.
  - `import_dictionary({ path? (nonBlank), content? (nonBlank, max MAX_IMPORT_BYTES), format?: ImportFormat (default 'auto'), scope?, dryRun? })` -> the `ImportReport` JSON `runImport` (cmd-import.ts) prints with `json: true`, without its leading `ok: true` (`content` is fed through the injected reader with `file: path ?? '-'`, so a `path` given alongside `content` only serves format detection; a `path` without `content`, which is read, goes through `containedPath` first, the error naming `lexicon import <file>`); throws `pass path or content: path is a dictionary file in this workspace, content the dictionary text itself (format defaults to auto); nothing was imported` when neither is given and relays the CLI's stderr when it exits non-zero. A project-scope call passes `leaveUntrusted` from `mcpWriteOpts(ctx)`, a dry run included; when it applies and a file was written the result adds `trusted: false` and `nextStep`, and for a dry run `trusted: false` and a `note` (the untrusted file is not read, so the rows carry no `created`).
  - `suggest_terms({ cwd?, limit? })` -> `TermSuggestion[]` from `suggestTerms({ loaded, history: await loadVoiceHistory(loaded.global.path), cwd, limit? })`; a failing `loadVoiceHistory` is logged and treated as empty. The first text block is always the bare array; an empty one gets a second text block, `No suggestions: there is no voice history yet (only <cliCommand('voice')> push-to-talk records one), ...` (or `the N dictations in the voice history hold no new garble or name, ...`), naming where suggestions come from and offering `add_term`. An explicit `cwd` goes through `containedPath` like `harvest_repo`'s `path` (the error names `lexicon suggest --cwd <dir>`), before the lexicon or the repo is read.
  - `apply_suggestion({ suggestion: { kind, canonical, alias?, aliases?, category?, reason?, confidence?, evidence?, count?, scope?, path? }, scope? })`: `alias` -> `addTerm({ canonical, aliases: [alias], source: 'user' })` (merge); `term` -> `addTerm` with the suggestion's `aliases` (trimmed, empties dropped), else `[alias]`, else `suggestAliases(canonical)`, plus its `category`; `never` -> `addTerm({ canonical, aliases: [], never: [alias] })`; `stale` -> `removeTerm(canonical, { scope })`, which edits one file (project first without a scope), then re-reads both files: `path` is the file it was removed from and `stillIn: { scope, path }[]` lists every trusted file that still holds the term (absent when none), with the `summary` naming the scope to pass to remove that copy too. `alias`/`never` without an alias is an error, and so is `alias`/`never` for a canonical the merged lexicon does not hold (`no term "<canonical>" in the lexicon ...`, nothing written; it used to create a term holding only that word). Without `scope` (the argument, else the suggestion's own `scope`), `alias` and `never` write to the scope of the merged term with that canonical (case-insensitive), as `lexicon suggest` does; `term` writes global. When that scope is `project` and the suggestion carries a `path` other than this server's project file (`resolvePaths({ cwd }).project ?? defaultProjectPath(cwd)`: a suggestion from `suggest_terms` with another repo's `cwd`), the call is refused before anything is written, the error naming both files and `lexicon suggest --cwd <dir> --apply`. Returns `{ kind, canonical, term?, path?, created?, removed?, stillIn?, trusted?, nextStep?, summary }`. The `addTerm` calls take `mcpWriteOpts(ctx)`; a project file left untrusted adds `trusted: false` and `nextStep` (also appended to `summary`), and a merge into a term that file already had reports the term this call passed.
  - `apply_suggestion({ suggestions: Suggestion[1..100], scope? })` (exactly one of `suggestion` and `suggestions`; both or neither is an error that writes nothing): each entry is checked and applied as `suggestion` is, in order. A run of consecutive `alias`, `never` and `term` entries bound for the same scope is one `addTerms` call (one read and one write); a `stale` entry, an entry for the other scope, or an `alias`/`never` for a canonical an earlier entry of the run adds ends the run first, so the writes keep the entries' order. A batch that throws is retried one `addTerm` per entry, so each failure names its entry. Returns `{ count, applied: (result & { index })[], failures: { index, kind, canonical, error }[], summary }`, with `isError` when nothing was applied and something failed; a refused entry is a failure row and the rest still apply.
  - `setup_lexicon({ company?, person?, clients?: InstallClient[], harvest?, serve?, apply? })`: preview by default. A `company` or `person` that is blank, holds a control character or is over `LIMITS.word` (80) characters once trimmed is refused before `runSetup` runs, preview or apply (`<field> must ...; nothing was written`, the problem from `setupNameProblem`). Without `apply` it calls `runSetup({ cwd, yes: true, json: true, dryRun: true, company?, person?, clients: <comma-joined, or 'none' when omitted or empty>, serve: serve === true, harvest: false only when harvest === false, harvestUntrusted: true })` and returns the `SetupPlan` (with `wouldHarvest` listed whether or not `harvest` was passed) plus a `next` line telling the model to show it and call again with `apply: true`; nothing is written. With `apply: true` it runs the same options without `dryRun` and with `harvest: harvest === true`, and returns `{ ok: code === 0, applied: true, summary: SetupSummary, stderr?, nextStep? }`, `nextStep` asking the user to review `summary.untrustedProject` (when present) and run `lexicon trust` (`cliCommand('trust')`) or answer `trust_project` `'trust'`, since the model's apply never trusts it, then (joined by ` Then: `) `summary.nextStep`; both calls pass `forAgent: true`, so `stderr` holds only failures; omitted `clients` install nothing (the plan's `detectedClients` is what the model offers), the repo is harvested only with `harvest: true` and the login service is installed only with `serve: true`. The description states what it asks for (company/product spelling, the user's own name, the clients) and what it does not do. A non-zero setup exit is reported as `ok: false`, not as an `isError`.
  - `serve_status({})` -> `GET http://127.0.0.1:<port>/health` (1.5 s timeout), where `<port>` is the one `readServeConfig()` finds in `serve.json` (read only, never created), else `DEFAULT_PORT` 41733: sent with the serve.json token when there is one (`probeHealth`): `{ up: true, url, ...health }`, `{ up: false, running: true, url, status, error, hint }` on a non-2xx answer (`error` is the server's own message), or `{ up: false, url, error, hint }` when nothing answers or a 2xx body is not lexicon serve's (`isLexiconHealth`; `error` is `FOREIGN_HEALTH_ERROR`, `something else answers on that port: its /health response is not lexicon serve's`, then), never an `isError`. `hint` comes from `SERVE_STATUS_HINTS` in `src/serve/config.ts`, shared with `lexicon serve --status --json`, except when nothing answers and `serveInstallBlocker({ cliPath: <cliDir>/index.js })` says this copy (a plugin or npx cache) cannot install the login service: then the hint names `lexicon serve` with its `npx @ashlr/lexicon serve` form and the blocker's reason, not `setup_lexicon` with `serve: true`. When `readServeConfig()` finds no usable config, every result adds `configured: false`, `configPath` (`getServePath()`) and `note` (`serve is not configured: <path> <unusableServeConfigReason(path)>`: missing, empty, not valid JSON, no usable token or unreadable, with the fix), the same three keys `lexicon serve --status --json` adds.
- Resources: `lexicon://glossary` (application/json; the same capped, trusted merged view as `lexicon://json`, plus `activePacks`, the deduplicated installed global and trusted project pack names; reloaded on every read), `lexicon://me` (text/markdown; the claude-md export capped at `MODEL_CONTEXT_MAX_CHARS = 16000` (exported) by `exportClaudeMdWithin`, whose closing line past the cap is `... N more term[s] left out to keep this short (the most used, then brand, person and product names, are kept). Call list_terms with a query (it matches canonical spellings and aliases) to look one up, or with offset and limit to page through every term, or export_lexicon with format claude-md for the whole table.` (the server instructions send every agent here at session start, and uncapped a 3000-term lexicon was about 236 KB), through `neutralizeMarkup`, plus one line naming a skipped untrusted or changed project file, its path through `pathForModel`, and `EMPTY_LEXICON_NOTE` (no terms yet, nothing to apply, offer the onboard prompt or `setup_lexicon` once) when the merged lexicon has no terms), `lexicon://json` (application/json; the merged lexicon, which leaves an untrusted or changed project file out; when one was skipped the object adds `projectTrust`, `skippedProject` (path only) and `note`, as in `list_terms`; when the merged lexicon has no terms `note` is (or, after a skipped file's sentence, ends with) `EMPTY_LEXICON_NOTE` without its leading `Note: ` (the sentence `lexicon://me` appends), so an empty global file next to an untrusted project file says both; otherwise it is the merged view unchanged, `scope` and `hits` included; the text is capped at `LEXICON_JSON_MAX_CHARS = 64000` (exported, four times `MODEL_CONTEXT_MAX_CHARS`): past it the most terms that fit are kept in `selectExportTerms` order, `omittedTerms` counts the rest and `note` ends with `N of M terms left out to keep this resource under 64000 characters (...). Call export_lexicon with format json for the whole lexicon, list_terms with a query to look one up, or list_terms with offset and limit to page through every term.`).
- Prompts: `voice-context` (the claude-md export, capped as `lexicon://me` is, through `neutralizeMarkup`, then the same path-only skipped-project note `lexicon://me` appends when a project lexicon was skipped, followed by `VOICE_CONTEXT_INSTRUCTION`, or by `EMPTY_LEXICON_NOTE` instead when the merged lexicon has no terms); `onboard` (no args; a user-role message, `ONBOARD_PROMPT`, under 25 lines: ask for company/product spellings and pronunciations, teammates, the clients in use, then call `setup_lexicon`, `add_term` for each remaining name showing the aliases, and end with one test sentence to dictate). Both carry `strictPromptArgs(name)` as their registered `argsSchema` (`strictPromptArgs(name: string, shape?: z.ZodRawShape): z.ZodObject`, exported: a `z.strictObject` whose unknown-key message is `unknown argument[s] "<key>"[, ...] for prompt <name>: it takes only "<declared>", ...` or `: it takes no arguments; omit arguments or pass {}`), set on the `RegisteredPrompt` because `registerPrompt` would wrap a raw shape in a stripping object: an undeclared argument is a -32602 `Invalid arguments for prompt <name>: ...`, omitted or `{}` passes, and `prompts/list` gives `arguments: []`.
- Loads: `normalize_transcript`, `list_terms`, `export_lexicon`, `suggest_canonical`, `lexicon_stats`, `list_packs`, both resources, the `voice-context` prompt and `add_term`'s conflict check call `ctx.load({ parseSkipped: false })`, and `suggest_terms` calls `loadLexicon({ cwd, parseSkipped: false })`, as the prompt hook does: each reads only `skippedProject.path` and `projectTrust` of a skipped file (`suggest_terms` and `add_term` read only the merged files), so an untrusted or changed one is never parsed. The tools that write, and `remove_term` and `learn_correction`, which name a copy of a term in the skipped file (`untrustedTermHolder`, `untrustedAliasHolder`, `learnCorrection`'s `untrustedProject`), load with the default.

## plugin/ (committed esbuild bundles)
- `plugin/mcp-server.mjs` (<- `src/mcp/server.ts`), `plugin/hook-main.mjs` (<- `src/hooks/user-prompt-submit.ts`) and `plugin/hook.mjs` (<- `src/hooks/plugin-entry.ts`, a few KB that enables the compile cache and imports `hook-main.mjs`): ESM, Node >= 20, every dependency inlined, unminified, shebang preserved, prefixed with a `createRequire` banner so bundled CommonJS (`yaml`) can `require` builtins. Built by `scripts/build-bundle.mjs` (`npm run build:bundle`); `npm run check:bundle` fails when they differ from `src/`. `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` (inline `mcpServers`) and `hooks/hooks.json` point at them via the bare `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}`, the only form Claude Code expands there; the repo's own `.mcp.json` runs `plugin/mcp-server.mjs` by relative path for working in this repo. `dist/` stays untracked.

## extension/ (browser extension)
Manifest V3 extension for the ChatGPT, Claude, Grok, Gemini, Perplexity, Poe and Copilot composers. Built by `scripts/build-extension.mjs` (`npm run build:extension`) into `extension/dist` (Chromium) and `extension/dist-firefox`; the build fails if a `node:` builtin reaches a bundle or a non-core bundle exceeds 200 KB. The full contract, including the adapter table, modes, popup and privacy rules, is `docs/EXTENSION.md`.
- `extension/src/core.ts`: the browser-safe slice of `src/core` (schema, matcher, normalize, suggest, and `conflictNote` from `conflict-note.ts`) plus `yaml`, re-exported as one ESM chunk shared by the worker and the options page. It must never import store, trust, harvest or anything that touches `node:` modules.
- `extension/src/adapters.ts`: `SiteAdapter { host; composer selectors; send button selectors }`, `ADAPTERS`, `GENERIC`, `adapterFor(hostname)`, `hostKey(hostname)`, `findComposer(doc, adapter)`, `composerFromTarget(target, adapter)`, `findSendButton(doc, adapter, composer)`. `deepActiveElement(doc)` (the focused element through open shadow roots; `findComposer` uses it), `selectionFor(doc, node)` (the shadow root's own selection, `ShadowRoot.getSelection` where the browser has it, for a node in an open shadow root, else `doc.getSelection()`; `holdsCode` and every caret read and write in `editable.ts` use it) and `eventOrigin(ev)` (`composedPath()[0]`, else `target`), so a composer inside an open shadow root is found. `siteRows(sites, seen): SiteRow[]` (`SiteRow { label; host; anySite? }`: the Options list, every `ADAPTERS` host, then each host with a stored switch, then each of `seen`, the `STORAGE_KEYS.anySiteHosts` list the background keeps (`shared.ts` `coerceHostList`, at most `ANY_SITE_HOSTS_LIMIT` (100) hosts, newest first: `background-core.ts` `handle(req, sender?: MessageSender { url? })` adds the host of the tab a normalize came from when it is http(s) and not `builtinPage`), each host once), `builtinPage(url)` (the page matches one of `BUILTIN_MATCHES`), `guardActive(settings, url)` (`siteEnabled` and either `builtinPage` or `anySite`: what the content script sets `enabled` to, through `content-boot.ts` `scopeActive`, at load, on every settings change and on every URL change) and `siteNote(settings, url)` (the popup's line: `Paused on this site.`, a `turn on "Any site" in Options` line for a site the guard does not cover, or `Corrections run before each send.`). `isEditable(el)` is false for an identifier field (`isIdentifierField`) and for code (`isCodeEditor(el)`: a `<pre>` or `<code>`, or anything inside `CODE_EDITOR_SELECTOR` = `.cm-editor, .CodeMirror, .monaco-editor, .ace_editor`); `editableRoot(node)` stops at code with null, so a page that embeds an editor is not its composer; for an adapter without an editor selector (`GENERIC`, Grok on X) `holdsCode(doc, root)` (a rich text root that embeds a code editor, or with the caret inside `CODE_SELECTOR` under it; a `<pre>` or `<code>` elsewhere in it no longer counts, since the content script masks code ranges as on a known site) also makes `findComposer` and `composerFromTarget` return null. `CODE_SELECTOR` = `pre, code, kbd, samp, ` plus `CODE_EDITOR_SELECTOR`: code inside a composer (see `codeRanges`). The "Supported sites" table in `docs/EXTENSION.md` mirrors `ADAPTERS`.
- `extension/src/background-core.ts`: `createBackground(deps: BackgroundDeps): Background` holds the token and settings (`chrome.storage.local` via `chrome-store.ts` `KeyValueStore`), calls `GET /health` (cached `HEALTH_TTL_MS` per base URL; the cache is dropped when any request finds nothing listening, times out, or gets a 401 or 403). After a 401 to the stored token no bearer request is sent with that token and base URL for `TOKEN_BACKOFF_MS` (30 s), doubling with each further 401 up to `TOKEN_BACKOFF_MAX_MS` (10 min); a normalize, Learn or Sync in that time falls back or fails with the rejection without touching the network, a success or a changed token or base URL ends it, and `{ type: 'status', probe: true }` (Options > Test connection) asks anyway. Only a `/health` body that `isLexiconHealth(raw)` accepts (a boolean `ok`, a non-empty string `version`, a whole-number `terms`, the fields of `HealthResponse`) counts as the server; any other 2xx body leaves `server` null with `serverError` = `foreignHealthMessage(baseUrl)`, so nothing is posted to another program on the port. In the Embedded mode `status` probes no server (`server: null`, no `serverError`) unless `probe` is set. `StatusReply.tokenRetryAt` (epoch ms) is set with `tokenError` while it holds, and `tokenBackoffNote(s, now?)` is the sentence `statusView` and `connectionTestLine` add for it, `POST /normalize`, `POST /learn` and `GET /lexicon` on `DEFAULT_BASE_URL` (`http://127.0.0.1:41733`) with `FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS` (1.5 s) for `/health`, `/normalize` and `/stats`, `WRITE_TIMEOUT_MS` (15 s, longer than the server's 10 s lock wait) for `/learn` and `LEXICON_TIMEOUT_MS` (10 s) for `/lexicon`, and falls back to `embedded.ts` `createEmbedded(yaml, opts?: EmbeddedOptions { budgetMs?; now? })` (`compileYaml`, `learnIntoYaml`, `lexiconToYaml`) when the API is down. A normalize whose `/normalize` body would be over `API_MAX_BODY_BYTES` (`shared.ts`, 1 MB, the server's `MAX_BODY_BYTES`, counted in UTF-8 bytes) is never posted, since the server would answer 413: with the server up it is checked by the embedded engine with `fallback` set to `tooLargeForApiMessage(bytes)` (`the text is <bytes> bytes as a request, over the 1 MB lexicon serve accepts, so the embedded lexicon checked it`) and the health cache is kept; with the server down the fallback still names why it is down. The embedded engine caches the compiled lexicon and its matcher index per YAML text (built on the first normalize after a change) and stops matching after `EMBEDDED_MATCH_BUDGET_MS` (250 ms) per call, returning the corrections found so far; its `normalize` returns `EmbeddedNormalizeResult` = `NormalizeResult & { incomplete? }`, set (`timeBudgetIncomplete`) only when the budget stopped it. `NormalizeOk.incomplete?` carries it, or a well-formed `incomplete` from `POST /normalize`, to the content script. Message types: `Request`, `Reply` (`NormalizeReply`, `LearnReply`, `StatusReply`, `SyncReply`, `PairReply`). `{ type: 'pair', baseUrl, token }` (from `pair.ts`): `baseUrl` must pass `pairableBaseUrl` (plain `http:`, host in `PAIR_HOSTS` = `127.0.0.1` | `localhost`, explicit port, no path/query/credentials) and `token` must match `TOKEN_RE` (32..128 hex); the token is proven with `GET /stats` under that bearer (`/health` needs no auth so it proves nothing), only then `saveSettings({ baseUrl, token, mode: 'api' })`, then `/health` for the reply `PairReply { ok: true, baseUrl, terms, version }`; any failure is `Failure` and stores nothing. `{ type: 'pairPort', baseUrl }` (from Options, after `chrome.permissions.request` granted `pairPortOrigins(baseUrl)`): `baseUrl` must pass `pairableBaseUrl`; calls `BackgroundDeps.setPairPort(origins | undefined)` and answers `PairPortReply { ok: true, origins? }` or `Failure`. `pairPortOrigins(baseUrl): string[] | undefined` (`shared.ts`) is `http://127.0.0.1:<port>/*` and `http://localhost:<port>/*` for a pairable URL off `DEFAULT_PORT` (`'41733'`), else undefined; `pairScriptMatches(origins)` their `/pair*` patterns; `background.ts` `setPairPort` is `pair-port.ts` `registerPairPort(api: PairPortApi, origins)` (`PairPortApi { scripting; permissions }`, the slice of `chrome.scripting` / `chrome.permissions` it uses): one registration of `pair.js` per port, id `pairPortScriptId(origins)` = `lexicon-pair-port-<port>`, made only when `permissions.contains` says the origins are granted (else throws `PAIR_PORT_DENIED_MESSAGE` and registers nothing); a registration already covering the same `/pair*` page (that port's, or the single-id `PAIR_PORT_SCRIPT_ID` = `lexicon-pair-port` of earlier releases) is replaced, every other port's is kept (one id used to hold one port, so pairing a third port dropped the second), and undefined (the default port) changes nothing. The permission-removed, installed and startup listeners call `prunePairPorts(api)`, which unregisters each port's script whose origins are no longer all granted. The manifest's `host_permissions` stay the chat sites plus loopback port 41733; other ports come out of the optional `<all_urls>`. A `POST /learn` success with a well-formed `conflict` adds `learnWarnings(heard, conflict, untrustedProject)` to the message: for an alias, the scoped `lexicon remove --alias` command; for a canonical conflict, ` But <conflictNote([conflict])>.`, the core's CLI sentence, so the popup names the same `lexicon remove <canonical> --<scope>` (shell-quoted, `conflictRemoveTermCommand`) step. `background.ts` is the `chrome.runtime` wiring.
- `extension/src/pair.ts` (built as the classic `pair.js`, manifest `content_scripts` matching `http://127.0.0.1:41733/pair*` and `http://localhost:41733/pair*` at `document_idle`; both origins are `host_permissions`; each other port's `/pair*` once registered by `setPairPort`): `pairFromDocument({ doc, send }): Promise<PairOutcome { ok, status }>` hides `#install`, reads `PAIR_META` (`lexicon-token`, `lexicon-port`; base URL = `http://<page hostname>:<meta port>`), sends `{ type: 'pair' }` (the background pairs only when that base URL is the sender tab's own origin and the tab is on its `/pair` page, `isPairPageOf(senderUrl, baseUrl)`, exported; otherwise it answers `Pairing must come from <baseUrl>/pair itself; run \`lexicon serve --pair\` again.` before any request or write), and writes `Paired with lexicon serve (N terms). You can close this tab.` (class `ok`, `<h1>` becomes `Paired`) or `Pairing failed: <error>` (class `error`) into `#status` (a request that did not reach the background gets `extensionRequestFailedLine`); a page without a token is refused without a message. Auto-runs only when `chrome.runtime.id` is set (never under the tests). `pairUrl(baseUrl)` in `shared.ts` builds the `/pair` link the Options page shows ("pair in this browser" / "pair again") next to a "Paired with <baseUrl> (token stored)" / "Not paired" line; Options listens to `chrome.storage.onChanged` so a pairing in another tab is reflected without a reload. For a Base URL off the default port, clicking that link asks for `pairPortOrigins(baseUrl)` inside the click, sends `pairPort`, then opens the page with `chrome.tabs.create`; a refused prompt shows `PAIR_PORT_DENIED_MESSAGE`.
- `extension/src/content-core.ts`: `installContent(deps: ContentDeps): ContentHandle` intercepts Enter and send-button clicks in the capture phase, sends the composer text to the worker, applies `applyCorrections` / `invertCorrections` through `editable.ts` (`readText`, `setWholeText`, `caretOffset`, `isEditable`, `editableRoot`; ranged `insertText` for ProseMirror, Lexical and Quill; for a textarea or input `insertText` while it has focus, else `setRangeText` and an `input` event, so the field's undo history is kept, with the React-safe value setter only when neither leaves the expected text), shows `toast.ts` `showToast` with Undo for `TOAST_MS`, then re-dispatches the original event. Code in a rich composer is never corrected: `editable.ts` `codeRanges(el): Array<[start, end]>` (the flattened ranges inside `CODE_SELECTOR` under `el`; empty for a text field, whose markdown code the matcher skips itself) and `maskCode(text, ranges)` (each character in them but a line break replaced by `_`, same length) are applied to every normalize the script sends (send, precheck, live), so the prose around a code block or inline code on ChatGPT, Claude and the other selector sites is still corrected, the code never reaches the engine (no match, no hit), and the reply's `output` is rebuilt from the real text with the replacements outside code. A reply carrying `incomplete` adds the `shared.ts` `incompleteNotice(incomplete)` line to the toast (`ToastOptions.notice`), and a send with nothing corrected but `incomplete` set still shows a toast with only that line (no Undo); a dry-run precheck that came back `incomplete` does not let Enter skip the send-time check. Live mode debounces `LIVE_DEBOUNCE_MS` and never rewrites between `compositionstart` and `compositionend` (an input event with `isComposing` schedules nothing; compositionend schedules the check); the unchanged-text precheck `PRECHECK_DEBOUNCE_MS`. Events are read at `eventOrigin(ev)`, so a composer inside an open shadow root is intercepted too. On an adapter with a send selector, Enter is swallowed only when `findSendButton` finds a button or the page is known to take a script-dispatched Enter (it cancelled a replayed one, or a real one the guard let through); otherwise it goes on as typed. A composer over `editable.ts` `MAX_COMPOSER_CHARS` (= `API_MAX_BODY_BYTES`, the server's request limit; `composerTooLarge(el)` measures a field's value or a rich composer's `textContent` without flattening) is never checked: no precheck or live check, and Enter or the send button goes on as typed with a `TOO_LARGE_NOTICE` toast. Each check reads the composer through `snapshot(doc, el): ComposerSnapshot { text; ranges; caret }`, one flattening for the text, `codeRanges` and `caretOffset` (both take an optional `Flattened`). A check that ends with the replay not taken and no button shows `NOT_SENT_NOTICE` (`Not sent: this site ignored the replayed Enter and its send button was not found. Press Enter again to send.`). The token never reaches this script. `content.ts` is the `chrome.*` wiring, through `extension/src/content-boot.ts`: `frameScope(win: FrameWindow): FrameScope | null` (`FrameScope { url; topUrl? }`: the top frame as `{ url }`; a subframe whose top window it can read (same origin) as `{ url, topUrl }` with `url` the top's for an `about:` or `blob:` frame; a cross-origin subframe only when `builtinPage` of its own URL, with `topUrl` from Chromium's `location.ancestorOrigins` when present; any other frame null), `scopeActive(settings, scope)` (`guardActive(settings, scope.url)` and, with a `topUrl`, that host's site switch) and `bootContent(deps: BootDeps): Promise<BootHandle | undefined>` (`installContent` in a frame `frameScope` covers, `enabled` kept in step with the stored settings and, through `watchUrl(win, read, onChange, pollMs = URL_POLL_MS)`, with the URL: popstate, hashchange, the Navigation API's `currententrychange` and a `URL_POLL_MS` (500) poll, since a content script sees no event for the page's `history.pushState`). The manifest's chat-site script has `all_frames` and `match_about_blank`, and the "any site" registration `allFrames` (a registration without it is replaced).
- `extension/src/popup.ts`, `options.ts`, `shared.ts`: popup (status, per-site switch, last `RECENT_LIMIT` corrections, Learn a word) and options (token, mode, embedded YAML validated with `parseLexicon`, Sync from local API, per-site switches, the opt-in `<all_urls>` permission). `Settings`, `defaultSettings()`, `coerceSettings()`, `siteEnabled()`, `STORAGE_KEYS`, `extensionRequestFailedLine(error): string` (`<error>. Reload this page; if it persists, reload Lexicon on the browser's extensions page.`, what Options and the /pair page show when the request to the background itself fails).
- Tests: `tests/extension.test.ts` drives both cores under jsdom with a fake background and a mocked `chrome.runtime` (skipped on Node 20).

## web/ (landing page, lexicon.ashlr.ai)
Next.js marketing site, deployed separately; nothing in `src/` imports it. `web/scripts/build-demo-bundle.mjs` runs on `prebuild`/`predev` and bundles the browser-safe slice of `src/core` into `web/lib/generated`, so the live demo on the page runs this repo's real matcher rather than a reimplementation. That makes it a build-time consumer of core exactly like `extension/src/core.ts`: a change to the matcher's public surface can break the landing page's build. See `docs/LANDING.md` for the deploy, and treat any change here as needing a redeploy.

## site/ (GitHub Pages demo, ashlrai.github.io/lexicon)
The older standalone demo: hand-rolled `index.html` + `app.ts` + `styles.css`, no framework, built by `scripts/build-site.mjs` (`npm run build:site`) into `site/dist`. Also bundles the browser-safe slice of core. It additionally hosts `install.sh`, which the documented one-line install pipes to `sh`, so it cannot simply be deleted in favour of `web/`.

## apps/macos/LexiconBar (menu bar app)
SwiftPM app, no dependencies, that fronts the CLI; it never links the TypeScript code. `Sources/LexiconBar/LocalAPI.swift` also talks to `src/serve` over loopback HTTP (that is how the menu knows whether the local API is up and who owns it), alongside `CorrectionBubble/` and `FixEverywhere/`. `scripts/build-macos-app.sh` builds and ad-hoc signs `apps/macos/build/LexiconBar.app`; the `macos-app` workflow and the release workflow zip it. User docs: `docs/MACOS-APP.md`.
- `LexiconBarKit` (testable, no AppKit): `CLILocator` (finds `lexicon` on PATH, `/opt/homebrew/bin`, `/usr/local/bin`, npm and Homebrew prefixes, or the path saved in Preferences), `CLIOutput` (parses `lexicon voice --json`, `lexicon daemon --once`, `lexicon path` and `lexicon stats --json` output; the app checks the local API with `GET /health` itself and runs no `lexicon serve --status`), `RestartPolicy` (5 s backoff, 5 restarts before giving up), `HotKey` (Carbon hotkey encoding for the recorder).
- `LexiconBar` (AppKit): `AppDelegate` builds the status item menu; `HotKeyCenter` registers ⌃⌥Space (push to talk via `lexicon voice --toggle --json`) and ⌃⌥V (`lexicon daemon --once`); `ChildProcessSupervisor` runs `lexicon daemon` and `lexicon serve` as child processes under `RestartPolicy`; `CommandRunner` spawns one-shot CLI calls; `Notifier` posts a notification after each run; `Settings` persists CLI path, whisper model, paste-vs-copy and hotkeys in `UserDefaults`; `PreferencesWindow` is the SwiftUI preferences sheet and `TextWindow` a read-only text window for `lexicon doctor` output.
- Everything the app does is a CLI invocation with the same files, trust gate and hit counters as the terminal, so this contract for the CLI is the app's contract too.
